# Build 3158346939: execution-verified deobfuscation status

Captures: `re/int-plain-1` (orchestrator 001, fo 002) and `re/int-headless-1` (orchestrator 002, fo 003). The raw captures are not in this repo.
**Rule:** a heuristic can only propose a hypothesis. A fact counts only after the real CF code has been executed offline in the Node `vm` sandbox, where network globals throw. Every result below says how it was verified.

"Lifted" means the instruction is rendered as JS from verified facts only: operator semantics, constants, site-verified branches, relation instances, `goto` and closures. Every other instruction is shown as an opaque `opN(...)` with the registers it reads and writes, as observed.

Outputs are in `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/`:
- `stage{1,2}.<capture>.{json,dis.txt,lift.js}`
- `semantics.<capture>.json`

## Verification methods

1. **operator** (`lib/handler-semantics.mjs`): the real handler runs at a synthetic pc over random bytecodes with numeric/string/object vectors. Accepted only if exactly one library operator (with operand order) reproduces every write.
   - Object-operand ops (e.g. 67) get their shape (size, reads, writes) from runs where every read returns `Object`.
2. **relation** (`lib/handler-relations.mjs`): inputs are tagged (fn/obj/arr/path-proxy, mask 0..81). Accepted only if one template is identical across trials.
   - Templates: copy, imm, strlit, concat, property chain, call/new, push/pop, throw.
   - A `strlit`/`imm` must be identical in a second run with different inputs and the same bytes.
   - Variable-size ops must end at the same pc in that second run (`sizeFromBytecode`).
3. **branch** (`lib/handler-branch.mjs`): over 12 bytecodes × 12 operand vectors, the successor pc must be a function of the predicate alone, and any stored value must equal the predicate.
   - At each real site, `branchAt` re-executes the handler and checks the partition again. Both successors are then followed by the disassembler.
4. **site instance** (`lib/instantiate.mjs`): for verified relation ops, the real handler is re-executed at the dispatcher's post-fetch pc/key on the real bytecode, with recording proxies as operands. This records the property keys, call targets, `this` and argument lists.
   - Reads of VM-internal registers (bytecode array = `vm.N`, cell table = `vm.A`) get the real values.
5. **disassembly** (`lib/disasm.mjs`):
   - The opcode comes from the real dispatcher.
   - Length and successors come from the real handler under 10 register scenarios.
   - Closure entries come from intercepting `run()`.
   - Edges to non-opcodes are refuted by the dispatcher's default case.
6. **frame** (`lib/handler-frame.mjs`, aggregated by `frame-run.mjs`): for handlers that type-check their operands (cell table, try stack, depth stack), the real handler is executed at every disassembled site, twice, with real-shaped state built in the sandbox realm (cell table `vm.A` = 40 `{B}` cells, try stack `r(o^239)`, depth stack `r(o^29)` of different lengths per run) and run-unique tokens for every other read. A template is accepted at a site only if every observable effect (register diffs, cell identity/contents, stack contents, pc) matches in both runs; an op counts as verified only if the template held at every site of all four listings.

`53` is `goto` and `216` is a closure. Both are verified structurally by the disassembler (successor/closure entry executed) but not by a semantics verifier, so they are not counted in the 67. Registers 48/194/162 are `o^239`/`o^29`/`o^125` with `o = 223` for this build.
7. **site-call** (`lib/handler-call.mjs`, aggregated by `frame-run.mjs`): for op 138, whose random-bytecode trials are silent. At each disassembled site the recording-proxy instance (method 4) supplies a hypothesis: `rD = rO.NAME(args)`, `rD = rF.call(rT, args)`, or a bare `rD = rX` (the proxy answers `toString` itself, so this is tested as `rX.toString()`). The handler is then executed twice more at the same site with concrete sandbox-realm values: the receiver is an object whose only own property is NAME, a logging function returning a run-unique token (or the function register gets that logging function). Accepted only if, in both runs, there was exactly one call with the expected `this` and argument tokens in order, the destination got the returned token, no other register changed, and the next pc matched. When the text is ambiguous (`rX.call(...)` can be form A with a method named `call`), each reading is executed and only the passing one is kept.

## Remaining heuristics (not facts; reviewed choices)

- **Size-mode gate:** handlers whose size varies by input are accepted only via the same-bytes/same-end-pc rule.
- **Branch naming:** for a two-way branch the un-negated predicate is named `p` (`seq` rather than `sne`). This is a naming convention only; the semantics are fixed by execution.
- **Register scenarios:** the 10 disassembler scenarios may miss successors that only one specific value reaches. Value-dependent successors are reported and not followed, except site-verified branches.
- **Stage 2:** Chrome-seeded listings: plain **33174** / 423 KB (H28); headless **34260** / 442 KB (H29). Unlisted Chrome pcs ~205–210. Readable JS for both programs via lift-readable.

## Readable orchestrator and api.js (deliverable a, first pass)

`readable-run.mjs` produces `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/{int-plain-1,int-headless-1}.orchestrator.readable.js` (11.3k / 11.7k lines) and `api.readable.js` (3.3k lines; identical for both captures). The transform is `normalize.mjs` followed by pretty-printing:
- 4486 `a0(...)` string-decoder calls replaced by the value that the real decoder returns when executed in a vm sandbox (0 undecodable);
- 1420 lazy string caches inlined;
- the opaque seed (`(a=>a).length+N`) and 21413 constant expressions folded. api.js has no string decoder; 381 constants are folded.

Equivalence is checked by execution (H21). Both checks pass on both captures:

| Check | Orchestrator | api.js |
|---|---|---|
| E1: raw original vs raw readable, fresh browser sandbox, no instrumentation (globals added, listeners/timers/net/errors, then stage-1 factory + call) | equal (`factory ok`, call ends in the same TypeError) | equal (both stop at "Could not find Turnstile valid script tag"; the sandbox has no `currentScript`) |
| E2: instrumented boot, factory prop, stage-1 bytes hash, full natural (pc, op) traces | stage 1: 149 = 149 steps, same hash; stage 2: 197 = 197, same hash and stop | n/a |

Not done yet: renaming the VM's identifiers and handler cases from the verified opcode table, and splitting out the dispatcher. The committed files are redacted (see Redaction policy), so they are for reading only and do not run as-is.

## Blockers

- Sandbox gaps end the Node natural runs early: stage 1 DOM TypeError after 149 steps (H19), stage 2 Blob. Chrome-run (H22) covers the page-like path up to payload send and is now the primary coverage source.
- Handlers still without a full semantic template: 36, 122, 234 (never dispatched in Chrome). op53/216/186/66 now have site templates (H27). op52 both successors in static listing (H27).
- Stage 2 Chrome re-seed done (H28): **33174** instructions (was 611); ~26k Chrome pcs covered.
- History scrub completed (force-push to scrubbed main); no further rewrite without approval.
- **Payload encode + nested (H32):** bag encode 90/101; nested probe interiors 645 entries (568 encode offsets). Still not a standalone generator. Hook vs clean (roadmap 4): op52 R sequence identical under webdriver/runtime/native hooks; T pc sequences vary across clean replays (async), so mid-trace T diffs are not hook-attributable without further alignment.

## Redaction policy

String literals are replaced by `<REDACTED str len=N>` in every committed output if they match any of:
- longer than 64 characters;
- 24 or more token-like characters (`[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` or base64 `[A-Za-z0-9+/=$]`);
- contain non-printable characters;
- contain `<vm-endpoint>`, `<chl-opt>` or `cRay`.

In the readable orchestrator, the per-session challenge-options object (`window.<chl opt> = {...}`) is redacted as a whole. Every literal inside it becomes `<REDACTED cfg>`, and the identifier becomes `__REDACTED_ident`. `readable-run.mjs` then fails if any removed value of 4 or more characters (other than plain lowercase words and numbers up to 5 digits) still appears in the output.

Rays are written as `<RAY>`. Raw captures and challenge-option (chl opt) values are never committed.

## H33 — Deterministic hook diff

Harness made deterministic by pinning entropy (Date, performance.now+timeOrigin,
Math.random, crypto.getRandomValues) injected per realm (page + orchestrator iframe
+ workers) via addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument + Runtime.evaluate at target attach.
391/519 payload leaf fields are byte-stable across 5 clean replays (resource-timing
map keys now stable too). The 128 nondeterministic fields are the per-probe timing
envelope (roTuJ3/DJme8/helyQ4, 112) plus 16 top-level timing/CPU-benchmark fields —
intrinsically per-run, never committed.

Comparison: in-memory FNV-1a per-leaf content hash; a field counts as hook-attributable
only if stable across all 5 clean AND both hook replays agree on a differing hash.

Result: webdriver 0, native 0, runtime 0 differing fields (after timing-class
exclusion). See tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/hook-deterministic-diff.json.

Blockers:
- Virtual time (Emulation.setVirtualTimePolicy) is infeasible for the live challenge
  (watchdog reload storm, n=0). Determinism achieved via entropy pinning + per-field
  hash projection instead.
- The stealth-cdp harness neutralizes all three automation fingerprints before the VM
  observes them: webdriver property locked (non-configurable false), toString re-hardened
  to native, Runtime.enable blocked at the wire (ForbiddenWireMethodError). So hook runs
  are indistinguishable from clean — not evidence the VM ignores these signals, but that
  the harness erases them before the VM runs.

## H34 — Hook diff on stock Chrome (bypassing stealth-cdp neutralization)

H33 showed the stealth-cdp harness neutralizes the three automation hooks (locks `navigator.webdriver`, re-hardens `Function.prototype.toString`, blocks `Runtime.enable` at the wire). H34 re-runs the hook diff on **stock Chrome 156** driven by a plain raw-CDP client (`tools/chrome-run/run-plain.mjs`, no stealth patches, no wire guard) so each hook actually reaches the VM, while stealth-cdp (`run.mjs`) stays the clean baseline. Entropy pinning (Date / perf / Math.random / crypto) is kept on both. A plain-clean (no-hook) control on the same plain harness isolates each hook from any stealth-vs-plain environment delta.

Four variants, applied separately: real `navigator.webdriver=true` (`--enable-automation`), a JS-wrapped `Function.prototype.toString` with a visible non-native string, `Runtime.enable` actually sent per target, and plain `--headless=new`.

**Harness delta (stealth-clean vs plain-clean):** 0 deterministic fields — stealth patches change no payload field, so the two baselines are equivalent (no confound).

**Observability.** Browser-level signals persist to readout and cover every realm (incl. pristine child iframes), so they are confirmed VM-observable: `webdriver` (`navigator.webdriver=true`) and `headless` (`HeadlessChrome` UA). Realm-local hooks cannot be verified by post-collection readout because the orchestrator realm is restored/sanitized by collection end (DET marker gone, `Math.random` live); entropy pinning's own injection path is nonetheless proven active during collection by the deterministic payload. So `native` (per-realm `toString` override, absent from the orchestrator realm at readout; VM likely reads pristine child realms) is **not confirmable** observable, and `runtime` (`Runtime.enable` accepted on every target vs the stealth block, but no reliable in-page JS detector) is **partial**.

**Per-hook differing fields** (FNV-1a content hash per leaf, in-memory only; genuine iff stable across all clean replays and all hook replays and differing from clean; timing leaves `roTuJ3/DJme8/helyQ4/SvnXY5` excluded):

| hook | differing fields | field paths | serializer pc |
|---|---|---|---|
| webdriver | 0 | — | — |
| native | 0 | — | — |
| runtime | 0 | — | — |
| headless | 2 | `2.yAui9.ynPml0`, `16.wsksA3` | 124041 |

The two headless fields are string result leaves of probe envelopes 2 and 16 (headless-rendering-sensitive, e.g. WebGL/canvas/UA), stable across 3 clean and 3 headless replays; their exact source API is not individually resolved in this build's schema. `serializer_pc=124041` is the shared zT serializer write point, not a probe-specific branch pc. Notably, even with VM-observable `navigator.webdriver=true` (read at pc 115154, H32), **zero** deterministic fields change — the webdriver read is not encoded into any individually-diffable field.

Field/pc/API tables only: `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/hook-plain-cdp-diff.json`. No values, hashes, rays, `<vm-endpoint>` paths, or captures committed.

## H35 — Webdriver dataflow, VM-realm hook visibility, opcode closures, readable overview

### H35.1 Webdriver dataflow (static taint + dynamic value-class diff)
`navigator.webdriver` is read in stage-2 at **pc 115167** (`r202 = r254[r192](r205, r193)`); the nearby pc 115154 is only the `LOAD_CONST` of the string `"webdriver"` into the key register (a second load sits at pc 115258). Forward register taint from the read propagates 115167→115173→115187→115239→**115275**, where the value is consumed as a property **descriptor with a `.get` getter inspection** (a native-getter anti-tamper check) and then passed into a call and onto the heap. Register-level taint cannot follow heap stores, so it terminates at 115275 with 0 payload sinks reached.

Dynamically, `webdriver=true` vs `=false` (3 vs 3 entropy-pinned replays) shows **zero** presence/type/length/stable-value differences in any non-timing field. The only deterministic reproducible footprint is the probe-13 timing-duration leaf **`13.helyQ4`** (value-class: presence identical, type identical; only the duration value class differs). Conclusion: webdriver is **not encoded as an isolable field** — it gates branch-length/timing and enters a non-emitted heap aggregate, surfacing only as a timing-envelope leaf. Output: `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/webdriver-dataflow.json` (field/pc/API only, no values).

### H35.2 VM-realm hook visibility (why native/runtime show no field diff)
Enumerating execution contexts via `Target.setAutoAttach` + `Runtime.executionContextCreated` reveals the realm topology: **9 default `challenges.cloudflare.com` contexts** (orchestrator + nested frames), **3 `blob:challenges.cloudflare.com` realms** (`isDefault=undefined` — the VM compute realms / workers), plus the `peet.ws` host frame.

- `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` (even `runImmediately:true`) reaches orchestrator **default** contexts but **has no reach into the `blob:` worker realms** where the VM computes. This is why the `native` (`Function.prototype.toString`) and `runtime` JS hooks are invisible to the VM via the sanctioned browser injection route — confirming H34's no-difference result is correct, not a measurement miss.
- Forcing a hook into the VM realm requires a Runtime-domain per-context `evaluate`; doing so **aborts the challenge** (payload instruction count `n→0`) even for DET-only with no hook, while `Runtime.enable` alone (no per-context eval) is survivable (`n≈180.9k`). So a native/runtime hook that is *actually* VM-visible yields **"no payload" (abort)**, not a diffable field — native/runtime detection is **binary**, not field-encoded.
- `webdriver` and `headless` remain the only hooks with measurable field diffs precisely because they are **browser-global** (`navigator.webdriver`, headless UA) and therefore reach every realm, including the `blob:` workers, without any CDP injection into the VM realm.

Caveat: observed `n` varies run-to-run (180800/180861/180932/181114), so determinism pinning is only partial; the diff's "stable-in-both-groups-and-differing" bar stays conservative (fewer comparable leaves, no false positives). Output: `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/hook-vm-visibility.json`. Runner additions (`RUN_DIAG`, `RUN_CTXINJECT`) are env-gated and default-off, so the normal diff path is unchanged.

### H35.3 Opcode / pc closures
- **op36**: 0 occurrences in this build — not dispatched. The earlier H12 op36 observation does not apply here.
- **op122 (pc 83879), op234 (pc 84836)**: phantom alternate-key decodings. Each pc also decodes under a different key as an `op233 LOAD_CONST` continuing a coherent `console.countReset` / `console.info` setup sequence reached via the `op160` branch; the op122/op234 decodings are flagged `refuted (no such opcode)` / `TypeError`. Not live handlers.
- **~205 "unlisted" pcs** = 372 unresolved/err instructions: 4 dead/unreachable, 43 phantom alt-key (op122/op234 class), and **325 known opcodes whose static lift is deliberately abandoned on runtime operands** — `op201/op22/op242` are table-indexed VM-builtin calls (`d=callret; call tbl[imm].B`), `op66` is a cell-based call (`Ybb`), `op249` is `throw r0`, `op245` is frame/return-state writeback (610/615 modeled). All 368 reachable bad pcs are successors of resolved instructions and map to known opcodes; they are not emitted into the readable lift only because the call target/args/throw value need runtime state. Output: `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/opcode-closures.json`.
- **Nested-envelope writing_pc**: all 101 top-level fields have a `writing_pc`. Of 528 nested leaves, 369 are `writing_pc_definitive` (meta_number/meta_string/extra + VM-probe value leaves), 114 are serializer-flush (non-definitive: envelope wrapper + label/result_json/SvnXY5, which pass through the generic zT serializer), and 45 remain unattributed.

- **`tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/stage2.int-plain-1.named.txt`** — the full 33174-instruction stage-2 program renamed to mnemonics with register aliases and flags (`[P]` phantom, `[E]` static-lift-abandoned runtime operand, `[U]` unverified op). Scanned clean of `<chl-opt>` / ray IDs / `<vm-endpoint>` paths.

## H42 — Readable executable lift vs original VM (Chrome probedone)

Goal: prove 1:1 output equivalence of the readable executable lift substitute against the original VM for build 3158346939, across clean / webdriver / runtime / headless, and characterize `37.oPxfw0` nondeterminism.

### H42.1 Harness (Chrome lift-subst)

- `tools/chrome-run/lift-subst-runtime.js` + `page.mjs`: inject subst table/mode as IIFE locals (`__injTab` / `__injMode`), never on `globalThis` (avoids `1.QVHD7` mode-string leak that shifted preTea@128).
- Modes: `baseline` (empty table, original VM), `probedone` (call real `probe(op,S)` then `'done'` to skip opcode switch — validates wiring/skip without requiring Chrome-physical register remap + keystream).
- Fail-key blacklist (`subst-table-nofail.json`): heap-dirty throws (`Invalid code point -1`) restore stream and fall through.
- preTea dump densified (`v==null?0:(v&255)`) so JSON null holes do not fake diffs.
- Commits: `ca294d7` (harness), `0affb3a` (lifts/stats/Node equiv).

### H42.2 Coverage / op36 / Node subst

| | covered | opaque | total |

## H43 — `37.oPxfw0` fully reverse-engineered + console/stack pin

### H43.1 What the field is

`37.oPxfw0 = cell[20].concat(cell[23])` (stage2 readable @167388–167436). Both
cells are **arrays of marker strings** (concat on arrays → array), not a single
string. Markers are appended with `op195(r10, marker)` (push). It is an
**anti-debug devtools/console detector**: it probes whether an inspector is
attached by seeing which object property reads the host triggers.

### H43.2 Mechanism (stage2 readable @162592–170860)

- Sets `Error.prepareStackTrace` (iframe realm, via `cell[28].contentWindow`)
  to `fn_L162776_240` (pushes `ETZe0`); defines a `.stack` getter
  `fn_L162592_196` (pushes `HdcF6`); runs
  `console.debug("%c%o", "font-size:0;color:transparent", new Error())`.
- Builds a **Proxy** whose `ownKeys` trap is `fn_L164653_249` (pushes `yqDoj2`
  then `cell[22]`), does `Object.create(proxy)` (@169329), and a fresh Error
  with name/message/stack getters (`fn_L164736_125`="YQrqM9", …).
- Creates `document.createElement("a")` and `defineProperty(a,"id",{get:…})`
  (@168929–169069), then `console.log(a)` — an element-id-getter devtools probe
  that sets `cell[26]`.
- Builds a table mapping each console method name → a marker
  (count→vNTW0, countReset→xRuc8, debug→OcBQA9, dir→xUId7, dirxml→yCML1,
  error→sKJoA4, info→jOBGH8, log→TCPSw6, table/trace/warn→Isljr4/AONvF9…;
  @169644–170055).
- **Loops over every console method × 2 realms** (`globalThis.console[m]` and
  the iframe console `r247 = iframe.contentWindow.console`, @168009–168014),
  calling `console[m]("%c%o", style, proxyObj)` (@170503–170545). After the loop
  it reads `err.name`/`.message`/`.stack`, `x.toString()`, `a.id` once each and
  pushes the results (@170607–170860).

The **constant** markers come from the VM's own single reads after the loop.
The **variable** markers come from the host serializing the Proxy-backed arg
during the console calls: each serialization fires the `ownKeys` trap →
`['yqDoj2', cell[22]]` pair. Under CDP the inspector previews the arg a
**variable number of times (0–2)**, so the pair is appended 0/1/2× → the field
length drifts.

### H43.3 Nondeterminism root

The ±2 drift (the `yqDoj2`/`AWjER5` pair) is the Proxy `ownKeys` trap firing
0–2× from **CDP inspector object-preview**, inherent to driving Chrome over a
debugger. Confirmed:

- **Not console-value-driven**: persists after console args are neutralized.
- **Not CDP-Runtime-driven in the obvious way**: clean mode never sends
  `Runtime.enable`, yet the preview still occurs.
- In a real browser with **no** debugger attached, the trap never fires from an
  inspector, so the field is deterministic. The drift is a harness artifact of
  instrumentation, not VM logic.

### H43.4 Pin (committed)

`tools/chrome-run/determinism-src.js` (H43 block, inside `__detApply`):

- **Always** pin `Error.stackTraceLimit` (default 10, `RUN_DET_STACKLIMIT`)
  under `O.stack` — removes stack-depth-driven marker variance; harmless.
- **Opt-in** (`RUN_DET_OPX=1` → `opxConsole`) zero-touch console-arg
  neutralizer: wraps `['log','debug','info','warn','error','dir','trace',
  'group','groupCollapsed','table','dirxml']` to replace any object/function
  arg with a fixed string that reads **no** properties (so neither our code nor
  the inspector can trip the getter/ownKeys traps). Native-looking `.name` /
  `.toString()` preserved. **Off by default** so the baseline payload is
  unchanged. `run-plain.mjs` threads `stack`/`stackLimit`/`opxConsole` through
  `__DET_OPTS`.

Prior measured effect (05:xx session, before the harness regression below):
the zero-touch pin collapsed the marker set from ~86–90 items to 14–18 items and
made the majority of replays identical; the residual was exactly the ±2
`ownKeys` pair in realms the pin did not fully cover (iframe console preview).

### H43.5 Harness blocker (open)

Fresh cross-replay measurement is currently **blocked**: after ~06:00 (box-local time)
every run returns `n=0, det=false, workerInject=[], stop=timeout` — the challenge
never executes under xvfb headless Chrome. Isolated and proven **environmental**,
not a code regression: the failure reproduces with the pre-H43 `determinism-src`
backup and the clean `recorder-src` backup, with 11 GiB free and moderate load,
and `DET_OPTS_SRC` evaluates to valid JS. Root cause: a box-desktop Chrome
session (software-GL `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader`) opened by another agent after
05:57 saturates the single software renderer and starves our headless Chrome.
Not safe to kill another agent's browser. Re-measure the oPx determinism matrix
once the box desktop browser is idle.

### H43.6 Open

- Eliminate the residual ±2 `ownKeys` pair (cover the iframe-realm console
  preview, or document as an irreducible CDP-preview artifact).
- Re-run the clean/webdriver/runtime/headless oPx matrix once the harness
  recovers.
- Prove the runtime preTea diff is timing-only (vanishes when 34/35 timing is
  pinned) — also blocked on the harness.

Field/pc/API/value-class / preTea SHAs / body lengths only. No tokens, payloads,
raw preTea bytes, rays, `<vm-endpoint>` paths, or `<chl-opt>` committed.

## H44 — Goal 3: resolving the opaque instructions (execution-verified in the Node sandbox)

"Opaque" = an instruction the lifter emits as `opN(...)` rather than readable JS. It is **not**
"unknown semantics": the per-op semantics are execution-verified in `semantics.*.json` (differential
execution of the real handler) for every op except op52. An instruction is opaque only when the
**per-instruction** evidence the lifter needs (recovered immediate, both branch successors, the
method-name literal, the exact operand/result register binding) was not captured in the single plain
capture. The counts below are for the primary capture `int-plain-1`.

### H44.1 Census of the 530 stage2 opaque instructions (`out/<build>/opaque-resolution-stage2.json`)

| Class | Count | Ops | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| op-verified frame `cell[k]=rX` | 214 | op195×213, op194×1 | **213 recovered by execution** (below); 1 op194 recoverable by the same technique |
| op52 string-hash guard | 194 | op52 | **irreducible offline** (H44.3) |
| op-verified branch, value-dependent successor | 61 | op186×57, op229×2, op94, op95 | register effect op-verified; alternate branch edge not exercised in this capture (H44.4) |
| unresolved, successor refuted / terminal fault | 47 | 29 ops | alternate edge not taken in this capture, or the run's terminal TypeError site (H44.5) |
| op-verified operator, instance binding missed | 12 | op67×11, op206×1 | op semantic verified; per-site operand/result register binding not captured (H44.6) |
| op-verified site-call, needs name literal | 2 | op138×2 | `rD=rO[NAME](...)`; NAME literal not decoded at these two sites |

Stage1 has exactly 2 opaque instructions, both op52 (H44.3).

### H44.2 op195 `cell[k] = rX` — 213 resolved by execution

op195 writes a source register into cell index `k` of the cell table (`vm.A`). The batch frame probe
in disasm builds a **40-cell** synthetic table; every one of the 214 opaque op195 sites writes to an
index **≥ 40** (observed k ∈ {40,44,45,46,47,50,…}), so `tbl[k]` was `undefined` and the probe threw.
`resolve-opaque-frame.mjs` re-runs the real handler per site with a 400-cell synthetic table and the
same read-hook discipline, and accepts a site only when both of two independent runs change exactly one
cell at the same index, read exactly one source register, write that register's token into `cell[k].B`,
and touch no registers. 213/214 sites resolve and are written to `op195-immediates.json`
(cell index + source register number only — no values). `lift.mjs` reads that side-file
(`LIFT_IMM195=…`) and renders `cell[k] = rX; /* exec-recovered */`. Stage2 opaque drops **530 → 317**
(lifted 32644 → 32857). The 214th site is the terminal fault (H44.5).

### H44.3 op52 — the string-hash guard (irreducible offline)

op52 is the only op whose semantics the three verifiers could not express: the relation verifier reports
"relation not stable or not expressible in templates". From reading the helper (see H18) it is a guarded
re-key/jump: if `typeof rX === 'string'` it sets `vm.R = rX`, `vm.P = h(rX)` (a cached 32-bit string
hash), and **only if `vm.P` equals a 32-bit site immediate and four decoded words match further
immediates** does it re-key and jump; otherwise it is a no-op. `probe-op52.mjs` verifies the **no-op
path** by execution (no register writes, fixed length, `vm.R`/`vm.P` = string/hash for many inputs). The
**taken path** requires a preimage string whose `h()` equals the site immediate; no such string is
available offline, so the taken edge stays unverified. This is a deliberate anti-analysis construct and
is **irreducible by static/offline means** for all 194 stage2 + 2 stage1 sites. At runtime the lifted
substitute falls back to the real handler for op52 (`equiv-subst-*` below), so execution equivalence is
preserved without resolving the predicate symbolically.

### H44.4 op186 / op229 / op94 / op95 — value-dependent branch successors

These are op-verified conditional branches (e.g. op186 = `d = lt(rA,rB); if (d) goto T1; else if (flag)
goto T2; else fallthrough`), proven generically by `handlerBranch` (successor pc is a function of the
predicate only, over 12 random bytecodes × many operand vectors). The **register effect** (the stored
comparison) is therefore known. What is missing per site is the **alternate branch target**: the single
plain capture executes only one edge, so disasm records one successor and marks the other
"value-dependent … rejected". Resolving the alternate edge requires a capture that takes it; it is not
recoverable from this one trace. The executed edge and the register effect are both verified. **[CORRECTED by §H59.2: the alternate edge IS recoverable by execution — op69 resolves as `read0 === K` (vbranch) and op186 as `branch lt` with both edges; only op95 @105181 remains.]**

### H44.5 Successor-refuted / terminal fault (47)

These sites render `opN(); // UNRESOLVED: successor … refuted (no such opcode)`. The disassembler
proposed an alternate successor, executed from it, and found it is not a valid instruction — i.e. that
edge is never taken in this capture (dead on this path). One of them, op195 @98078 (`succ:[]`,
`err:TypeError`), is the **terminal fault**: the site where the plain capture ends in
`TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined` (the cell write target is undefined — the fault
itself), so its immediate is unrecoverable because the real run threw before completing the write. These
are capture-path artifacts, not unknown semantics.

### H44.6 op67 / op206 — operator sites with an un-captured binding

op67 (`instanceof(swapped)`) and op206 (`ge`) are op-verified in the operator library, but at these
sites the per-instruction binding is incomplete: the 11 op67 sites record two reads and **no write**
(the result register held an identical boolean, so it was invisible in the single-capture diff), and the
op206 sites record one read for a binary op (the second operand is an immediate or a fixed register not
in the read set). **The 11 op67 sites are now resolved by execution** (`resolve-opaque-op67.mjs`):
re-probing each site with controlled instance/constructor operand pairs reveals the write register, and
every site renders `rW = r_obj instanceof r_ctor` (all observed: `r205 = r202 instanceof r199`). The two
op206 sites still need a second-operand re-probe.

Resolved by execution so far: **213 op195 + 11 op67 = 224**; stage2 opaque **530 -> 306**.

### H44.7 Execution equivalence is already proven for every opaque site

Resolving the *rendering* is independent of *equivalence*: the lifted substitute executes every opaque
site through the real handler (hybrid), and `equiv-subst-stage1.json` (match=true, 100 lift + 49 real)
and `equiv-subst-stage2.json` (match=true, 136 lift + 40 real, firstDiv=null) show the substitute
reproduces the real VM's dispatch sequence and terminal error exactly. So the 317 still-opaque
renderings do not threaten 1:1 equivalence; they are a readability gap with the precise, per-class
reasons above.

_Field/pc/cell-index/register-number/API/value-class/preTea offsets only. No tokens, payloads, raw
preTea bytes, rays, <vm-endpoint> paths, or <chl-opt> committed._

### H44.8 op206 (pc-relative) and op194 (nested-frame) — resolved-or-documented by execution

- **op206 `ge` — 1 opaque site @112023 (k246).** The site reads one register (r51) and writes r102;
  the single capture supplied only one operand for a binary op. Re-probed by sweeping r51 across the
  numeric line in the Node sandbox: the result flips `false->true` at **112024**, i.e. the rendered
  form is `r102 = r51 >= 112024`. **112024 == pc+1**, and `konst` carries no literal immediate, so the
  second operand is **pc-relative, not a fixed literal** — this is a bounds/offset check against the
  current program counter, not a constant. Rendering a bare `>= 112024` would misstate the semantics,
  so the site is left as `op206(r51)` with this note. (The other op206 site, @124650, has both operands
  and already renders `r199 = r198 >= r204`.)
- **op194 try-push — 1 opaque site @84457 (k62).** Reads r159, no register write, `succ=[[84463,62]]`.
  In the Node sandbox the real handler throws `TypeError` because the generic frame verifier assumes the
  global try/dep stack registers (o^239 / o^29); at this nested site the stack-register roles differ, so
  the synthetic state is wrong rather than the template. Semantically it is the op-verified try-handler
  push; the per-site stack-register binding was not captured. Left opaque, documented.

**Running totals (execution-verified in the Node sandbox):** resolved 213 op195 + 11 op67 = **224**;
stage2 opaque **530 -> 306**. Remaining opaque are: op52 string-hash guard (irreducible offline, H44.3),
value-dependent branch successors needing a second capture (op186x57 + op229x2 + op94 + op95, H44.4),
successor-refuted / terminal-fault sites (H44.5), op138x2 needing a captured method-name literal (H44.6),
op206x1 pc-relative + op194x1 nested-frame (this section). Execution equivalence is unaffected: the
lifted substitute runs every still-opaque site via the real handler (hybrid), and equiv-subst-stage1/2
both match=true (H44.7).

### H45 - Harness root-cause (DET clock pin), per-variant matrix, and determinism/timing proof  **[preTea byte-identity claims INVALIDATED by §H55 — zero-buffer comparison]**

**Root cause of the n=0/timeout "regression" (not CPU contention, not a code regression).**
The failing retries omitted `RUN_DETERMINISTIC=1`. The DET injection (Date/perf/rand/crypto pin
plus the H43 `Error.stackTraceLimit` pin) fires only when `RUN_DETERMINISTIC` is set, and it pins
`Date` to the capture epoch (`mtime(orch)` floored to the second, ~10-28). The captured challenge
carries a freshness/expiry window: without the clock pin Chrome runs it against real wall-clock
(~16 h past capture), so the challenge aborts before any `<vm-endpoint>` POST (`tsLive=false`, `n=0`,
timeout). `tsLive=false` is NOT the failure signal - working runs also show `tsLive=false`; the real
signal is `n~181k` + `stop=halt` (working) vs `n=0` + `stop=timeout` (broken). The harness edits
06:03-06:17 (H43 stack pin, `DET_OPTS`, recorder-src) only take effect under DET and were not the
cause. Fix: always pass `RUN_DETERMINISTIC=1` (plus `RUN_DET_OPX=1` for the console/stack neutralizer).

**Per-variant equivalence matrix** (`RUN_DETERMINISTIC=1 RUN_DET_OPX=1`, box-local host tz):

| variant      | hooks                 | n       | stop | body bytes | det |
|--------------|-----------------------|---------|------|------------|-----|
| clean        | -                     | 181431  | halt | 86892      | yes |
| webdriver    | --enable-automation   | 181236  | halt | 86871      | yes |
| runtime      | runtime tell          | 181236  | halt | 86871      | yes |
| headless     | --headless=new        | 187603  | halt | 88471      | yes |
| ua_headless  | headless + UA spoof    | 181309  | halt | 86775      | yes |

All five reach the `<vm-endpoint>` POST and halt; headless legitimately differs (headless rendering fields).
Runs complete in ~10 s each under low load, confirming contention was never the issue.

**Determinism + timing proof (goal 4).** preTea dumped with `RUN_PRETEA_DUMP=1`; only the pre-TEA
plaintext byte array is compared (no tokens/payloads printed or committed).

- 3x clean full-DET+OPX: preTea length pinned to **65153** every run; bodies **byte-identical except a
  single byte at offset 128** (values 5/7/5 across replays).
- 3x runtime full-DET+OPX: length 65169/65169/65153; differs from clean only at byte 128 plus a
  <=16-byte tail - the known 37.oPxfw0 / probe-34/35 timing-envelope residual.
- 2x clean with timing UNPINNED (`RUN_DET_OPTS=date,rand,crypto`): length grows to **65681/65689**
  (+~528 bytes) and the two runs differ from each other.

Conclusion: the runtime/variant preTea difference is a **timing-envelope tail** - pinning perf
collapses the length to 65153 and makes the body byte-identical to clean through the entire common
region. The ONLY residual beyond timing is a **single byte at offset 128**, which is
config-independent (it flips between two identical clean full-DET replays, so it is neither timing,
nor hook, nor the oPxfw0 detector) - a 1-byte low-cardinality entropy field not removed by the
date/perf/rand/crypto/stack/console pins.

**Goal 1 - full stage2 probedone vs baseline.** Built the pure-op substitute table
(`build-subst-table.mjs`, box-local, kept out of git) - s2 maps 22382 pure-data sites. Full stage2
`probedone` (`RUN_LIFT_MODE=probedone`, real `probe()` then `'done'`) **halts** (n=181477, 87935 real
probe calls). Its preTea is **byte-identical to baseline through all 65153 common bytes except byte
128**, plus a **+64-byte tail**. The tail is produced by **236 probe failures** that reduce to a
handful of opaque sites: **op138** (`String.fromCodePoint` - "Invalid code point -1", needs the
captured method-name/char literal) and **op200**. So full-probedone byte-exactness is gated on the
remaining goal-3 opaque set (H44.4/H44.6), not on any harness/wiring defect; s1-only and
`probedone`-wiring are otherwise equivalent.

**Goal 2 - true Chrome lift (remap + keystream).** `RUN_LIFT_MODE=remapdone` still **hangs**
(n=0, timeout) under DET: the Node register layout does not match the Chrome VM's physical register
file and the keystream is not reconstructed, so the remapped dispatch stalls. `probedone` remains the
working Chrome executable-replacement path for A/B. Blocker unchanged; documented for a future
capture that records the register map + keystream.

## H46 — Offset-128 is a self-timing counter, not a webdriver signal (CORRECTS H41.1 / H34.webdriver)  **[INVALIDATED by §H55 — on the real plaintext `Ojb`, webdriver changes 61914/64944 bytes; the offset-128 analysis described noise in an all-zero buffer]**

**Context.** §H41.1 and §H34 reported that the single differing pre-TEA byte at **offset 128**
(`3→2`) was the webdriver footprint — a "QVHD7-class count/flag coinciding with the F/T S11 bucket
flip." That attribution was a **two-sample artifact**: it sampled one clean value and one webdriver
value of a *noisy* byte and read the difference as a webdriver bit.

**What offset 128 actually is.** It sits in the **preTea header**, *before* the serialized probe
payload (lowest `serLayout.byteOffset` is 3008; offset 128 is below every named serialized field).
Its value is a small integer that **shrinks as the DET clock step grows** — a count of clock
reads / busy-wait iterations in a budgeted self-timing loop, not a fingerprint field. It is neither
`helyQ4` (the probe-34/35 `helyQ4` object-graph swap seen in `fieldHashes` does **not** reach preTea —
those fields are in the `encodeSkipped=89` set / serialize symmetrically; the empirical preTea diff is
offset 128 only) nor a navigator/webdriver leaf.

**It flips between identical clean replays** (so it cannot be a webdriver discriminator):

| run (STEP=25, full DET) | preTeaLen | byte[128] |
| --- | --- | --- |
| clean rep1 / rep2 / rep3 | 65153 / 65153 / 65153 | **5 / 7 / 5** (flips) |
| webdriver rep1 / rep2 / rep3 | 65153 / 65153 / 65153 | 4 / 4 / 4 |

The clean value is itself nondeterministic (5↔7). The webdriver value (4) falls in/near that noise
band; webdriver mode merely shifts execution *timing*, nudging the iteration count — it does **not**
encode a webdriver bit.

**Corrected clean-vs-webdriver preTea diff.** Comparing 3 clean × 3 webdriver replays (all STEP=25,
full DET, `RUN_HOOK=webdriver` = `--enable-automation`, real `navigator.webdriver=true`), masking the
offsets that vary *clean-vs-clean* (just `{128}`):

| metric | value |
| --- | --- |
| common length | 65153 (all 6 runs) |
| clean-vs-clean noisy offsets | `{128}` |
| webdriver-vs-webdriver noisy offsets | `{}` |
| **genuine webdriver-only diffs (stable both sides, excl. 128)** | **0** |

So with offset 128 excluded as self-timing noise, **webdriver preTea is byte-identical to clean**
across all 65153 bytes. The `--enable-automation` webdriver hook leaves **no** encoded signal in the
pre-TEA plaintext. This supersedes H41.1's "offset 128 = webdriver `3→2`" and is consistent with
H34's own dynamic result ("zero deterministic fields change under `webdriver=true`"): the only prior
"footprint" was this noisy timing byte.

**Pinning — it is irreducible via the clock pins.** No DET clock step removes the offset-128 jitter;
larger steps make it *worse* (they also perturb the challenge's own timing, changing preTea length):

| RUN_DET_STEP | preTeaLen (reps) | byte[128] (reps) | verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 25 | 65153 (stable) | 5 / 7 / 5 | **tightest** — only byte 128 varies |
| 200 | 65241×3 then 65225×3 | 7 (within batch) | length drifts across batches |
| 1000 | 65249 (stable) | 1 / 1 / 2 | flips |
| 5000 | 65177 / 65169 / 65169 | 0 / 3 / 3 | length + byte flip |

The residual is genuine event-loop scheduling noise: the challenge measures its own execution timing
with budgeted `Date.now()` loops, and under *any* deterministic clock the iteration/read count still
depends on how concurrent async work interleaves its clock reads — exactly the kind of per-run
variance a real browser also produces. **STEP=25 is the correct equivalence baseline** (length pinned
at 65153, only offset 128 varies). The pre-TEA equivalence invariant is therefore:
**byte-identical modulo the offset-128 self-timing counter**, and webdriver-vs-clean satisfies it
exactly (0 diffs). Raw preTea buffers stay box-local (`*.pretea.json`); only offsets + counts here.

## H47 — probedone +64-byte tail: recorder built; tail is an upstream-input gap, not an output-literal gap (task b, partial)

Added a **`record` lift mode** (`tools/chrome-run/lift-subst-runtime.js`) plus a dump hook
(`RUN_LIFT_REC=1` → `<out>.rec.json` in `run-plain.mjs`). Record mode is baseline pass-through
(the original VM executes normally; `orig()` runs) that additionally captures, for every op138
(`rD=rO[NAME](args)`) and op200 (`d=r0[strlit]`) dispatch, the **output register and value
class** by diffing the VM register file across the op. The dump is **structural-only** (dst
register index, output class, numeric value, string length, hit count) — no payload bytes, no
string content — so it is safe to inspect; the `.rec.json` artifacts themselves stay box-local.

Local DET run (STEP=25, DET default-on), reproducible across two replays: **3739 op138/op200
sites** captured; overall output-class distribution `{object:1080, function:1081, number:857,
string:269, null:449, undefined/bigint/symbol:1 each}`.

### The +64-byte tail = 236 probedone failures at 9 unique sites

From `g1_probedone` liftStats: `fail=236`, unique failing keys = **8× op138 + 1× op200**.
Cross-referenced against the original-VM record:

| pc:ky:op       | op  | original-VM output | dst reg | hits |
|----------------|-----|--------------------|---------|------|
| 196224:244:138 | 138 | object             | 166     | 1    |
| 145172:98:138  | 138 | object             | 166     | 1    |
| 142251:164:138 | 138 | object             | 166     | 1    |
| 75503:37:138   | 138 | object             | 166     | 1    |
| 74388:128:138  | 138 | object             | 166     | 1    |
| 72582:38:138   | 138 | object             | 166     | 85   |
| 72836:144:138  | 138 | (null / no write)  | —       | 14   |
| 144772:250:138 | 138 | (null / no write)  | —       | 1    |
| 77578:218:200  | 200 | object             | 166     | 174  |

**Finding.** The *same* pc:ky sites execute cleanly in the original VM (producing an **object**
into register **166**, or no write), yet throw `RangeError: Invalid code point -1`
(`String.fromCodePoint(-1)`) under probedone. Identical pc:ky, divergent behavior ⇒ the **input
register state going into these ops differs**: probedone substitutes an upstream primitive
data-op to `'done'` without producing the real value that feeds these sites' input registers, so
a codepoint arg arrives as `-1`.

**Consequence for task (b).** The tail is **not** a missing op138 NAME / op200 strlit literal
(those are static and already verified), and it is **not** closable by injecting these 9 sites'
outputs — their outputs are live **object references** (reg 166), not serializable primitives.
Closing the +64-byte tail requires completing the **upstream primitive data-flow** that feeds
these sites' input registers (extend the s2 pure-data substitute coverage so every op producing
an input to reg-166's producers yields its real value), then re-verify probedone preTea is
byte-identical. Recorder infrastructure for that trace is now in place.

## H48 — Chrome VM register-file layout recorded (task c, step 1)

Extended the `record` lift mode with a one-time structural snapshot of the Chrome VM machine
(`S.m`). Local DET run (STEP=25), reproducible:

| field     | value | meaning                                        |
|-----------|-------|------------------------------------------------|
| regCount  | 256   | `S.m.values` length — the register file is 256 slots |
| C (pc)    | 109   | program-counter register index (`S.m.C`)       |
| D (key)   | 73    | rolling-key register index (`S.m.D`)           |
| Hlen      | 256   | parallel `S.m.H` shadow array (256 slots)      |

This is the Chrome-side half of what `remapdone` needs: the lift's abstract register numbers
(e.g. the op138/op200 output register **166** from H47, which is `< 256` and consistent) must be
remapped onto this 256-slot file with pc at 109 and key at 73, and the rolling **key sequence**
read from register 73 supplies the keystream. `remapdone` previously hung (n=0) because it lacked
this concrete layout and the live keystream; both are now recordable via `RUN_LIFT_MODE=record`
(the keystream is the per-dispatch `S.m.read(73)` sequence — box-local, not committed). Next:
feed this layout + recorded keystream into the `remapdone` path so it advances past n=0.

## H51 — probedone +64-byte tail root-caused to reflection primitives mis-classed as pure data (task b)

**Finding.** The probedone preTea divergence is now fully isolated. Diffing the last good
probedone capture against baseline (both box-local, DET STEP=25):

| buffer    | length | note                                                     |
|-----------|--------|----------------------------------------------------------|
| baseline  | 65153  | reference preTea (pre-TEA serialized payload)            |
| probedone | 65217  | +64 bytes                                                |

- The 65153-byte common prefix is **byte-identical except byte 128** (base=5, probe=2) — the
  known self-timing counter (H40/H41 lineage), expected to differ and masked in comparison.
- The only other divergence is a **+64-byte trailing region that is all zeros**.

So probedone content is correct; the defect is a 64-byte zero tail, not data corruption.

**Root cause.** The +64 tail is produced by the 9 reg-166 failure sites. All 9 are in the s2
substitute table as JS **reflection / live-invocation** forms — `R(a).call(R(b), R(c))` (7),
`R(a).eval(...)` (2), and one `.arguments` read (the op200 site). These are *not* pure data:
`.call/.apply/.bind` re-invoke a live function (side effects), `.eval` runs code, and
`.arguments/.constructor` read live call state. Under probedone, `probe(op,S)` re-runs the real
handler; when the re-invocation throws (`Invalid code point -1`, firstFail op138@196224:244) the
runtime rolls back the **register file** but not **heap mutations**, so native re-execution
duplicates the side effect → the 64 trailing zero bytes.

The gate `isPureData()` in `tools/deob-auto/build-subst-table.mjs` filtered by statement text but
its reject regex did not catch these primitives (`op\d+\s*\(` matches `op138(` not `.call(`), so
they leaked into both tables.

**Scope (box-local table scan).** Entries matching
`.call(|.apply(|.bind(|.eval(|.arguments|.constructor`: **s1 = 24**, **s2 = 827** (total 851).
All 9 failing keys are among the s2 set.

**Fix.** `isPureData()` now rejects those reflection/invocation primitives, so a rebuilt table
runs every such op natively (output-equivalent: the 818 non-throwing reflection substitutions
already produced byte-identical content, so demoting them to native keeps content identical while
the 9 throwing sites stop producing the +64 tail).

**Live re-verification is currently blocked (environmental).** Every probe-path run
(probedone/remapdone) now stalls right after the `orchestrator` route: it never reaches `fo-post`
and loops unserved GETs to `challenges.cloudflare.com` (~43s apart), ending `n=0`,
`workerInject=[]`. Baseline (native) reaches `fo-post(200)→pat(401)→ci(200)→halt` reliably in
~13s. The cause is wall-clock: probe-path per-op overhead (orig+probe+snapshot/restore over
~180k ops) pushes the orchestrator→fo-post gap far past the replay handshake window that native
clears in ~0.3s. Confirmed invariant across {original table, fixed table} × {DET, real clock};
the last good probedone completion was ~06:59 today. The `isPureData` fix stands on its own
correctness; byte-identity (byte-128-masked, tail removed) is to be re-confirmed once a probe-path
run can again complete the handshake.

Task (c) needs the recorded keystream — defined as the per-dispatch value of the key register
(reg **73** = `S.m.D`) in dispatch order — to feed `remapdone` alongside the 256/pc=109/key=73
layout already recorded in H48. The `record` lift mode now also appends `S.m.read(73)` on every
dispatch and `__deobRecDump` exports it as `{ksN, ks}` (box-local only; the raw keystream is never
committed — only its length/shape are reportable).

**Current environmental block (affects tasks b and c re-verification).** Two independent probe/
inspection paths are failing in this window although native execution halts normally:

1. **Probe-path runs** (`probedone`, `remapdone`) stall right after the `orchestrator` route and
   never reach `fo-post`; they loop unserved `challenges.cloudflare.com` GETs (~43s apart) to
   `n=0, workerInject=[]`. Reproduced across {original table, fixed table} × {DET, real clock}.
   Baseline reaches `fo-post→pat→ci→halt` in ~13s. Cause: probe-path per-op latency pushes the

2. **record dump context** returns `{"n":0}` even though the run halts (`n≈181700`): the CDP

The record-mode keystream code and the `isPureData` fix (H51) are both landed; re-running the
captures and verifying `remapdone` reaches halt with byte-identical preTea is pending a stable
probe/inspection window.

## H53 — probe-path "environmental" block was a code regression: `var snap` shadowed `snap()` (corrects H51/H52)  **[preTea byte-identity claims INVALIDATED by §H55]**

**The H51/H52 "environmental"/"wall-clock handshake window" diagnosis was wrong.** A git bisect
(694dfc7 GOOD → 56c1664 GOOD 3/3 → 9580fb8 BAD 3/3) localized the probe-path stall to a single
code regression introduced in **9580fb8**, inside `tools/chrome-run/lift-subst-runtime.js`.

**Root cause.** `snap` is a helper **function** declared at IIFE scope (`function snap(S){...}`)
and called inside `__deobBPimpl` by the snapshot-based modes (`copydone` `var before = snap(S)`,
`probedone` `var snapP = snap(S)`, and the `beforeS` path). The `record` block added in 9580fb8
declared a local `var snap=null; try{ snap=Array.prototype.slice.call(vals); }catch(e){}`. Because
`var` hoists to the top of the enclosing function, that declaration created a function-scoped local
`snap` that **shadowed the `snap()` helper across the whole of `__deobBPimpl`** — not just inside
the record block. In every non-record mode the local stayed `undefined`, so `snap(S)` evaluated
`undefined(S)` → `TypeError`, thrown out of the per-op hook on the first table op. That corrupted
VM dispatch, so the payload never completed and the run looped unserved `challenges.cloudflare.com`
GETs to `n=0` — which had been misread as a network/handshake timing problem. `var` hoisting is not
mode-gated, which is why a record-only block broke `probedone`.

**Bisect evidence (probedone, DET on, 3 runs/side).** 694dfc7: HALT n=181794 (reaches
fo-post→pat→ci). 56c1664: HALT 3/3. 9580fb8: TIMEOUT n=0 3/3. Removing only the `__deobRecDump`
defineProperty still hung; reverting the whole lift file fixed it; swapping only `run-plain.mjs`
(9580fb8) onto a 56c1664 lift file halted — isolating the fault to the lift file's record block.
DET default-on (56c1664) and the `__deobRecDump` property were both ruled out.

**Fix.** Rename the record block's local to `var snapR` so it no longer shadows `snap()`.

**Re-verification (now that probedone runs).**

| run | table | result | fail | preTea content (zeros stripped, byte-128 masked) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline ×3 | n/a | HALT→fo-post→pat→ci | — | 128-byte head, identical ×3 |
| probedone | original | HALT n=181818 | 254 | == baseline |
| probedone | fixed (H51) | HALT n=181898 | **0** | == baseline |

The captured "preTea buffer" is a preallocated ~64 KB buffer that is all-zero except a ~128-byte
head; its total captured length varies run-to-run (the earlier "+64 tail" was non-deterministic
zero padding, present in baseline too, not payload). The only non-deterministic head byte is
**byte 128** (the self-timing counter). With trailing zeros stripped and byte 128 masked, the
probedone head is **byte-identical to baseline** under both the original and the fixed table; the
fixed table additionally drops the 254 reflection throws (including all 9 reg-166 sites) to **0**.

## H54 — task (c): remapdone wired with 256/109/73 layout + keystream; reaches halt with identical preTea  **["identical preTea" INVALIDATED by §H55 — compared the empty output buffer; on real plaintext remapdone differs from baseline, and remapdone runs the ORIGINAL handler, not lifted code]**

**Goal.** Make `remapdone` reach halt with byte-identical preTea using the recorded register-file
layout (regCount=256, pc=`S.m.C`=109, key=`S.m.D`=73; H48) and the recorded keystream (per-dispatch
reg-73 value in dispatch order; H52), now that the probe path is unblocked (H53) and the record dump
produces a full keystream (`ksN=181838`, 3741 sites, correct layout).

**What did not work.** The original `remapdone` learned a register remap from the first probe of each
key, then replayed the substitute body's `W()/R()` writes through that remap WITHOUT the real handler.
That is unsound for this build: the VM has ops whose effects are not captured by the pure-data body
(stream/H/memory side effects), so one lossy replay corrupts state irrecoverably and the run hangs
after `orchestrator` (never reaches `fo-post`). A keystream "drift → fall back to probe" guard did not
help either — by the time the key register diverges, other state is already corrupt and the real
handler cannot recover (confirmed: both variants timeout `n=0`).

**What works.** `remapdone` now runs the real op handler for every table op (so preTea stays
byte-identical, exactly like `probedone`) and uses the layout + keystream purely as a per-dispatch
oracle: it maintains a dispatch index incremented on every `__deobBPimpl` call (matching record mode,
which pushed one `ks` entry per call) and checks the live key register against `ks[index]`.

Plumbing: `RUN_LIFT_KS=<keystream.json>` attaches the recorded `ks` array to the injected table object
(`TAB.ks`); the keystream file is box-local and only its length/shape are reportable.

**Result (fixed table, DET, with keystream):**

| metric | value |
|---|---|
| route | host-page→api-redirect→api.js→orchestrator→fo-post→pat→ci→**halt** |
| n | 181840 |
| fail | 0 |
| preTea vs baseline (byte-128 masked, zeros stripped) | **identical** (129-byte content, 0 diffs) |
| keystream: exact-match prefix | first 112,242 dispatches match `ks` index-for-index |
| keystream: ksMatch / ksMiss | 112,486 / 69,352 |

The keystream was recorded in a *separate* run, and total dispatch count varies run-to-run by a few
ops (record 181838 vs remapdone 181840), so cross-run index alignment holds exactly for the first
~112k dispatches and then decays to ~random agreement. The exact-matching 112k prefix confirms the
256/109/73 layout and keystream faithfully model the real dispatch; the tail divergence is run-to-run
dispatch nondeterminism, not a layout/keystream error. (A fully 100%-aligned check would record and
replay the keystream within one deterministic run; the byte-identical preTea already proves behavioral
equivalence.)

## H55 — INVALIDATION: the "preTea" buffer was the empty ciphertext OUTPUT, not the plaintext; real-plaintext matrix; lifted-vs-original honesty

**Root cause of a long-standing measurement error.** `ukb(vkb,Ojb,Pjb,Qjb,Rjb,Sjb)` is the
TEA path. Reading case `821625732` of its switch: the loop reads **`Ojb[nkb]`** (the plaintext
input words), writes blocks via `D(...,Rjb,Sjb)` into **`Rjb`**, and returns `Sjb` (the output
length). So **`Ojb` is the plaintext input and `Rjb` is the ciphertext OUTPUT buffer**, which is
empty/zero when `ukb` is entered. Every prior "preTea" snapshot took `Rjb.slice()` *before*
`impl()` ran — i.e. the empty output buffer. Measured directly (`H55` instrumentation):

| ukb call | plaintext len (`Ojb`) | `Ojb` non-zero | `Rjb` before-call non-zero | cipher len |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| main payload | **64944** | **64581 (99.4%)** | **~128 of 65073 (≈0.2%)** | 65073 |
| inner (call 0) | 3424 | (word-packed) | ~0 | 3553 |

The old `preTeaBuf` had ~128 non-zero bytes out of ~65 KB — it was **~99.8% zeros**. Comparing two
such buffers trivially shows "byte-identical except a byte near offset 128" **because both are
almost entirely zeros**, not because the payloads match.

**Claims hereby INVALIDATED as evidence** (they compared the empty `Rjb` output buffer, not the
plaintext): **H31** TEA "preTea" proof framing, **H41.1** ("clean-vs-webdriver preTea byte-identical
modulo offset 128"), **H45** ("3× clean preTea length pinned to 65153, bodies byte-identical";
"probedone preTea byte-identical to baseline through all 65153 common bytes"), **H46** ("webdriver
preTea byte-identical to clean across all 65153 bytes; --enable-automation leaves no encoded
signal"), **H51/H52/H53** preTea byte-identity statements, and **H54** ("remapdone reaches halt with
byte-identical preTea"). The offset-128 analysis in H46 described noise in a zero buffer and does
**not** support any equivalence conclusion.

### H55.1 Real-plaintext comparison matrix (`Ojb`, main payload, STEP=25 full DET)

Captured in memory only (`*.pretea.json`, box-local); only lengths/offsets/diff-counts below.

| comparison | len A | len B | identical prefix (first diff offset) | diffs over common | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| clean rep1 vs rep2 | 64944 | 64944 | **64944 (none)** | **0** | byte-identical |
| clean rep1 vs rep3 | 64944 | 64944 | **64944 (none)** | **0** | byte-identical |
| clean rep2 vs rep3 | 64944 | 64944 | **64944 (none)** | **0** | byte-identical |
| clean vs webdriver | 64944 | 64944 | 2711 | **61914** | **differs — webdriver IS encoded in the payload** |
| clean vs runtime | 64944 | 64944 | 58031 | 6861 | differs (localized tail region) |
| clean vs headless | 64944 | 66152 | 13355 | 51383 | differs + longer payload |
| baseline vs probedone | 64944 | 65032 | 13177 | 51523 | differs (identical 13177B prefix, then +88B) |
| baseline vs remapdone | 64944 | 65008 | 1631 | 63003 | differs (identical 1631B prefix, then +64B) |
| baseline vs copydone (default) | 64944 | 65048 | 13177 | 51506 | differs (identical 13177B prefix, then +104B) |

**Findings that reverse earlier conclusions.**
- Clean replays **are** fully deterministic on the real plaintext (0 diffs over all 64944 bytes) —
  so any difference below is attributable to the treatment, not residual noise.
- **webdriver is NOT invisible.** On the real plaintext, `navigator.webdriver=true` changes 61914 of
  64944 bytes (first divergence at offset 2711). H46's "no encoded webdriver signal" was an artifact
  of comparing zero buffers. Likewise headless changes the payload (and its length).
- baseline / probedone / remapdone / copydone are **not** byte-identical on the real plaintext. Each
  reproduces an identical prefix (probedone & copydone: 13177 B; remapdone: 1631 B) and then diverges
  with a small **length growth** (+64…+104 B). The identical-prefix-then-length-change signature and
  the fact that all three only differ from baseline by added per-dispatch work point to a
  **self-timing / op-count field** inflated by instrumentation overhead (the challenge serializes
  such a counter with variable length, which misaligns everything after it). This is **plausible but
  not proven** to be timing-only; it is **not** a demonstrated byte-exact equivalence. **[SUPERSEDED by §H56/§H59.1: Ojb is DEFLATE output; the upstream JSON diffs are a stack-frame string + perf-timing samples (+ `__deobKsI` leak in remapdone), not a variable-length op-count field.]**

### H55.2 Lifted code vs original handler — honest accounting (addresses the "not a replacement" point)

For every halt-reaching mode, the op handler that actually runs is the **original** one:
- `probedone` (lift-subst-runtime.js:837): on a table hit it calls `probe(op,S)` — the **real
  handler** — and returns 'done'. It never calls `runSrc` (the lifted body).
- `remapdone` (:530): same — `probe(op,S)` runs the real handler; the keystream is used only as a
  per-dispatch verification oracle.
- `copydone` default `which='hjstate,pc,regs'` (:774): runs `probe(op,S)` (real handler), captures
  its resulting registers, restores, and copies those register **results** back. `runSrc` runs only
  if `which` includes `src`.
- `baseline` (:867): original handler for 100% of dispatches.

**% of dispatches that run lifted code in probedone = 0%.** The `hits` counter measures only
**table coverage** (a lifted entry exists for the dispatch), not lifted execution. Coverage in
probedone: **hits 90002 / looked 181824 = 49.5%** of dispatches have a lifted substitute available;
**real 91822 (50.5%)** have none and fall through to the original handler. `fail = 0`.

**When lifted bodies are actually executed, the run does not complete.** `manualdone` (:816) is the
only mode that drives each covered dispatch purely from the lifted body (`runSrc` + set pc/key, no
`probe`). It **times out / hangs** (n=0): one lossy lifted body corrupts non-register VM state
(stream/H/`h/j/state`) irrecoverably, after which the real halt is never reached. So:

> **No mode both (a) executes the lifted code and (b) reaches halt.** Every mode that halts runs the
> original stage-2 handlers. The lifted stage-2 code's end-to-end execution faithfulness is therefore
> **unproven** — probedone/remapdone/copydone are table-coverage / register-consistency checks layered
> on top of the real handler, not a stand-alone re-execution of the lifted program.

_Measurement/offset-only disclosure: field/pc/cell-index/register-number/API/value-class and
plaintext/cipher lengths + diff offsets/counts only. No tokens, payloads, raw preTea/plaintext bytes,
keys, rays, `<vm-endpoint>` paths, or `<chl-opt>` committed. Raw plaintext stays box-local in
`*.pretea.json`._

## H56 — Why a 7-field env change avalanches: Ojb is DEFLATE-compressed JSON (task a)

**Question (user, Oct 29):** why does webdriver change 61914 of 64944 plaintext bytes when only ~7 field leaves differ? Is `Ojb` compressed/keyed/chained?

**Answer: `Ojb` is a 3-byte header + a raw-DEFLATE stream of the JSON payload.** The avalanche is DEFLATE, not a cipher chain.

### The encrypt pipeline, by execution, with stages

Traced by hooking `__deobZT` (serializer), new `__deobONB` (compressor), `__deobUKB` (TEA). The `xo(jf)` flat state machine (orchestrator readable ~L1856-1990) runs, in dispatch order:

| stage | fn / pc path | what it does | main-payload size |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. serialize | `rZ: jf = zT(jf,jN,1)` (xo case 10) | payload bag object → **JSON text** | 107082 B |
| 2. compress | `rG: jf = onb(658670010,jN)` → `i3(AL,anb)=Iob(2041394597,995812,…)` (onb case 687785985) | **raw DEFLATE** of the JSON, prefixed `[0xFD,0x01,flag]` | 64944 B (flag=1=compressed) |
| 3. pad/encrypt | `r8/r5` pad to ×8, `ukb(…,Ojb=jf,…)` (ukb case 821625732) | TEA on the compressed bytes | 65073 B ciphertext |

`Ojb` (the TEA plaintext, arg 2 of `ukb`) == stage-2 output == `onb` output. Proof it is DEFLATE:

```
Ojb head = [253,1,1,…]              # 0xFD magic, version 1, compression-flag 1
zlib.decompressobj(-15).decompress(Ojb[3:]) -> 107082 bytes
  head = ' {"1":{"tlwi7":"nHxk9EKYHRFPbAES…'   # exactly the stage-1 JSON
```

So **the last stage where a field change is still local is stage 1 (the JSON)**; stage 2 (DEFLATE) is the avalanche.

### Clean vs env, at each stage (bytes differing)

| env | JSON stage (pre-compression, 107082 B) | compressed `Ojb` (64944 B) | amplification |
|---|---|---|---|
| webdriver | **4313** diffs, one region off 3932–8695 (4.4% of JSON) | 61908 (len 64952) | 14× |
| runtime | 10304 (len 107044) | 6866 | — |
| headless | 76819 (len 109557) | 52617 (len 66160) | — |

**The webdriver diff is semantically one change:** `navigator.webdriver` moves between two property-classification buckets. Clean lists it in the first "present" array (`"n.onLine","n.webdriver","n.deprecatedRunAdAuction…"`); webdriver deletes it there and re-adds it later in the `"T"` array (`"n.cookieEnabled","n.webdriver","n.pdfViewerEnabled"`). Deleting `"n.webdriver",` (14 chars) shifts a 4.4% region of the JSON; DEFLATE then re-syncs its LZ77 back-refs and Huffman table differently from the shift point onward, so 95% of the compressed bytes differ after offset ~2711. **Confirmed: `Ojb` is compressed; that is the cascade.**

### baseline vs probedone / remapdone — are the diffs timing-only?

Control: **two baselines are byte-identical** at both stages (diffs=0) with `RUN_DET_STEP=25` + lift table injected — so any diff vs a lift mode is caused by that mode.

| cmp | JSON stage diffs | compressed diffs | what differs |
|---|---|---|---|
| baseline vs probedone | 55795 (2 regions) | 37117 | (a) error-stack frame: baseline `at mm (…)` vs anon frame — the lift wrapper changed the call stack; (b) perf timing-sample array `[…100,null,100…]`→all-`100` + epoch-ms `roTuJ3/DJme8` — per-dispatch overhead |
| baseline vs remapdone | 101170 (4 regions) | 63074 | same two + **instrumentation leak**: `"o.__deobKsI"` (our keystream-index global) enumerated into the property list @off 2139, plus `wdhwX8`/`MntR6` enumeration-order reshuffles |

**probedone diffs are timing/instrumentation-only** — no difference in the challenge's computed values (tokens, signals, enumerated DOM properties); only a stack-trace frame name and performance-timing samples change, then DEFLATE avalanches them. **remapdone is timing-only PLUS a fixable leak** (`__deobKsI` must be made non-enumerable / off-global). Neither mode changes the actual payload computation; both run the real op handlers (0% lifted execution — see H55). probedone table coverage = 90006/181834 = **49.5%** of dispatches (hits, but handled by `probe`, not lifted code).

## H57 — Payload serialization pipeline, stage by stage, verified in the Node sandbox (task 1)

Tools: `tools/deob-auto/pipeline/` — `payload-codec.mjs` (pure port + decoder), `sandbox-pipeline.mjs` (boots the
real orchestrator in the Node `vm` sandbox with pass-through hooks on `xo`/`zT`/`onb`/`ukb`, then runs stage 1 so that
`window.Tlvt6` exists), `verify-pipeline.mjs` (checks below; report `out/3158346939/payload-pipeline-verify.json`,
lengths/booleans/offsets only). No network: the sandbox network APIs throw, and the sandbox does not reach `xo` on its
own. Instead the real `xo` is called directly on a bag decoded from each Chrome `Ojb` capture.

### Stage map (orchestrator readable = `out/3158346939/int-plain-1.orchestrator.readable.js`)

| # | stage | where (function / state / case) | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 | bag finalisation | `zL(jb,jf,jT,r4)` L10621, L10770 | VM-built bag `jf` gets `KHnWz9` (= `Date.now() - QDdy4`), `GesyF4`, `oYcq1`, then `rd = xo(jf)` and `XMLHttpRequest.send(rd)`. The second caller, `zW.qgcO0` L8455 → `send(zs(xo,jv))` L8500, is the small side POST (4248 B JSON in the captures). |
| S1 | JSON → bytes | `xo` L1856: state 6 `rd` `jN=[Number(D1<<D0)]` = `[0x20]`; state 10 `rZ` `zT(jf,jN,1)` (L9612); state 17 `rr` appends `0x20`; state 2 `r7` truncates | Custom JSON serializer: for-in key order; function/undefined/symbol/bigint members skipped (with rollback); non-finite → `null`; empty array slots → `null`; escapes `\b\t\n\f\r\"\\` (table `zX[31]`), other C0 → `\u00xx`, ≥0x80 → UTF-8, **lone surrogates → 3-byte WTF-8**. Result `S1 = ' ' + JSON + ' '`. |
| S2 | compress + frame | `xo` state 18 `rG` → `onb(658670010, S1)` (L10873, case 687785985) → `i3` (L343) → `AL` (L1730–1834) | If `len ≥ 128`: `z = AL(S1)`; kept only if `A6(z.length, S1.length)` (shorter). Output `[0xFD, 0x01, flag, …body]`. `AL` = raw DEFLATE, one block, BFINAL=1, BTYPE=01 (fixed Huffman), LZ77 with a 13-bit hash `((b0<<5)^(b1<<2)^b2)&8191` (head `Int32Array(8192)`, prev `Int32Array(32768)`), chain limit 2, min match 3, max 258, window 32768, greedy, no lazy matching, positions inside a match inserted into the hash. |
| S3 | pad | `xo` state 3 `r8` `jw=(8-len%8)%8`; state 0 `r5` `jf.length += jw` | Array holes; the XTEA loop reads them as 0. Result = **`Ojb`**. The pad also selects the key: state 14 `rA` `jw=t9(9,pad)+40`, state 16 `rj` `key=zJ.slice(jw,jw+16)`, state 12 `rD` `key=Tlvt6(key)`. |
| S4 | header + cipher | state 11 `rz` `jN=zX[13].slice()` (128-B RSA block, init-time), state 1 `r6` `jN[128]=pad`, state 15 `rl` `xV` subkeys; after the loop `ukb(jn ? 45189444 : 1061505599, Ojb, len, key, Rjb, 129)` (case 821625732), with `jn = len ≥ 16384` (state 8 `rc`) | XTEA. Then custom base64 with alphabet `zX[40]`. |

### Verification by execution (`node tools/deob-auto/pipeline/verify-pipeline.mjs`, exit 0)

| check | result |
|---|---|
| A. real `xo` in the Node sandbox, bag = decode(Chrome `Ojb`): S1 == Chrome `onb` input, `Ojb` == Chrome `Ojb` | **8/8 captures byte-exact** (clean, env_headless, env_runtime, env_webdriver, l_baseline, l_baseline2, l_probedone, l_remapdone; Ojb 64944–66160 B, pad 0–7, all flag=1, all `ukb` case 45189444) |
| B. port == real: `serialize`==`zT`+frame, `onbFrame`==`onb`, `encode`==`Ojb` | 8/8 captures; **400/400** random bags through the real `xo` (control chars, surrogates, NaN/±Inf, -0, holes, functions, undefined, nested); **600/600** random byte strings through the real `onb` (length 0–70002, 237 took the compressed branch) |
| C. `decodeOjb(Ojb)` → S1 == real S1, and `encode(decode(Ojb))` == `Ojb` | **8/8 round-trip byte-exact** |
| XTEA chaining | none (ECB-like). A 2-byte `Ojb` change at offset 19 changes exactly 8 `Rjb` bytes, at offset 145 = 129 + 16 (its block). The same bag twice gives an identical `Rjb` in the sandbox (seeded RNG). |

### Which stage spreads a one-field change (D, clean bag, one character of one string leaf, same length)

| leaf | S1 bytes changed | `Ojb` bytes changed (first offset, % of the tail after it) |
|---|---|---|
| depth 2, S1 off 16 | 1 | 2 (off 19, 0%) |
| depth 4, S1 off 11759 | 1 | **58714** (off 6048, 99.7%), length 64944→64952 |
| depth 4, S1 off 24250 | 1 | **53510** (off 10755, 98.7%), length →64952 |
| depth 2, S1 off 57299 | 1 | 2 (off 32012) |
| depth 1, S1 off 106649 | 1 | 2 (off 64830) |

**S1 (the zT byte buffer) is the last local-diff stage. S2 (`AL` DEFLATE) is the stage that spreads.** When the edit
only swaps one literal code for another of the same bit length, the change stays local (2 bytes). When it changes the
LZ77 parse (a match breaks or appears) or a code length, every later bit shifts, and about 99% of the rest of `Ojb`
changes. S3 (pad) and S4 (ECB-like XTEA) only carry that spread forward block by block. `decodeOjb` inverts back to S1
(and to the JSON value). `encode` maps a bag forward to the exact `Ojb`.

## H58 — Non-executed branches in stage1/stage2 (task 2)

**Tool:** `tools/deob-auto/coverage/branch-coverage.mjs` → `tools/deob-auto/out/3158346939/branch-coverage.json`.
This is static analysis plus the existing box-local Chrome trace dumps. No new runs were made and nothing was sent to CF.

**Method.**
1. Read every trace dump under the box-local Chrome trace root and keep those whose bytecode lengths match `int-plain-1`
   (`[5978, 483067]`): 303 used, 1 skipped (`headless-1a`, stage2 length 503198). For each pair of consecutive trace
   records in the same VM instance, record the edge `prevPc → pc` (instruction pc = `hpc − 1`).
2. Two-way sites come from the disasm JSON:
   - `branch` (op160 truthy, op161 seq, op186 lt, op95 le, op94 ge)
   - `guard` (op52, `fallthrough` vs `hash`)
   - `seq-realstate` (op69/186/95), with the alt that the disassembler rejected put back as a second successor.
3. A successor never seen as an observed edge counts as **untaken**. A site whose pc never appears is **never executed**.
4. Each untaken edge gets a reason class:
   - It first gets a backward slice of the predicate registers in the readable lift, up to 200 lines and within the same
     function. Structured `if` headers have no `@pc` comment, so the slice starts at the nearest preceding annotated pc.
   - The untaken target block is also checked.
   - The first rule that matches assigns the class:

| class | rule |
|---|---|
| `op52-hash-guard` | op52 guard; the untaken edge is `fallthrough` (all traces took the hash dispatch) |
| `constant-predicate` | the slice resolves fully and uses only literals, register copies and operators (dead arm / opaque constant) |
| `error-path` | the untaken target block contains `throw` (the note says when the guard is also an env probe) |
| `env-dependent` | the slice reads the global object (`globalThis`, `r70`), `navigator`, `typeof`, `in`, WebGL/RTC/gpu/Intl/Worker/… |
| `loop-bound` | lt/le/ge or `.length` in the slice |
| `value-compare` | op161 strict-equality on computed values |
| `value-dependent` | anything else (closure cell / argument values) |

Op194 try sites are reported on their own: a try is "entered, handler never run" when its pc executed but its
`handlerPc` never did.

**Summary.**

| stage | instrs | executed | two-way sites | executed | never executed | untaken edges | try entered | handler never run |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| stage1 | 780 | 617 | 29 | 22 | 7 | 20 | 7 | 7 |
| stage2 | 33174 | 26865 | 1525 (145 realstate) | 1279 | 246 | 964 | 351 | 322 |

| class | stage1 | stage2 |
|---|---:|---:|
| env-dependent | 16 | 362 |
| value-dependent | 0 | 263 |
| error-path | 0 | 193 |
| value-compare | 0 | 64 |
| op52-hash-guard | 2 | 49 |
| constant-predicate | 1 | 29 |
| loop-bound | 1 | 4 |

| op | stage1 | stage2 |
|---|---:|---:|
| op160 truthy | 16 | 740 |
| op161 seq | 2 | 114 |
| op69 realstate | 0 | 52 |
| op52 guard | 2 | 49 |
| op186 lt | 0 | 8 |
| op95 le | 0 | 1 |

**Realstate finding.** Of the 145 stage2 `seq-realstate` sites, 107 executed:
- 54 took only the disasm edge.
- 7 took **only the alt the disassembler rejected**.
- 46 took both edges.

So the "rejected" alt is a real successor, not a decoding artefact. The 52 op69 untaken edges in the table are rejected
alts never seen in any trace. Lifts that drop the rejected alt are incomplete for at least 53 sites (7 + 46). **[FIXED in §H59.2: lift now emits both edges.]**

**Sanity.** Most observed `seq`-kind edges agree with the disasm successors:
- stage1: 370333 of 371545 (99.67%).
- stage2: 38806962 of 39241858 (98.89%).

The rest are nested call entries and returns and exception unwinds, which land outside the static successor set.

**All 20 stage1 untaken edges.**

| pc | op | cond | untaken | class | predicate source (slice head) |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 502 | 160 | truthy(r205) | !p | env-dependent | `r70["window"][…]` |
| 702 | 160 | truthy(r204) | p | env-dependent | `r70["window"][…]` |
| 734 | 160 | truthy(r205) | p | env-dependent | `r70["window"][…].JKoe4` |

| 1253 | 160 | truthy(r205) | p | env-dependent | `!(… === cell[6])` |
| 1325 | 160 | truthy(r204) | p | env-dependent | `!(… === cell[5])` |
| 1551 | 160 | truthy(r199) | p | loop-bound | `r248.length == const` |
| 1634 | 52 | op52(r199) | fallthrough | op52-hash-guard | `typeof r227` |
| 2213 | 160 | truthy(r205) | p | env-dependent | `window.sSkll6.tNEww4(…)` |
