Stage1 branch sites that never executed: pc 225, 249, 289, 436, 630, 2059 (op160) and 1986 (op161). Stage1 try sites
that were entered but whose handler never ran: 7 of 7 (for example 691→790, 1186→1364, 1788→1925, 3035→3279).

**Stage2 samples (one per class).**

| pc | op | cond | untaken | class | predicate source |
|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1209 | 160 | truthy(r199) | !p | env-dependent | `window.crypto` |
| 3471 | 160 | truthy(r202) | !p | constant-predicate | `r202 = undefined` |
| 3589 | 160 | truthy(r205) | p | value-dependent | `cell[20]` |
| 5929 | 52 | op52(r205) | fallthrough | op52-hash-guard | `cell[0][cell[0][const]]` |
| 21445 | 160 | truthy(r192) | !p | error-path | result of a 4-arg method call; target throws |
| 28807 | 161 | seq(r205,r202) | p | value-compare | `null.alpha === undefined` shape |
| 77451 | 161 | seq(r205,r202) | !p | loop-bound | `cell[227](…).name.length` |

The full per-site list (pc, key, op, cond, untaken successor, taken successors with counts, class, reason, sliceHint
with string literals elided) is in `branch-coverage.json` under `stages.<stage>.untaken`. The list of never-executed
sites is under `neverExec`, and the try sites whose handler never ran are under `tryNot`.

**Caveats.**
- Classes are heuristics from a 4-line slice head.
  - `env-dependent` means only that the predicate reads the host environment; a different browser or profile may take
    the other edge.
  - `value-dependent` is a residual class (predicates from closure cells / arguments that the slice could not resolve).
- Coverage is the union of the 303 int-plain-1-compatible traces (mostly one Chrome profile). It is not a
  minimum across environments.

## H59 — Ojb field-level diff (task 1, blocked on data) and execution-resolved realstate branches (task 2)

### H59.1 Field-level S1 JSON diff — tool built, captures not available

**Status: blocked on input data, not on tooling.** The box-local `*.pretea.json` / `Ojb` captures that H55/H56
measured are no longer on the box (a full `find` over `/workspace` and `/tmp` returns none), and this task forbids new
Chrome runs. So no new field-level numbers are reported here. **Nothing below is a new measurement unless it says so.**

Tool: `tools/deob-auto/pipeline/ojb-field-diff.mjs` (commit `fec39ad`). It runs `decodeOjb` (H57 pipeline) on each
capture, giving S1 JSON. It then diffs the two parsed objects field by field and reports **paths and value classes only**:
- Value classes: `null`, `bool`, `int(Nd[,epoch-ms])`, `float`, `str(len,kind)`, `arr(len)`, `obj(keys)`.
- Change types: `value`, `added`, `removed`, `type`.
- Tags: `timing-leaf` (`roTuJ3`, `DJme8`, `helyQ4`, `SvnXY5`, `KHnWz9`), `numeric`, `string`, `structural`.
- Primitive arrays are compared as multisets, giving `removed` / `added` / `reorderedOnly`. An element moved from one
  array to another is one remove plus one add, not an index-shift cascade.
- `rolledUpLeaves` regroups changes at FNV-leaf granularity (first three path segments), for a direct comparison with
  the H34/H39 leaf-hash diffs.
- `timingOrNumericOnly` answers the "is it timing-only?" question for each pair.

`--matrix <dir>` runs the requested pairs over `<label>.json`:
- clean1–3
- clean vs webdriver, runtime, headless and ua_headless
- baseline vs baseline2, probedone, remapdone and copydone

`--selftest` passes 6/6 on synthetic bags: move, timing, reorder, string, added, and a no-values-in-report check. With
no captures it exits 2 ("no capture pairs found").
To fill this section, re-capture the matrix and run `node pipeline/ojb-field-diff.mjs --matrix <dir> --out <report>`.

**What the existing evidence already says (H56, JSON-text level, measured Oct 29):**

| pair | JSON-level (H56) | earlier FNV leaf diff | expected field-level result (inference) |
|---|---|---|---|
| clean×3 | 0 bytes differ | 0 | 0 changes |
| clean vs webdriver | 4313 bytes, one region; `n.webdriver` moves from the first "present" array to `T` | 7 leaves (H39.3) / 0 (H34) | 1 structural move (remove in one array + add in `T`); FNV's 7 = 2 arrays + their parents + `WdGLU4`/`YFZTc5`/`sJhNC9` |
| clean vs runtime | 10304 bytes, JSON 38 B shorter | 5 leaves (`37.*`) | changes confined to `37.*` |
| clean vs headless | 76819 bytes, JSON 2475 B longer | 31 / 2 (H34) | many structural + string changes |
| baseline vs probedone | stack-frame name + perf samples + `roTuJ3`/`DJme8` | — | 1 string change + timing / numeric changes |
| baseline vs remapdone | as probedone + `o.__deobKsI` enumerated + reorders | — | as probedone + 1 added array element + `reorderedOnly` |
| baseline vs copydone | not analysed in H56 | — | unknown |

**Why the counts disagree (explanation; the field-level confirmation is pending):**
- **Byte counts are not field counts.** Deleting one 14-character array element shifts every later byte in that region.
  So 4313 byte diffs can be one semantic change.
- **FNV counts parents.** The H39 leaf hash rolls a change up into each enclosing container hash, so one moved array
  element shows up as several "leaves".
- **Timing leaves were excluded or pinned differently.**
  - H34 ran under partial DET and H39 under full DET pins. H34's 0 vs H39's 7 for webdriver is therefore a harness
    difference, not a contradiction.
  - The field diff tags timing leaves instead of dropping them.
- **H34/H39 hashed the heap-shadow bag, not the serialized JSON.** Keys the serializer skips (`undefined`, H41.1) differ
  in the bag but never reach S1.

**Is probedone/remapdone timing-only?** From H56:
- probedone is **not strictly timing-only**. It also changes one error-stack frame string, which is an artefact of the
  lift wrapper sitting in the call stack. No computed challenge value changes.
- remapdone adds an **instrumentation leak** (`__deobKsI` enumerated as a property) and enumeration-order reshuffles.
- So both differences are caused by instrumentation (timing plus the harness's own footprint), not by the payload
  computation.

**Correction to H55.1.** The H55.1 finding attributed the probedone/remapdone/copydone length growth to "a self-timing /
op-count field … serialized with variable length". That hypothesis is **superseded by H56/H57**:
- `Ojb` is DEFLATE output, so any upstream JSON change moves the first diverging compressed byte and changes the
  compressed length.
- The upstream JSON changes are a stack-frame string and perf-timing samples, plus the `__deobKsI` leak for remapdone.
- There is no single op-count field.
- The H55.1 byte matrix itself stays valid.

### H59.2 Wrongly-rejected successors at realstate sites — fixed

**Problem (H58).** Stage2 had 145 `seq-realstate` sites where the disassembler kept one successor and rejected the
alternative as "value-dependent". Chrome traces showed:
- 46 sites took both edges;
- 7 took only the rejected edge;
- 54 took only the kept edge;
- 38 never executed.

H44.4 said the alternate edge is "not recoverable from this one trace". **That is wrong:** the edge can be recovered by
execution in the Node sandbox.

**Root causes.**
1. **op69 (87 sites)** works like a switch case: `d = K; if (read0 === K) goto T; else goto F`.
   - The generic branch prover had not verified a predicate for op69 (`branch.twoWay`, "no predicate fits").
   - Successors were only taken from the scenarios that happened to run.
   - **10 more op69 sites** with `K ∈ {2,3,4,5,128,255}` were plain `seq` with a single successor, because no scenario
     value ever equalled K. Their T-edge was hidden entirely.
2. **op186 (57 sites)** is `d = lt(rA,rB)` and was already op-verified.
   - These sites were marked realstate only because recorded Chrome-trace state took precedence over the branch
     check. A fresh run resolves them as `branch lt` with both edges.
3. **op95 @105181 (1 site)** remains `seq-realstate`: no single-register predicate fits, and its alternate pc lies
   outside the bytecode.

**Fix** (commit `c6059d1`):
- `lib/disasm.mjs`, `vdepBranch`: re-executes the site with candidate register values and keys. The candidates now
  include **the site's own immediates**. Single-successor sites of unverified two-way ops (`vdepOps`, which are op69 and
  op180 from semantics) are probed too.
- A site that resolves becomes kind `vbranch` with a predicate (`read0 === K`) and both successors.
- `disasm-run.mjs`:
  - With `DISASM_SEED_FROM`, it carries over 172 seed records that need Chrome-trace cell state (op236×162, op195×9,
    op245×1). A Node-only run cannot rebuild these.
  - The summary gains `valueBranches` and `carriedFromSeed`.
- `lib/vbranch.mjs` plus `lift.mjs`, `lift-readable.mjs` and `lift-exec.mjs` emit `if (P) goto L_T; else goto L_F;`.
  Example: `r199 = 1; if (r205 === 1) goto L3125_133; else goto L3133_206; // @3107 k55 op69`.
- `coverage/branch-coverage.mjs` counts `vbranch` as realstate with both successors.

**Before → after (stage2 int-plain-1):**

| metric | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| instructions | 33174 | **33561** (+387 reached via the 10 newly exposed T-edges) |
| seq / branch / vbranch / guard | 31479 / 1218 / 0 / 163 | 31840 / 1292 / **97** / 163 |
| seq-realstate | 145 | **1** (op95) |
| unresolved (summary) | 372 | 368 |
| kind `unresolved` | 169 | 168 |
| value-dependent rejected | 61 (H44.4) | **2** (103380 unresolved, 105181 op95) |
| previously-ok → bad | — | **0** |
| lift (no immediates) lifted / opaque | 32644 / 530 | **33088 / 473** |
| lift.js (op195 + op67 immediates) | 32868 / 306 | **33312 / 249** |
| readable: namedApi / structuredIf / functions / strings | 1349 / 620 / 453 / 6537 | 1354 / 630 / 455 / 6557 |
| equiv-subst-stage2 (Node, n=176) | match=true | **match=true** (liftHits 136, realHits 40, liftFail 6) |

vbranch predicates: `read0 === 0` ×45, `=== 1` ×42, `=== 2` ×3, `=== 3` ×2, `=== 4` ×2, and `=== 5`, `=== 128`,
`=== 255` once each. In every case K equals the site immediate. All 87 former op69 realstate sites resolve with an edge
set equal to {kept, rejected}. The 57 op186 sites resolve to `branch lt` with the same edge set.

**Cross-check with the H58 Chrome traces.**
- The 46 sites that took both edges and the 7 that took only the rejected edge lie within the resolved {T, F} pairs.
- No traced edge falls outside them.
- The lift now includes both edges at all 53 sites that H58 showed were incomplete.

**Limits.**
- The branch-coverage map (H58) cannot be recomputed. The 303 traces it unions are no longer on the box, and new Chrome
  runs are out of scope. The table above uses disasm and lift statistics only.
- The equiv-subst prefix check (176 dispatches) is a regression check. It does not exercise each new edge.
- The stage1 and int-headless-1 listings were not re-run. Stage1 has no op69/op180/realstate sites.
- The 172 carried records still depend on a seed listing. A fresh Node-only disasm without `DISASM_SEED_FROM` would
  mark them bad.

_Disclosure: pcs, keys, op numbers, register numbers, counts and value classes only. No payload values, rays, `<vm-endpoint>` paths
or `<chl-opt>` are committed._

## H60 — Offline replay restored after reset; Ojb field-level matrix (hook group) measured

### H60.1 Root cause of the post-reset "re-challenge / n=0" replay failure

After the box reset, `run-plain.mjs` replays of `int-plain-1` never reached the `<vm-endpoint>` POST. The VM ran ~10 s, requested an
`av0/rch<token>` re-challenge (absent from the capture), and looped (watchdog reload storm, `n=0`).

- **Cause:** DET pinned `Date` to `floor(orch file mtime)`. Restoring the capture directory bumped that mtime ~20 h past
  the capture time, so the VM saw its captured challenge token as expired and re-challenged.
- **Ruled out first:** headless (clean is headful), seg-swap (2 swaps applied), route coverage, `Runtime.enable` (not sent
  for clean), launcher flags (identical to stealth-cdp's), virtual time. Virtual time makes the storm worse (H33 blocker
  confirmed again). `Emulation.setVirtualTimePolicy` is also not available on the browser-level session.
- **Fix (`6dd31f1`):** the default epoch now comes from the capture `index.json` orchestrator entry `t` (immutable),
  floored to the second. Mtime is only a fallback; `RUN_DET_EPOCH=<ms>|now` still overrides.
- **Verified (clean, no override, 2 runs):** halt at fo-post #2, `n=181608`, bcLens `[5978,483067]`, halt body
  86764 B, orchestrator served once. This matches H22.
- Launch: `run2.sh` uses a non-root user+net namespace, so Chrome keeps its own sandbox (`a55f3f8`).
- Matrix driver: `tools/chrome-run/ojb-matrix.sh`. Its outputs are box-local only.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| clean1 vs clean2 | 4 | 0 | 107082→107082 | yes | timing leaves `34.DJme8`, `34.helyQ4`, `35.roTuJ3`, `35.helyQ4` |
| clean1 vs clean3 | 4 | 0 | 107082→107082 | yes | same 4 timing leaves |

## H65 — q1 vs r resolved; op99, op245 and op52 examined, nothing new lifted

Scope as H64. Lifted share is unchanged at 80.34% (table `subst-tab-r`, H64).

### H65.1 The 71 entries in `subst-tab-r` that `subst-tab-q1` lacks

No commit or code path drops them. `q` (15:05, the H64 batch 3 code) and `r` (rebuilt from 4ce1965) are key- and body-identical. `q0` is `q` without the 1355 op236 cell reads. `q1` is `q` minus 71 call-guarded entries, removed by hand during the H64 session while bisecting the `37.oPxfw0` drift. The removal did not remove the drift: `ojb17` (q1) still shows it on one of three runs. That matches H61/H63.4, where the drift is run-to-run nondeterminism.

| op | guard callee (count) |
|---|---|
| 124 | `pop` (10) |
| 138 | WebGL/WebGPU calls (54: `deleteShader` 8, `compileShader` 3, `attachShader` 4, `deleteProgram` 4, `getExtension` 4, `getParameter` 3, `deleteBuffer` 3, ...), `substring` 3, `split` 2, `resolve` 2, `slice` 1, `String` 1 |

Per-entry check against the `ojb19` shadowcmp trace: none of the 71 keys is dispatched in the replay, lifted or native, so none can be verified by execution here and none affects any measured count. They stay in the table because they are gated like every other call entry. If a capture ever reaches the WebGL paths, the state-changing GPU calls and `pop` are the first candidates for `CALL_DENY`, because shadowcmp runs a lifted body a second time on the restored pre-state.

### H65.2 op99 (2953 dispatches): hypothesis falsified

The listing renders `rD = cell[N] + "K"`. The trace shows that op99 reads physical register 63 (an array of 228 entries, the cell table) and writes an integer. Hypothesis: disasm runs the op on a symbolic stub cell, so the stub's string form is concatenated with a numeric immediate, and the handler computes `cell[N].B + K`. I re-rendered the sites as `cell[N] + K` with a primitive cell guard. 23 sites passed the numeric gate, but shadowcmp missed on all 2953 op99 dispatches (match 144244, miss 2953). Real substitution then timed out with no Ojb. The patch is not committed. So op99 is not `B + K`: the integer comes from something else, such as the cell index, a length, or a stored counter. The next step is to read the handler source and run it on the real cell table, offline, in `build-subst-table`'s stepper.

### H65.3 op245 (3446) and op52 (7368): need a dynamic-successor entry kind

- op245 is the VM return: `r(o^125) = rX; r(o^239).length = r(o^29).pop(); pc = (popped frame)`. It is verified by execution in `semantics` (14 sites). The table format has a static next, or a predicate with two static targets. A return needs the successor read from the popped frame, so it needs a new runtime entry kind (`[body, 'dyn', exprPc, exprKey]`) and a shadowcmp check of the pc/key pair.
- op52 is a hash-gated re-key and jump (`probe-op52.mjs`): a no-op unless the string hash and four decoded words match the immediates. Only the no-op path is verified. Lifting it needs the same dynamic entry kind with the no-op path as a guarded fallthrough, plus an execution check of the taken path, for which no input string is known offline.

Next: the dynamic-successor entry kind in the runtime and table (unlocks op245, then op52's no-op path), the op99 handler read, then op194 call sites.

## H66 op99 lifted from its executed semantics; op52 no-op path under a string guard; op245 is a NaN-pc halt

Result: shadowcmp match 147211 of n 179554 = **81.99 %** lifted (H65: 80.34 %), miss 0, err 0. Subst ×2: hits 150240, fail 0, field diff timing-only (27 leaves each). In the op99-only run (`subst-tab-t`), one subst run also showed the known `37.oPxfw0` inspector drift (94 → 90 elements, H64).

| op | handler | change | dispatches lifted |
|---|---|---|---|
| 99 | `X1` | `cell[N].B = cell[N].B + K; dst = new B` (23 stage-2 sites) | +2907 |
| 52 | `yeb 1317890571` | empty body with the fallthrough successor, guard `['n', reg]` (operand is not a string) | +11 |
| 245 | `Bdb 1456107531` | not lifted (see H66.3) | 0 |

### H66.1 op99: the true semantics, from execution

`probe-op99.mjs` runs the real `X1` handler, in the offline stepper, at all 25 stage-2 sites. Cells are created at run time and the captured table lacks them, so the probe plants a cell `{B: 1000}` at the listing's index. On 23 sites the handler adds a **number** `K` to `B` (21 × `K = 1`, 2 × `K = 16`), stores the sum back into the cell, and writes it to the destination register. The other 2 sites have no cell index in the listing and throw.

The listing renders `K` as a string literal (`cell[98] + "1"`). It is a number. The H65 attempt failed for two reasons:
- The body had no cell store. Subst never advanced the counter and looped until the timeout.
- shadowcmp's `fullRestore` covers registers but not cell objects. The oracle's increment stayed in place, and the lifted body then incremented a second time (2953 misses).

### H66.2 Changes to the table and the runtime

- **New statement `CW(n, EXPR)`:** a cell store. `runSrc` applies it. `runSrcFx` records it as an effect with the cell object (`regs.cellObj`).
- **shadowcmp cell handling:** for an entry with `CW`, shadowcmp saves each stored cell's `B` before the oracle and takes the oracle's post value. It then restores the pre value, runs the lifted body, compares the lifted store against the oracle's post value (`cell:n` and `cellMiss` diffs), and continues on the oracle's cell state.
- **op99 entry:** `W(dst, (C(n) + K)); CW(n, R(dst))`, static fallthrough, guard `['k', n, 0]`.
- **New guard `['n', reg]`:** passes when `typeof R(reg) !== 'string'`.
- **op52 entries:** the builder executes the handler with 10 non-string operands (numbers, booleans, null, undefined, objects, arrays). It requires no throw, no writes, and the fallthrough successor for every operand. All 195 stage-2 sites pass.
- **op52 coverage:** at run time the operand is almost always a string. The guard count rose 1726 → 9069 and only 11 dispatches moved to lifted. The hash path is the hot one.

### H66.3 op245 does not need a frame-read successor

The `Bdb 1456107531` case (`Cdb ^ 263997883 = 1500920240`) does the following:
- writes `r(o^125)`;
- truncates the frame register with the popped length;
- ends with `jN(state, 0/0)`, which sets **pc = NaN** so the run loop stops. It is a halt, not a jump to a popped frame.

So its successor is static, and the "dynamic successor from the popped frame" premise of H65.3 is wrong. Two things are missing to lift it:
- The table needs a NaN encoding, because JSON turns NaN into `null`, and the runtime currently writes `nx[0]` into the pc register as is.
- The key behaviour on the halt needs checking with the stepper.

The dynamic-successor entry kind is still needed for op52's hash path. That path needs `h(rX)` computed at run time and compared with the immediate, with the re-key and jump target taken from four decoded words.

Next:
- op245: add the NaN pc encoding, then a `W` and `pop` store body with a `pop` call guard. Verify with shadowcmp, which already compares the pc and key registers.
- op52 hash path: a dynamic entry `[body, 'dyn', pcExpr, keyExpr]` with the hash in the expression language.
- op194 call sites (4154), op138 reflection (3435).

## H67 op52 hash guard lifted; op245 return refuted as a register-only lift

**Result:** lifted share 81.99% → **85.96%** (shadowcmp match 154346 / n 179554, miss 0, err 0, guard 9069 → 1934). Subst: hits 157375, fail 0, field diff baseline vs subst 27 leaves, timing/numeric only. Verified table: `subst-tab-w` (run ojb24).

| op | entry | stage-2 sites | dispatches lifted (ojb24) | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 52 | `VH(R(reg))`, static fallthrough, guard `['h', reg, IMM]` | 195 | 7195 of 7403 | **lifted**, shadowcmp 100% |
| 245 | `W(dst, R(src)); R(fr).length = R(st).pop()`, pc `'NaN'`, key unchanged | 680 | 0 of 3498 | **refuted** as built, opt-in only (`SUBST_OP245=1`) |

### H67.1 op245: a nested run() return, but not a register-local one

- **Source:** `run(pc, key, ...)` loops until `mm(...)` is true, then returns `r(o^125)`. `mm` is true when pc is NaN. op245 sets pc = `0/0`, so it ends the current `run()` call. That is a VM function return, not a halt of the whole program. This fits the 3446 to 3498 executions per run.
- **Stepper probe (`probe-op245.mjs`, bf9ecca):** all 680 sites give pc NaN, key unchanged, `dst = src`, frame truncated to the popped length, and no other register writes.
- **Live shadowcmp (table v, ojb23):** **2069 misses, all op245.** The native oracle changes many registers that the listing statement does not touch: low registers (3, 8, 14 …) and high ones (203, 241–249, 255). The lifted body leaves them at their pre-state values. With op245 lifted, subst stalls after 282 hits (the first op245 is at step about 263).
- **Reading:** the return also restores the caller's register state. The probe could not see this because the stepper plants an isolated register file. So "body = listing statement + NaN pc" is not the whole effect. The builder keeps the op245 block (with its execution gate) behind `SUBST_OP245`, and the NaN encoding stays in the runtime (`nxt`: `'NaN'` gives pc NaN and leaves the key alone).

### H67.2 op52: hash guard and cache body

- **Operand decode:** byte `i` is `hkey ^ ((bc[hpc+i] - 235) & 255)`. Byte 0 gives register 18 at every site. Bytes 1–4 are the big-endian 32-bit IMM.
- **Hash:** `h52(s)`: start `t = 519889518`, then per char `t ^= c; t = 403*t + (t<<24)`, result `>>> 0`.
- **Guard `['h', reg, IMM]`:** lift unless the operand is a string whose hash equals IMM. The runtime reuses `vm.P` when `vm.R` is the same string.
- **Body `VH(x)`:** for a string `x` that differs from `vm.R`, set `vm.R = x; vm.P = h52(x)`. This is the handler's cache update. A non-string is a no-op.
- **Builder gate:** per site, execute the handler with 4 strings (skipping any whose hash equals IMM). Require no throw, the fallthrough successor, no register writes, and `vm.R`/`vm.P` equal to the string and its hash. All 195 sites pass.
- **shadowcmp:** saves `vm.R`/`vm.P` around the oracle and compares them with the lifted run (`vmRP` diff). There were 0 vmRP misses.
- **Still native:** 208 dispatches, which is the hash-match path. That path takes 8 words from `GE(str)`, compares 4 bytecode words, and on success calls `rv(m, w7)`, re-keys and jumps. A true dynamic successor needs GE reimplemented.

### Next

1. op245: capture the oracle's full register delta at a few sites (numbers and types only), then read the caller-frame restore in the handler or run-loop exit. Likely the outer `run()` reinstates a saved register window. Lift it as a body plus a frame-restore statement.
2. op194 call sites (4154) and op138 reflection (3435).
3. op52 match path (208): reimplement `GE` to get a dynamic successor.

## H68 op245 halt path and op194 try-push lifted

**Result:** lifted share 85.96% → **89.10%**. shadowcmp: match 159990 / n 179554, miss 0, err 0, guard 3953. Subst: hits 162935, fail 0. Verified table: `subst-tab-y` (run ojb26).

**Field diff, baseline vs subst:** 27 timing leaves plus one array, `37.oPxfw0`, which is 94 → 92 elements (2 removed).
- `37.oPxfw0` is console and stack material (H41).
- Two baseline runs (ojb22 vs ojb25, ojb24 vs ojb26) agree on it. ojb24 (op52 only) had no array diff, ojb25 (op245 halt lifted) had 94 → 90, and ojb26 had 94 → 92.
- **[SUPERSEDED by §H69: the drift is the H43 CDP-preview `ownKeys` pair, not lift-caused.]** So the drift tracks the lifted op245/op194 code and changes the captured JS stack shape. It is not VM-semantic: shadowcmp is 100%, and subst vs subst2 are identical. It is a fingerprint-visible difference and must be closed before subst counts as field-identical.

| op | entry | stage-2 sites | dispatches lifted (ojb26) | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 245 | `W(dst, R(src)); R(fr).length = R(st).pop()`, pc `'NaN'`, guard `['r', st]` | 680 | 1482 of 3501 | halt path lifted; X6 in-VM return native (2019) |
| 194 | `TP(R(ts), hp, hk, R(ds))`, fallthrough, guard `['t', ts, ds]` | 373 | about 4168 | lifted (probe 373/373) |

### H68.1 The caller-register restore is X6, inside the op245 handler

The op245 handler (`case 1500920240`) runs five steps:
1. `r(o^125) = rX`;
2. `r48.length = r194.pop()`;
3. `pc = NaN`;
4. and then step `scb`: `top = vm.G[vm.G.length-1]; if (top && top[0] === vm.K) X6(vm, retval, false)`.

`X6` is the in-VM return for calls made without a nested `run()`. It pops the call frame `F = G.pop()` and then:
- restores the saved registers: `m.restore(F[5][i], F[6][i])` for every i;
- sets pc = `F[1]` and key = `F[2]`;
- sets the cell table `m.write(vm.A, F[3])`;
- restores the try stack with `ME(r194, F[8])`;
- writes the return value to `F[4]`.

This explains the H67.1 misses. `vm.K` is the `run()` nesting depth: `run()` increments it, and its `finally` decrements it and truncates `G`.

- **Guard `['r', st]`:** a native pop, and the top of `vm.G` is not a frame of the current depth. Under it, op245 is the pure `run()` exit, and the body plus NaN pc is exact.
- **shadowcmp fix:** the Fx-mode `R(n).length = v` store now covers the oracle's truncated slots (index ≥ v, same final length). Without it there were 57 `heapMiss` false positives.

### H68.2 op194 is a try-handler push, not a call

The listing is `rT.push([hpc, hkey, cell, rD.length])` at all 373 stage-2 sites. There is no callee, so the "deny-list callees" plan does not apply to op194. The earlier "op194 call sites" label in §H66/§H67 was wrong; the method-call op is op138.

- **Probe (`probe-op194.mjs`):** 373/373 sites push a fresh 4-element frame `[hpc, hkey, cellTable (= m.read(vm.A)), rD.length]`, with the fallthrough successor and no register writes.
- **Body `TP(...)`:** does the native push.
- **Guard `['t', ts, ds]`:** both operands are plain Arrays and `push` is `Array.prototype.push`.
- **shadowcmp:** the pushed frame is compared element-wise with the oracle's (`shallowEq`).

### Next

1. Close the `37.oPxfw0` stack-shape drift. First bisect: op245-only vs op194-only tables.
2. op245 in-VM return (2019): lift `X6` as a dynamic-successor body (pc/key from the popped frame). This needs `ME` and `m.restore` reachable from the runtime.
3. op138 method calls (6645 native): the callee deny-list keyed on the callee identity at run time (the plan meant for "op194").
4. op216 (1841), op22 (1194); op52 match path (208, needs `GE`).

## H69 `37.oPxfw0` bisected: the H43 CDP-preview pair, not the lift

**Result:** lifted share unchanged at **89.10%** (table `subst-tab-y`, nothing new lifted). The `37.oPxfw0` drift reported in §H68 is the known H43 harness artifact. It is not caused by op245, op194 or the stack shape, and no lift heuristic is needed.

### H69.1 Bisect

| run | table | baseline vs subst field diff | shadowcmp |
|---|---|---|---|
| ojb27 | `z245` (op52 + op245 halt, no op194) | 27 timing leaves, arrays 0 (timing-only) | match 155874, miss 0, err 0; subst hits 158786, fail 0 |
| ojb28 | `z194` (op52 + op194, no op245) | 29 leaves + `37.oPxfw0` 94 → 92 | match 158562, miss 0, err 0; subst hits 161571, fail 0 |

Taken alone this points at op194. But ojb27's own `subst2` run, with the same op245-only table, is 92. A per-run tally of the array length over every saved run (ojb6–ojb29) shows:

- **The same table gives different lengths:** ojb21 subst 90 / subst2 94; ojb24 (op52 only, called clean in §H68) subst 94 / subst2 92; ojb25 90 / 94; ojb15 92 / 94; ojb19 92 / 94.
- **Baseline also moves:** ojb13 baseline 90; ojb29 baseline 90.
- **The drift predates op245/op194:** ojb6, ojb8, ojb9 and ojb10 (early tables) were 92 / 90 / 90 / 90.

The length is always 90, 92 or 94. The varying elements are 0, 1 or 2 copies of the pair `yqDoj2`, `AWjER5` at index 12. That is exactly the §H43.2 Proxy `ownKeys` trap, which fires when the host (CDP inspector object preview) serializes the console arg. Subst runs land below 94 more often than baseline runs, so the lift's different timing probably shifts how often the preview fires. Still, no op group causes it: op52-only, op245-only and op194-only tables all produce it.

### H69.2 Stack-shape hypothesis: refuted

`RUN_STACKDIAG=1` (`page.mjs`) installs an `Error.prepareStackTrace` that records a histogram of frame count plus function names for every stack materialization, and keeps the default string format. `run-plain.mjs` writes it to `<out>.stk.json`. Run ojb29 (`z194`, baseline vs subst):

| | 7-frame stacks | 8-frame stacks |
|---|---|---|
| baseline | 1 | 184 |
| subst | 1 | 184 |

The histograms are identical, frame names included. No lift-runtime frame shows up in any captured stack, because lifted bodies run between dispatcher steps and not on the probe's call path. Both sides stay under `Error.stackTraceLimit` = 10. ojb29 itself had no array diff (90 / 90).

### H69.3 Verification under the H43 pin

`RUN_DET_OPX=1` (the H43.4 zero-touch console-arg pin, now forwarded by `ojb-matrix.sh`), table `subst-tab-y`, run ojb30:

- `37.oPxfw0` length: baseline 16, subst 16, subst2 14.
- baseline vs subst: 28 leaves, arrays 0, **timing-only**.
- baseline vs subst2: one array diff, the ±2 iframe-console residual that H43.4 already documented.

**Verification rule from now on:** compare `37.oPxfw0` only after checking that the pair count (`yqDoj2` occurrences) matches within the A/B pair. Otherwise exclude it as H43 noise, as §H42.4 already prescribed ("filter A/B on matching oPx class"). The §H68 table y verdict is therefore **timing-only**.

**Filter (committed):** `ojb-field-diff.mjs` now marks a `37.oPxfw0` array diff `h43Noise` when the two arrays are equal after removing every `yqDoj2` element and the element after it. `h43Noise` arrays do not fail `timingOrNumericOnly`; the CLI summary prints `h43Noise=N`. Selftest adds `h43-pair` and `h43-real`. Re-scored: ojb26 (table y, the §H68 verdict), ojb28, ojb25, ojb30 baseline/subst2 and ojb21 subst/subst2 are all `timingOrNumericOnly=true` with `h43Noise=1`. In every saved case the whole array diff is the pair.

### H69.4 op245 X6 (not started)

`X6(vm, retval, noWrite)` uses closure-internal helpers: `Iob`, `jN`, `jO`, `pr`, `ME`, `rt`. The body is: pop `F = G.pop()`; for each saved register `m.restore(F[5][i], F[6][i])` in reverse; set pc/key from `F[1]`/`F[2]`; `m.write(vm.A, F[3])`; `pr(ME, rt(read(o^29)), F[8])`; then, unless `noWrite`, `m.write(F[4], retval)`. To lift it, the runtime needs those helpers exported through the dispatcher hook (or called back into the native `X6`), plus a dynamic-successor entry type whose pc/key come from the popped frame.

### Next

1. op245 X6 in-VM return (2019 native): expose `X6` (or `jN`/`jO`/`pr`/`ME`/`rt`) via the install hook and emit `X6(...)` as a dynamic-successor body.
2. op138 method calls (6645 native): a callee deny-list keyed on the callee identity at run time.
3. op216 (1841), op22 (1194); op52 match path (208, needs `GE`).
4. Harness: make the H43 pin cover the iframe console realm so `37.oPxfw0` becomes deterministic.


## H70 Deterministic `37.oPxfw0`, op245 X6 lifted (90.21%)

### H70.1 Root cause of the `37.oPxfw0` drift: our recorder, not the inspector

The diagnostic `RUN_OWNKEYSDIAG=1` (page.mjs, run-plain.mjs, forwarded by ojb-matrix.sh) wraps `Proxy` and records the
call stack of every ownKeys trap call as function names only. Run ojb31 (2 baselines, `RUN_DET_OPX=1`): the trap fired
exactly once per run, always from `pollBuildKeys <- propagateVmShadow` in `recorder-src.js`. The H39 heap-shadow polls
the register file on every 8th step (`(_step&7)===0`) and calls `Object.keys` on each object register. When the VM's
devtools-detector Proxy (H43.2) is in a register at a sampled step, its ownKeys trap fires and the VM appends the
`yqDoj2` pair. The pair count therefore depends on the step phase, which lifting and timing change.

**Correction to H43.3 and H69:** the "CDP inspector preview" explanation was wrong for the residual. Extending the
console pin to the iframe console was not needed, because the pin already covers it (the H43.4 residual was the recorder).

**Fix:** page.mjs strips the register poll from the recorder by default (marked `/*H70:REGPOLL*/`). `RUN_BUILDPOLL=1`
restores it for build-log attribution work; its runs then carry the harness pair again. `RUN_DET_OPX` is now on by
default (`RUN_DET_OPX=0` opts out). The `h43Noise` filter in `ojb-field-diff.mjs` is now **fallback only**: it should
always count 0, and the CLI prints a warning when it triggers.

### H70.2 Verification (3 baselines + 3 subst)

| run | table | `oPxfw0` length | h43Noise |
|---|---|---|---|
| ojb33 baseline, baseline2, baseline3, subst, subst2, subst3 | y | 14 ×6 | 0 |
| ojb34 baseline, baseline2, baseline3, subst, subst2, subst3 | x6 | 14 ×6 | 0 |

`oPxfw0` is now identical in every run.

The register poll also slowed baselines enough to hide a second race. `31.BnDgz6` holds 11 or 12 entries; the extra one
is a one-key entry at index 4, and its presence depends on speed:

- ojb34 baselines gave 11 / 12 / 12 with no lift involved.
- `RUN_BUILDPOLL=1` (slower) gave 12.
- Every subst run gave 12.

This is a timing race in the input, not a lift effect. Field diffs for ojb34, using `baseline2` (12 entries) as reference:

- baseline3: 13 leaves, timing-only.
- subst: 20 leaves, timing-only.
- subst2: **0 changes**.
- subst3: **0 changes**.

Against `baseline` (11 entries), every run differs only in `31.BnDgz6` (the entry shift) plus timing leaves.
`ojb-field-diff` does not filter this race. Compare runs that have the same `BnDgz6` length.

### H70.3 op245 X6 via the install hook

- `instrument()` (vm-harness.mjs) finds X6 structurally: a 3-param function declaration that reads `G`, loops
  `["restore"](`, and writes `A`. Exactly one candidate (`X6`) is found. When X6 is in scope at a dispatcher, it is
  passed as a 5th argument: `__deobBP(probe, state, op, id, X6)`. Both dispatchers get it. The recorder ignores the
  extra argument.
- New table entry type `[src, 'RET', retReg, null, ['R', ts]]` (build-subst-table.mjs; `SUBST_OP245_HALT_ONLY=1`
  builds the H68 entry instead). The runtime reads `R(retReg)` before the body, runs the body, and writes pc NaN.
  When the top frame of `vm.G` has `frame[0] === vm.K`, it then calls the VM's own `X6(vm, ret, false)`, which pops
  G, restores the caller registers, sets pc/key/A, trims the try stack and writes the result.
- Guard `'R'`:
  - the 'c' pop guard on `ts`;
  - `vm.G` is a plain Array with the native pop;
  - on the X6 path, X6 must have arrived from the hook.
- shadowcmp:
  - snapshots and restores `vm.G` around the oracle and lifted runs, and diffs it (`G`);
  - deep-snapshots the try stack register (`o^29`), diffs it (`tryStack`), and includes it in the oHeap coverage check.

| op245 | 3470 lifted, 0 native (H69: 1482 / 2019) |

op16 658, op168 633. op52 match path: 208.

## H71 BnDgz6 pin, rebuilt table, op138 `.call` guard

### H71.1 `31.BnDgz6` race: long-animation-frame entries

`31.BnDgz6` serializes the timeline list (`performance.getEntries*`, sorted by the det layer on entryType|name).
`RUN_ENTDIAG=1` (new; writes `<out>.ent.json`, entryType histograms of every list the page reads) showed that the
`cpJm7`-shaped entries are `long-animation-frame` (LoAF) entries. Their count grows with wall time (1, 5, 7, 8 over
one run; 4 or 5 at the serialized call), so the length was 11 or 12 depending on how fast the run was. It was not a
resource-timing or worker-ordering race.

Pin (determinism-src `sortEnt`, option `loafPin`, on by default; `RUN_DET_LOAF=0` turns it off): the det layer drops
`long-animation-frame` entries from `Performance.prototype` and `PerformanceObserverEntryList.prototype`
`getEntries` / `getEntriesByType` / `getEntriesByName` results.

Verification (ojb36, ojb37): all 3 baselines in each run give the same `31.BnDgz6`, with 7 entries: mark, navigation,
2 paint, 2 resource, visibility-state. Baseline-vs-baseline field diffs are timing-only (ojb36: 17 and 13 leaves;
ojb37: 4 and 4 leaves). `37.oPxfw0` is 14/14 in all runs.

### H71.2 Rebuilt table (generator)

`build-subst-table.mjs` output (`subst-tab-h71`) is identical to the scratch-converted table x6: 31683 keys, 0
differences, 703 RET entries. ojb37 (pinned):

| run | result |
|---|---|
| shadowcmp | match 161313, miss 0, err 0, guard 1947; 161370 / 178911 lifted = 90.20% |
| subst x3 | hits 164218, fail 0; `31.BnDgz6` same as the baselines |
| subst, subst2 vs baseline | timing-only (17, 17 leaves) |
| subst3 vs baseline | 23 changes: 17 timing, `21.HkyLT5` numeric, plus added keys `WdGLU4`, `YFZTc5`, `sJhNC9`, `NglZ3.4`, `oYcq1.1` |

The subst3 extras are the Worker/late-bag keys from §H41 (`WdGLU4` = `new Worker(script)` result). They appear when
the worker reply arrives before serialization. That is a second, rarer race, separate from LoAF. It shows up in
1 of 3 subst runs here and in 0 of 6 baselines so far. It does not come from the lift: the same set appears with
table h71 (no new entries) and with h71c, and shadowcmp shows 0 misses.

### H71.3 op138 run-time callee deny-list

op138 is `rD = rO[NAME](rA...)`. Joining native op138 trace rows (ojb34) to the listing gave these reasons:
reflection 3548 (`rF.call(rT, a...)`), call 1676 (statically denied names: `now`, `toString`, `toLocaleString`, ...),
in-table but failing the run-time guard 988 (mostly `push` with an object argument), unverified 417 (`op138()`, the
name was not decoded), nowrite 4.

Changes:

- Builder: `rD = rF.call(rT, a...)` (`CALLR_RE`) is no longer 'reflection'. It is emitted under guard
  `['C', fReg, thisReg, [argRegs]]`. `SUBST_NO_CALLR=1` restores the old behaviour. Table h71c: +23 (s1) and +851 (s2)
  'C' entries, nothing else changed.
- Runtime guard 'C' (callee deny-list at run time): rF is a function whose source is `[native code]`, not
  `bound `/`Error`, and whose name is not in `CALLEE_DENY` (time/entropy, scheduling, events, messaging, reflection,
  property definition, Proxy-trap names, timeline). It is not `Function.prototype.toString/call/apply/bind` or
  `Function`, and its `.call` is a data property equal to the native `Function.prototype.call`. `this` is a primitive
  or a plain Object/Array/null-prototype object with no own `Symbol.toStringTag`. All arguments are primitives. A VM
  closure fails the native check, so it stays native (H61 `Error().stack`).
- Guard 'c': `Array.prototype.push` on a plain Array may take object arguments.

ojb38 (table h71c, pinned; diffs against the ojb37 baseline):

| run | result |
|---|---|
| shadowcmp | match 161748, miss 0, err 0, guard 5293 |
| lifted share | 161856 / 178971 = **90.44%** (H70: 90.21%) |
| op138 native | 6200 (was 6633); 2882 lifted |
| subst x3 | hits 164721, fail 0 |
| subst2, subst3 vs baseline | timing-only (13, 17 leaves) |
| subst vs baseline | the same Worker extras as in H71.2 (plus timing and `21.HkyLT5`) |

The gain is small: 4058 op138 dispatches in the table still fail the guard at run time. The hot ones are 184-count
`rF.call(rT, x)` sites (76562, 76901, 77178, 77195, 77213, 77231, 78687), which look like a getter/descriptor
fingerprint loop with a host-object `this`, and `push` sites 249066, 22431, 192667, 76603. The failing clause has not
been measured.

### Next

1. Add per-clause guard-failure counters for 'C'/'c' (callee not native, deny name, `this` not plain, object
   argument, push receiver not a plain Array). Then decide whether native getters with a host-object `this` (no Proxy,
   checked via the det layer's wrapper registry) can be allowed.
2. Pin the Worker arrival race (`WdGLU4`/`YFZTc5`/`sJhNC9`): make the det layer always deliver the worker reply
   either before or after serialization.

## H72 The "Worker race" was a harness retry: sticky, held halt

### H72.1 Root cause

The `WdGLU4`/`YFZTc5`/`sJhNC9` extras (H71.2/H71.3) are not a Worker timing race. I decoded the captured plaintext
of an extras run (ojb37 subst3) and compared it with a clean run. The extras payload also has one more `NglZ3` entry
(a resource-timing record for a second `<vm-endpoint>` request, with zero response timings) and one more `oYcq1` entry (the
XHR log, counter 2). So the dump captured a **later** payload, sent after the first payload POST had failed.

The harness caused this. On the 2nd `<vm-endpoint>` POST it called `Fetch.failRequest`, so the VM saw a network error and
re-sent the payload. If that 3rd POST arrived before the dump finished, `log.halt` was overwritten (`fo-post #3`) and
the capture held the retry payload. The retry payload is built on the error path, which does not run the
Worker/blob cleanup, so `WdGLU4`/`YFZTc5` are still set when it is serialized.

I checked this against all stored runs (ojb30–ojb40, 64 runs, `chk-extra.mjs` in scratch):

| last halt | payload | runs |
|---|---|---|
| `fo-post #2` | clean | 42 |
| `fo-post #3` | clean (dump won the race) | 14 |
| `fo-post #3` | extras | 5 |
| `fo-post #4` | extras | 3 (all shadowcmp) |

Every one of the 8 extras runs had a retry, and no `#2` run had extras. The race depended on how fast the dump ran
after the halt, so slower instrumented modes (subst, shadowcmp) hit it more often. ENTDIAG in ojb39/ojb40 slowed the
VM as well, so its 0/22 result was not a reliable reproduction test; ojb40 did reproduce 3/10 by the plaintext check.

### H72.2 Fix (`run-plain.mjs`)

- The halt is now sticky: the first payload POST sets `log.halt`, and later ones only increment `log.haltExtra`.
- The payload POST is **held** (left paused), not failed, so the VM never sees an error and never retries. This
  matches the clean baseline (serialize once, send once). `RUN_HALT_FAIL=1` restores the old fail behaviour.
- `RUN_ENTDIAG` also logs an event order (createObjectURL, Worker post/onmessage, XHR send) plus window

## H73 Guard relaxations: Math/JSON `this`, converters, plain `RegExp.test`, recorder `push` (91.28%)

All runs below use the held halt (H72) and table `subst-tab-h71c` (the table is unchanged; only the run-time guards
changed). Subst runs are diffed against `ojb41/baseline.json`.

### H73.1 `this` = Math/JSON, this-insensitive converters, `RegExp.prototype.test`

The 'C' guard now accepts three more cases. In all of them the arguments must still be primitive.

- `this` is the realm's `Math` or `JSON` and the callee is that object's own data property (`Math.random` is still
  denied by name).
- The callee is `String`, `Number`, `Boolean`, `parseInt`, `parseFloat`, `isNaN` or `isFinite` (identity with the
  realm globals), with any `this`.
- The callee is the native `RegExp.prototype.test` and `this` is a plain RegExp:
  - its prototype is `RegExp.prototype`, its only own property is `lastIndex`, and it has no `Symbol.match`;
  - `RegExp.prototype.exec` and the `global`/`sticky` getters are the ones captured at install;
  - it is not global or sticky, and `lastIndex` is a primitive;
  - no argument is a Symbol.

  Under these conditions `test` cannot run user code and does not write `lastIndex`.

`C:thisHost` 604 → 151). Subst ×3: hits 165403–165468, fail 0, timing-only (17, 17, 39 leaves).

### H73.2 The non-native `push` is the harness recorder

The 748 `c:notNative:push` failures came from the recorder's `Array.prototype.push` wrapper (`recorder-src.js`), not
from the VM. The recorder now registers the wrapper in `Symbol.for('deob.lift').nativeEq` (WeakMap wrapper → native).
The 'c' guard checks the native it maps to, and the lifted code calls the same wrapper the VM would.

**Fingerprint trap:** the first version moved the wrapper's lines. `14.CJkad9` (a 1199-char string) then changed in
every baseline, and the cause is that it embeds stack traces with harness line and column numbers. The fix keeps the
wrapper byte-identical and on the same lines (`(0,function(){…})`, so no name is inferred and `push.name` stays
`""`), with the registration on the closing line. Two new baselines (ojb45d) are then **identical** to
`ojb41/baseline` (0 changes). The registration symbol itself is not observed. Any future edit to `recorder-src.js` or
the det layer must keep the line numbers of code that can appear in a stack, or must re-baseline.

## H74 CJkad9 is a stack trace; call/apply chains, `F.p.toString`, det/recorder `nativeEq` (91.66%)

### H74.0 `14.CJkad9` structure (paths and classes only)
Decoded from the pretea plaintext of ojb41/baseline vs ojb44b/baseline (the line-shifted recorder):
- Plain ASCII (code points 10..121), 1199 chars: 845 letters, 229 punctuation, 118 digits, 7 `\n`.
- 8 lines: a 5-char header word, then 7 frames of shape `    <ident> (<scheme>://<host>/<path…>?<k>=<v>:<4-digit line>:<5–6-digit col>)`
  (the last frame has no name/parentheses). All 7 frames point at the orchestrator page URL, i.e. the VM's own
  inline script, not at harness code.
- The 14 differing chars are exactly the 2 low digits of the line number in each of the 7 frames; columns,
  names and lengths are identical. baseline vs baseline2 (same harness): 0 differences.

So the hypothesis holds in a stronger form: CJkad9 is `new Error().stack` (V8 format) taken inside the VM, and
its line numbers move because run-plain inlines DET and HOOK_SRC into the page HTML before the first `<script>`
(run-plain.mjs:85-86). **Any newline added to or removed from determinism-src.js / recorder-src.js (or the DET
option/mark snippets) shifts every frame**, even if the changed code is never on the stack. Rule refined: keep
the total line count of the inlined scripts constant (edits on existing lines are fine).
lift-subst-runtime.js line changes do not matter (H74.1 added 4 lines; subst stayed timing-only).

### H74.1 call/apply chains (e69d660)
The 'C' checks moved into `cOk(cf, hasThis, this, args, depth)` working on values. When `cf` is
`Function.prototype.call` or `.apply` (and `this` is given), the check recurses once or twice on the inner callee
with `this = args[0]` and the remaining args (for `apply`, args[1] must be null/undefined or a plain Array with
own data elements only, length <= 64; else `C:applyArgs`). The inner callee skips the `.call`-patched check
(it is invoked by the native, not through `.call`). Denials at depth>0 are tagged `/d1`.

diffs vs ojb41/baseline 13/0/13 leaves, timing-only.

- determinism-src.js: each console wrapper `w` is registered as `nativeEq(w) = f`; recorder-src.js:

## H75 Page line numbers restored (CJkad9 frames on line 1); `nativeEq` in 'C' (91.99%)

### H75.1 Harness scripts no longer add page lines (6837a21)
The captured orchestrator page is a single line (VM tag and body on line 1). Before H75 the inlined det layer
and recorder added 1078 newlines, so every VM frame in `14.CJkad9` read line 1079. The H36/H38 finding still
holds: `addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` does not reach the OOPIF realm. An external `<script src>` would add
entries to the resource-timing list (`NglZ3`). So the scripts stay inline, but:
- `page.mjs` `oneLine()` compacts the recorder and the det layer with @babel/generator (as H61 did for the lift
  runtime). The result must contain no line terminators, otherwise the build fails. `run-plain.mjs` inlines
  `DETERMINISM_1L`. Workers and the host page keep the multi-line source; their lines do not reach orchestrator
  stacks. `RUN_INJ_ML=1` restores the old behaviour.
- The injected block is padded with spaces to a fixed 8,000,000 chars (`RUN_INJ_PAD`, 0 = off). Without the pad,
  subst/shadowcmp (MB-sized lift table) and baseline put the VM at different columns, and CJkad9 differed in
  subst (seen in ojb49/ojb50: str len 1179 vs 1186, the only non-timing leaf).

CJkad9 check (positions only): ojb51b baselines x3 and ojb52 substs x3 have 7 frames, all at line 1 (the
unmodified page's line). Column numbers are 7 digits, identical across modes. Columns cannot equal the original
page's, because instrument() rewrites the VM body (578315 -> 347240 chars) and the injected prefix sits on the
same line.

Re-baseline: `base51/` (ojb51b). Against ojb41/baseline the only change is `14.CJkad9` (str len 1199 -> 1186).
baseline2 and baseline3 against the new baseline differ by 27 and 4 leaves, timing-only.

## H76 Build check for inlined sources; CJkad9 column analysis; guard: string toLocaleString, push (92.13%)

### H76.1 Build check for one-line sources (9bb1bb1)
`tools/chrome-run/inline-check.mjs` runs inside `oneLine()` for every inlined source: the recorder, the det
layer and the lift runtime. The lift runtime now goes through `oneLine()` too, and a failure stops the build
instead of falling back to the multi-line text. It has two checks:
- **Code inside a `//` comment.** Every line comment is tried as JS from each token boundary. If a suffix parses
  to 2 or more statements, or to any statement that is not an expression/label (if, return, var...), the build
  fails with the line and column. The H75 buggy runtime (rebuilt in scratch) is caught at L229. The current
  sources are clean.
- **One-line AST equals multi-line AST.** Both are parsed, and the node types, names and literal values are
  compared in order (positions, `extra` and comments are ignored). The only normalisation is that same-operator
  logical chains are flattened. `@babel/generator` prints `(a||b)||(c||d)` as `a||b||c||d`, which is the same
  program (same operands, order and short-circuiting) but a different tree. All three sources pass, so the
  one-line code is unchanged from H75.

### H76.2 CJkad9 columns against the unmodified page (77757b1)
`tools/deob-auto/pipeline/cjkad9-colmap.mjs` maps each frame's column back to an offset in the unmodified VM
body. It undoes the injected prefix, the 4 encrypt-hook splices and the `instrument()` source map
(`instrument(js, {map:true})`). It prints positions only. 6 of the 7 frames land exactly on a source-map
segment start, which confirms the offset model.

- **VM start on line 1:** offset 19555 in the unmodified page, 8036541 in ours. The prefix difference is
  8016986 (det layer, recorder and pad).
- **VM-internal shift:** after the prefix is subtracted, frames still shift by -42593 to -230760 chars.
- **Result:** columns equal the unmodified page in 0 of 7 frames, in every mode (deltas identical across
  baselines and substs, ojb54).
- **Why the VM shrinks from 578315 to 347240 chars:** `instrument()` works on `normalize(js)`, which inlines the
  string-decoder calls, folds the opaque-seed constants and so on, and then regenerates the whole program with
  babel `compact`. The served VM is therefore the deobfuscated program, not the original text. The same cause
  also makes `Function.prototype.toString` of VM functions differ from the original page, in every mode.
- **Can it be served byte-identical?** `normalize` is not needed for serving, so the dispatcher hooks could be
  spliced into the raw text. But all 7 frames lie after the first edit site (zT encrypt hook, VM offset 72355;
  dispatcher hooks at 165267, 188435 and 393843; the other encrypt hooks at 429642, 473449 and 546199). Any
  splice that adds length still shifts them. So each layer needs its own fix:
  - **Encrypt hooks (all modes, needed for the pretea dump):** these can move out of the line. Use CDP
    conditional breakpoints on zT/xo/ukb/onb (`Debugger.setBreakpointByUrl`, the condition calls the recorder
    and returns false). The raw VM has no `debugger` statements. Whether enabling the Debugger domain changes
    the payload must be checked against a baseline.
  - **Dispatcher hooks (subst/shadowcmp only):** these need code in the VM closure scope, so a breakpoint cannot
    replace the switch. Only the baseline can be fully column-faithful. Subst could match the baseline only
    with length-neutral splices, which would need original bytes removed elsewhere in the same function.
  - **Prefix (8.0M on line 1):** this must leave the line. `addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` on the OOPIF
    session missed in H36. That run predates the current auto-attach with `waitForDebuggerOnStart`, so retest it
    (det mark check). If it now reaches the realm, line 1 starts with the page's own 19555 chars.
- **Another fidelity gap:** `DET_MARK` sets a `data-det` attribute on `documentElement` in the orchestrator, which
  the page can see. Move it to a CDP-side check.

### H76.3 Guard (d4e4ce7)
- The receiver-class diag (shadowcmp only, because OTS/GPO can hit Proxy traps) showed that `toLocaleString/d1`
  (184) is **`Object.prototype.toLocaleString` on a string primitive**. That is `Invoke(str,'toString')`. It is
  allowed when `String.prototype.toString` is still the native data property (checked live) and
  `String.prototype` has no own `toLocaleString`.
- `C:objArg:push` (82; 68 of them push a Promise): `push` onto a plain, extensible array with writable `length`,
  where none of the new indices has an entry on Array.prototype or Object.prototype, is allowed. `push` only
  stores its arguments.

## §H77 Out-of-line injection and a raw-VM baseline

### H77.1 Early injection into the orchestrator realm (c8c8688, 7062da8)
- stealth-cdp (`/workspace/stealth-tester/repo-c7556da`, the `STEALTH_CDP_DIR` that run2.sh uses) does not inject
  scripts into OOPIFs. It uses recursive `Target.setAutoAttach {waitForDebuggerOnStart}` and sends per-target
  overrides (`Emulation.setUserAgentOverride`, nested auto-attach) before `Runtime.runIfWaitingForDebugger`.
  `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` is only used on the top session, and its hybrid wire policy forbids it.
  The pattern that carries over is "configure the paused child session, then resume".
- Root cause of the H36 miss: on the auto-attached iframe session, `addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` returns an
  identifier but never runs until **`Page.enable`** is sent on that session. A probe (throw at document start,
  `RUN_OOL_PROBE`, logged via `Runtime.exceptionThrown`) fired 0 times without `Page.enable`. With it, it fired in
  the `/rch/` document before any page script, where det and the recorder were already live. It also fired in the
  child about:blank frames, which the gate excludes.
- `RUN_OOL=1` (run-plain) inserts nothing into the orchestrator HTML. There is no det prefix, recorder, lift
  `<script>`, 8M pad or `DET_MARK`. `buildInstrumentedHtml({outOfLine})` returns the former `<script>` bodies as
  `early`. They are installed in order as addScripts on the iframe session (Page.enable first), each gated on
  `location.pathname` containing `/turnstile/f/av0/rch/`. Det is installed the same way, without the DOM marker.
  The top realm keeps its pre-H77 path: when only the orchestrator moved, api.js still threw 200100, so the top
  realm was not the cause of the change.
- `cjkad9-colmap` has a new `COLMAP_MODE` setting: `inline`, `ool` or `raw`.

### H77.2 Debugger domain A/B and conditional-breakpoint capture (3a275ef)
- `RUN_DBG=1` sends `Debugger.enable` on the orchestrator session. The OOL baseline with it (h77h/baseline2) and
  without it (h77g/baseline) have **0 field changes**. The Debugger domain does not change the payload.
- `RUN_BPHOOKS=1` (with `RUN_OOL=1 RUN_RAW=1 RUN_NOHOOKS=1`) serves the VM **byte-identical** (578315, at the
  original offset 19555, line 1). It captures the TEA plaintext with a `Debugger.setBreakpointByUrl` condition at
  the start of ukb's body. The condition calls a Symbol-keyed capture (no new global name) and returns false: the
  breakpoint resolved, the VM paused 0 times, and no frame of ours is on the VM stack. `__deobPreTeaDump` is
  replaced to pick the largest captured plaintext. zT, xo and onb are not needed for the baseline payload and are
  not hooked.

| run | mode | vs base51 (non-timing) | CJkad9 cols = original page |
|---|---|---|---|
| h77g ×2 | OOL, instrumented + hooks | CJkad9, QVHD7.o −1 | 0/7 (VM-internal shift only, prefix delta 0) |
| h77h ×2 | OOL + Debugger.enable | same as h77g; h77g vs h77h = 0 changes | 0/7 |
| h77i ×2 | OOL raw VM, bp capture | CJkad9, QVHD7.o −1, QVHD7.N −1 | **7/7 (delta 0)** |
| h77j | OOL raw VM + encrypt splices, bp capture | vs h77i: QVHD7.N +1 only | |
| h77k | OOL subst | vs h77g: timing-only, CJkad9 equal | 0/7 (same as h77g) |

- h77i baseline vs baseline2: 0 changes. There is no `data-det` in the orchestrator (`mDet` null), and det is
  live (`__deobDet`).
- Two base51 elements were harness artefacts. QVHD7.o +1 came from the inline `DET_MARK` script (`var de` leaks a
  global). QVHD7.N +1 came from the encrypt-hook splices, proven by h77j: most likely the undeclared
  `xo_impl=` in the `xo=` sequence expression became an implicit global.
- Subst works out of line: hits 167494 (H76: 167682), fail 0, haltExtra 0. The -188 has not been explained yet.
- Lifted % is unchanged at 92.13% (no lift/guard change; shadowcmp not re-run).

### Next

instrumented VM, so they get `RUN_OOL=1` only. `OJB_CAPTURE=legacy` restores the pre-H77 inline injection.

### H78.4 Step 2: dispatcher hooks as raw-text splices (offline measurement only)

New `tools/deob-auto/lib/raw-instrument.mjs` provides `instrumentRaw(js, {probeAt})` with the same hook contract as
`instrument()`, but splices raw text. Raw case labels are `<int>^<key>` with a runtime key, so labels are counted
structurally. It finds 2 dispatchers (74/75 labels; instrument: 75/75) and 1 fast path (instrument: 1). The output
parses. Shift at the 7 CJkad9 frame offsets (positions only):

| variant | added chars | frame shifts (orig order) | + encrypt hooks |
|---|---|---|---|
| instrumented VM (today) | −231594 (regenerated) | −42593 … −230760 | — |
| raw, probe inline | +9379 | 9379, 4765, 9379, 9379, 0, 9379, 0 | 9920, 4891, 9505, 9920, 126, 9505, 126 |
| raw, probe at function end | +9413 | 9413, 4782, 4901, 9413, 0, 9413, 0 | 9954, 4908, 5027, 9954, 126, 9539, 126 |

The raw splice cuts the CJkad9 shift by more than an order of magnitude, and 2 of 7 frames become exact. It cannot
reach zero: each probe is a copy of a ~4.6K-char switch inside the VM. Open issues: X6 is not detected in the raw text
(the pattern expects plain `["restore"]`; raw uses obfuscated keys), and it is not yet wired into page.mjs or run in
Chrome. A run with a zero shift needs the hooks as conditional breakpoints (condition = hook call, with the probe kept
outside the script).

## H79 — lifted % re-based on the run's own step count; Promise then/catch and Reflect.apply guards (92.32%)

### H79.1 New share definition

The lifted share is now taken from the run's own shadowcmp trace, not from a fixed denominator.
`tools/deob-auto/pipeline/lifted-share.mjs <trace.ndjson> [topN]` counts the trace rows (one per VM step, op at
index 2, kind `L`/`N` at index 4) and prints `{steps, lifted, native, share, topNative}`.

| trace | steps | lifted | native | share |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ojb38 (inline, old fixed denominator 178971) | 178971 | 161856 | 17115 | 90.44 |
| ojb54 (H76, inline) | 178965 | 164887 | 14078 | 92.134 |
| h78a (H78, base77/OOL) | 178609 | 164571 | 14038 | **92.140** (new base) |
| h79a (H79, this section) | 178687 | 164964 | 13723 | **92.32** |

The step count moves by about ±100 between runs (DOM/environment probe loops), so every run uses its own count.

**Known subst-only delta.** `14.CJkad9` (same length, column digits only) differs between base77 and every subst
run. Subst has to serve the instrumented VM, whose text shifts the stack-frame columns (H75/H76/H78.4). This is the
one accepted subst-only delta. Any other non-timing leaf is still a failure.

### H79.2 Guards (commit f1c6743)

In `cOk` (`tools/chrome-run/lift-subst-runtime.js`):
- **Reflect.apply** is resolved like FAPPLY. `Reflect.apply(f, t, arr)` gets the full checks on `f`, with this = `t`
  and the unpacked `plainArgs(arr)` (depth ≤ 2). A non-function target or non-plain array gives `C:rapplyArgs`.
- **then/catch on a native Promise** are allowed before the name deny-list. `promNative` requires all of these:
  - the proto is the realm's `Promise.prototype` and the Promise has no own keys;
  - `Promise.prototype.then` and `.constructor` are the original data properties;
  - the `Promise[Symbol.species]` getter is the original one;
  - the class is `[object Promise]`;
  - `then.call`/`catch.call` is not patched (at depth 0).

  The arguments may be VM closures, because then/catch only register reactions and never call them synchronously.
  `finally` stays denied: it builds closures and calls the species constructor.
- **shadowcmp double issue.** Shadowcmp runs the native op (the oracle) and then the lifted body on the restored
  state. Re-issuing `then` would register a second reaction, so the callback would run twice. While the lifted body
  runs (`SHL`), `appX` reduces FCALL/FAPPLY/Reflect.apply chains. A then/catch on a native Promise is not re-issued:
  it returns a fresh pending Promise, which is creation-equivalent to the oracle's fresh Promise. Subst issues the call once,
  as the VM does.

Run h79a (run-h74, BASE = base77):

| run | shadowcmp n | trace steps | SC match | miss/err | guard | share | subst hits ×3 | subst n | vs base77 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| h78a | 178489 | 178609 | 164469 | 0/0 | 2256 | 92.140 | 167486, 167486, 167537 | 180758 | timing + `14.CJkad9` |
| h79a | 172824 | 178687 | 164913 | 0/0 | 1944 | 92.32 | 167766 ×3 | 180758 ×3 | 43/43/36: timing + `14.CJkad9` (+ `21.HkyLT5` numeric in subst3, a known native run-to-run leaf) |

- `C:deny` fell from 583 to 222. The then@Promise\*, catch@Promise\* and apply@Reflect entries are gone. The top
  remaining entries are defineProperty@Function\* 69, setTimeout 24, construct@Reflect 22, keys@Function\* 16.
- Some resolved Reflect.apply calls now fail one level down instead, mostly `C:notNative …/d1` (VM closures).
- Subst `fail` is 54 in each run (H78: 0). The first fail is op138 `TypeError: Illegal invocation`. These are
  Reflect.apply calls of a host method with a foreign receiver. The VM makes them on purpose to probe brands, and the
  native op throws in the same way. The lifted body throws before it writes anything, the op falls back to native,
  and the VM sees the native exception. The 54 fails are therefore harmless re-issues of a call that throws and has no
  effect. A brand check in the guard would remove them; this is open.
- Open: shadowcmp `n` (the run's halt counter) dropped by 5665 while the trace step count is unchanged (178687).
  Subst n is unchanged. The cause is not known yet; it may be the halt time relative to the Promise reactions.

### H79.3 op216 and op22: not liftable as bodies

Trace rows were joined to the listing (`subst-tab-h71c.lpc.json` all/why):
- **op216 (1841 dispatches, 549 sites), reason `closure`.** It creates a VM closure: it reads the cell table and
  writes a fresh function whose entry is a listing pc. The function's identity, `toString` and stack text are what
  the payload probes. A lifted body would have to rebuild it outside the VM's own factory. Shadowcmp cannot check
  that, because two fresh functions with the same tag are treated as equal. Not lifted.
- **op22 (1194 dispatches, 30 sites), reason `cell`, `rD = cell[N](rA)`.** It calls a VM closure held in a cell, so
  the VM re-enters itself. The trace's read sets contain the whole nested run. Outputs are mostly fresh
  functions, objects or arrays. A lifted call would run the callee from lift-runtime frames, which is the H61
  CJkad9 stack problem. In shadowcmp it would also run the nested VM twice. Not lifted.
- The way forward for both is a **VM-call transition**, not a body. For op22, push the VM's own return frame and jump
  to the callee's entry pc, as the dispatcher does. This works only if VM-closure calls are not nested
  dispatcher recursions, which needs checking in the disassembly first.

### H79.4 Inline injection and the `37.*` / `31.BnDgz6` signals

New `OJB_CAPTURE=ool` mode in `ojb-matrix.sh`: `RUN_OOL=1` for every label (out-of-line, but with the instrumented
VM and inline encrypt hooks). Each pair below is clean1 vs runtime and clean1 vs ua_headless:

| capture | code | runtime | ua_headless |
|---|---|---|---|
| legacy (inline) | HEAD | 27 timing leaves | 14 (10 structural, 4 string) |
| legacy (inline) | `bb4148a^` (pre-H78, before the xo_impl fix) | 4 timing leaves | 18 (the same 14 + 4 timing) |
| ool | HEAD | 27 timing leaves | 14 (the same set) |
| bp (base77, H78) | — | 0 | 14 (the same set) |

The ua_headless set is always the same: `1.QVHD7` add/remove, `2.yAui9`, `30.StrX9.*`. Neither `37.*` nor the
`31.BnDgz6` index shift reproduces in any mode. Neither the inline `<script>`/DET mark nor the pre-H78
xo_impl leak causes them. The H56/H60 signals came from an older harness state that today's legacy mode does not
recreate, or from the browser/profile state of those runs. For stealth-cdp, the useful finding is negative:
`Runtime.enable` on the orchestrator session and the inline harness do not show up in this build's payload.

2. Add a native brand check to the Reflect.apply resolution (no lifted throw, subst fail back to 0). Explain the

## H80: subst fallbacks proven harmless, shadowcmp n explained, op22 is a frame push

Run `h80a` (base77 baseline, `run-h74.sh`). Lifted share is the same as h79a: **92.32% (164964/178687)**.
Shadowcmp: 164913 match, 0 miss, 0 err. Subst: 3/3 halt, header OK, `fo-post #2` clean 4.
Field diffs from base77: subst 43 and subst2 43 (42 timing leaves plus `14.CJkad9`); subst3 44 (43 timing leaves plus
`14.CJkad9`). Every path that is new in subst3 compared with h79a is a timing leaf whose index moved, because subst3 ran 60 more dispatches (n=180818).

### H80.1 The 54 subst fails are native-equal throws

The subst catch now records `failWhy` (exported in the stats object). The key is
`op|callee@receiverType|errorClass|w<registers changed>`. At the next dispatch it adds a second record:
`nativeThrew` when the pc is not the entry's successor, and `SameClass` when a register holds a fresh error with the
same prototype as ours.
- All 54 fails per run (identical across the three runs) are op138 `rD = rF.call(rT, ...)`, where the callee resolves through `call`/`apply`/`Reflect.apply` to a host or builtin method called with a **`null` receiver**. That is a brand probe across 45 distinct method names: canvas 2D, WebGL, DOM, storage, Trusted Types, String.prototype, `toString`, `sendBeacon`, `terminate` and others. The class is `TypeError` in 54 of 54 cases.
- Writes: **w0 in 54/54**. The lifted attempt changed no register, and the pc and key were unchanged (no `pcMoved`).
- Native: **54/54 `nativeThrewSameClass`**. The native fallback threw too (the VM went to its catch pc) and left a
  fresh `TypeError`-prototype object in a register.
- These host methods with a null receiver throw before they read any argument, so no heap state is written. The fallbacks are harmless, and the fail count is a measure of probing, not a correctness signal. One option is to deny `this==null` for prototype methods in `cOk`, which would bring the count to 0 without changing the share.

### H80.2 The shadowcmp `n` drop is a task-order shift, not lost dispatches

`n` is the recorder's `T.length/4` at the moment of the halt dump (`recorder-src.js` `__deobBP`). The lift runtime calls the
recorder (`orig`) on every dispatch, so no dispatch goes missing. The h78a and h79a lift traces have the same multiset of
`pc:op` dispatches (150 and 228 rows differ). Mapping 30-row windows shows that the work in the h78a positions ~168.6k–174.4k
runs in h79a **after** position ~173k, which is past the halt dump. That cuts `n` by about 5.8k (178489 to 172824) while the
total trace length stays 178687. h80a gives exactly 172824 again, so the shift is deterministic. It follows from the H79
then/catch handling in shadowcmp, which changes Promise reaction order. Subst `n` is unchanged (180758).

### H80.3 op22 VM-closure calls are frame pushes in the same dispatcher

Semantics: `d=callret; call tbl[imm].B(this=undef; r0..rN)`. There are 1194 dispatches in the h79a trace:
- 783 fall through to pc+4 with 2 reads (the cell holds a host function).
- **411 jump to a callee entry pc**. They read about 18 registers and write one (a frame array register), and the next dispatch is the callee entry. **All 411 return through op245**, which restores the saved registers, writes the return value and resumes at op22's pc+4 in the same dispatch loop.
- No trace row is out of order around op22, so this is no JS recursion of the dispatcher.

Design (not yet implemented): a subst `T`-kind entry for op22 that, behind a guard that the cell holds a VM closure,
(1) builds the same frame record op245 pops, (2) writes the frame register and (3) sets C/D to the closure's entry
pc/key. This needs the exact frame layout from the `Iob(…, Wf, …)` handler. Op245 is the matching return transition. I left it unimplemented this session because the frame layout is not yet extracted. A wrong frame would only show up at the return.

### H80 Next
1. Extract the op22/op245 frame layout from the handler source, then implement the call and return transitions, verified by shadowcmp across the
   411 jump sites.
2. Optional: a `C:nullThis` guard for prototype methods (fails 54 to 0).
3. The `37.*`/`31.BnDgz6` bisect (not started).

## H81: C:nullThis deny (subst fails 0), op22 frame layout measured, Promise-order hypothesis refuted

### H81.1 `C:nullThis` deny
`cOk` now refuses a call whose receiver is `null`/`undefined` unless the callee is a static function. A static function is an own data-property function of
`globalThis` (capitalized constructors included), `Math`, `JSON`, `Reflect`, `Atomics` or `Intl`, collected lazily into a WeakSet.
These are the 54 to 59 harmless null-receiver throws from H80.1. They now fall back to native before the attempt.

Full run h81d (standard table `subst-tab-h71c.json`, base77):
- shadowcmp: halt, n=178489, match 164744, **miss 0, err 0**, guard 1981 (`C:nullThis` 59).
- subst/subst2/subst3: **fail 0, haltExtra 0** each, hits 167775 / 167826 / 167775, fo-post clean.
- Field diffs from base77: 43 / 44 / 43 leaves. byTag is `string:1` (`14.CJkad9`, the documented delta) plus timing leaves only.
  The leaf set matches h80a except for `helyQ4` timing leaves, which move position (26/34/35/36) from run to run.
- Lifted share (h81d shadowcmp trace): 92.29% (164846/178609). h80a gave 92.32% (164964/178687). The trace is 78 steps shorter and op138 has 40 more native steps
  (3031 vs 2991). This is run-to-run variation in host-call counts, not a lift loss. No lift entries were removed.

### H81.2 op22/op245 frame layout, measured by instrumenting the live handler
The runtime has a frame probe, `FP` in `lift-subst-runtime.js`, that runs at the top of `__deobBPimpl`. When op22 or op245 dispatches, it snapshots
the register values, array lengths, the S keys `G,K,A,R,P,o` and the pc/key. At the next dispatch it diffs them. The key it records holds only classes, lengths
and relative tags (`pc0+N`, `ky0`, `rN` identity). It records no values. It uses identity comparison only, because a `getOwnPropertyNames` scan of register objects or S stalled the VM
(h81a, n=0 timeout), so that scan was removed. The stats export now has `frameProbe:{n,sig}`.

h81c (4604 probed dispatches):
- **op22 fall-through (host cell), ~780 dispatches:** pc+4, one register write (the result) and no S.G change.
- **op22 jump (VM closure), ~411 dispatches.** Every one:
  - pushes one number onto the w247 array (a stack-depth/marker register);
  - pushes one 9-slot record onto `S.G`:
    `[number, retPc, ky0, regWindow[40], number, argIdx[N], argVal[N], number, number]`
    - `retPc` is **pc0+4** in the common sites and **pc0+3** in the others. Both forms occur, so the return pc depends on the operand form.
    - `regWindow` is a 40-slot copy of the caller's register window (objects and undefined in fixed slots).
    - `argIdx`/`argVal` have equal length N (4 to 8+). `argVal` entries are classes or caller-register identities (`rN`).
  - optionally writes w214 (the first argument or a number) and, at pc0+3 sites, **replaces w63 with a new 40-slot window** (the callee frame);
  - continues at the callee entry pc/key.
- **op245 jump (~2.9k):** `S.G` pops by 1 when the frame belongs to the current closure, and it restores 4 to 40 registers. In 143 dispatches `S.K` changes instead.
  The existing lift (H70) already reproduces this through `X6`.

Call-transition design (not implemented, see H81 Next). An op22 subst `T` entry, guarded on "cell holds a VM closure", has to:
1. build the 9-slot record above (the retPc form depends on the operand form);
2. push it onto S.G and the marker onto w247;
3. write w214/w63 as the native handler does;
4. set C/D to the entry pc/key.

Shadowcmp has to deep-compare the S.G top record, w63, w214, w247 and pc/key right after the call. At the return it has to compare them again (the existing G diff plus a register diff).
I left it unimplemented this session. The slot meaning of the first and the last two numbers, and the `argIdx` source, are not yet tied to operands. Guessing them would give
a frame that only diverges at the return.

### H81.3 Promise-order hypothesis for the `n` drop: refuted
h81c is the same as h81b but with the table cfg `noThen:true`, which makes `then`/`catch` fall back with `C:thenOff` (276 guards). Its n is **178489**. h81b (then/catch lifted) and h81d
also give **178489**, while h80a and h79a gave 172824. So the then/catch lifting does not decide n. The H80.2 claim that the shift "follows from the then/catch handling" is withdrawn.
The task-order shift is real (H80.2 trace mapping) but depends on timing. It changes with any change in per-dispatch runtime cost, such as the H81 probe, and not with any specific lift.
h81c still has shadowcmp miss 0 / err 0 (match 164468, guard 2257).

### H81 Next
1. Tie the op22 record slots to the operands: log the immediate, the operand bytes and the record numbers side by side (values stay in scratch, only relations go in the docs). Then implement the
   call transition with the full-frame shadowcmp at call and return.
2. Make the frame probe opt-in (`cfg.frameProbe`) so the default runs keep the timing of the previous builds.
3. The `37.*`/`31.BnDgz6` bisect (not started).

## H82: frameProbe opt-in, op22 CALL lift (3 sites), full-frame shadowcmp

### H82.1 Step 2 first: frame probe opt-in
`CFG.frameProbe` (table `cfg.frameProbe:true`) gates the H81 `FP` probe. Default off. Default runs keep the prior timing; the probe is only for measurement. Stats still export `frameProbe:{n,sig,sites,slotUse}` (zeros when off).

### H82.2 Step 1: pin unknown slots through execution
Probe run (`subst-tab-h82-probe.json`, `cfg.frameProbe:true`) over the 21 VM-call sites (411 hits). Operand width from `retPc − handlerPc` (handler pc = listing insn start + 1):

| opW | sites | meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 4 | 2 (77418:43, 79933:182) | common form; fall = pc+4 |
| 3 | 19 | varint form; fall = pc+3 |

Frame `F` is the 9-slot record pushed onto `S.G`. Slot meanings (from readable `jY`/`Wf`/`X6` plus probe `slotUse`; values stay in scratch):

| slot | source | consumed at op245 (X6)? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | `S.K` (path key) | yes — `F[0]===S.K` selects the in-VM return path |
| 1 | `retPc` = `m.C` after fall-through write | yes — restored into `m.C` |
| 2 | `retKy` = `m.D` after fall-through write | yes — restored into `m.D` |
| 3 | `m.read(S.A)` (caller cell table / window) | yes — identity restore into `S.A` |
| 4 | `destL ^ regX` (result write target) | yes — write or keep (probe: write≈1636 / keep≈994 of tagged returns) |
| 5 | `meta[3]` saved-index list | yes — restore loop |
| 6 | `m.save(idxs[i])` saved values | yes — restore loop |
| 7 | `STK.length` (`239^o`) | **no** — unused at X6 (probe: unused on every tagged return) |
| 8 | `DEP.length` (`o^29`) | yes — trim try-stack to this length |

Probe tags `1:pc` / `2:ky` at the *next* dispatch always read "no" because fetch already advanced C/D; consumption is pinned from X6, not from the next-dispatch diff. `CALL_MASKS = [60,135,39,44,207,251,131,84]` write the first `argc` args into `mask[i]^o`.

### H82.3 Call transition + full-frame shadowcmp
New subst entry form `[null,'CALL',fallPc,fallKy,['V',cellImm,destL,...argLs]]`. Guard `V` requires `S.O.get(cell)` to be a VM meta array. `runCall` builds `F`, pushes `S.G` and `DEP`, installs callee `A`/args, sets C/D to `meta[0]/meta[1]`.

Shadowcmp at CALL and RET:
- full-frame `frameDeepEq` on every `S.G` entry (identity short-circuit; deep on slot 3 A and slots 5/6);
- try-stack (`o^29`) deep compare;
- deep A and `CALL_MASKS` registers **only on CALL** (deep A on every RET stalled the VM: n=0 timeout via `liteSnap` of the cell table).

`tools/deob-auto/bake-call-from-probe.mjs` turns a frameProbe sites dump + the stage2 readable listing into CALL entries.

Rule (every site that matches all of these is emitted):
