## H123 — No partial-then-fallback; derived edges, CLOS and nested CALL (2026-10-30)

### Finding: the 67 H122 fnFail were dual execution
I instrumented each mid-function bail in H122 (per-step trace of what the readable fn did before it restored and handed over). **All 67 were partial-then-fallback.** None bailed strictly before its first step.
- **65**: bailed on a `c`/`C` call-guard purity check after 3–13 readable steps. Those steps were VM-internal (register/cell writes, no native calls: fxNative 0), then the state was restored and the interpreter re-ran the same invocation.
- **1** (`fn_document_227253`): hit the step cap (`stepKeys.length + 64`) after 98 steps.
- **1** (`probe_22_helper_184670`): hit the loop cap after 138 steps, **including 8 native calls**. The interpreter then ran those calls again, so this was real duplicated native execution.

Restoring registers does not undo the native calls, so H122's "fnBoth=0" was wrong: it did not count partial-then-fallback as dual execution.

### Fix (option b): decide at entry only
- The purity guards are dropped in exclusive FN mode. A readable fn performs calls itself, exactly as the interpreter would; only semantic prechecks remain (`fnSemWhy`: callee is a function, and so on).
- Entry-only fallback (`fnEntryFb`) is allowed only before the first step, when no state has been touched.
- Any bail after the first step is a loud assert: `fnBoth++`, `fnPartial++`, `fnMidAssert++`. The error is thrown, with no restore and no interpreter hand-off. `dualPathAssert` now counts this as fnBoth.
- The loop cap is 4,000,000 steps and only asserts a hang. A readable fn runs to its RET, like the interpreter.
- When a callee is a VM closure, `c`/`C` is a plain JS call through the closure wrapper. That is a separate invocation, which makes its own XOR decision at its own entry (`fnVmCall` 2, `fnNested` counts these).

### Coverage
1. **Edges from static opcode semantics (s2x).** `compile-fn-bodies-h123.mjs` derives FN-only entries (`s2x`, 409) from `stage2.int-plain-1.json` listing records. It uses `x.frame` and `x.inst` statements plus `succ`, keyed by runtime-dispatched native coordinates (`native-sites-h123.json`, 1721 sites, coordinates only), because hkey is post-fetch and cannot be computed statically.
   - Supported ops: 211 `cell[i]=newCell()` (93), 142 `rO["lit"]=rV` (208), 1 `new rC(…)` (57), 138 `rO.NAME(…)` (51).
   - Skipped: ops 16, 113, 114 and 168 (VM-frame call forms); redacted literals (233, 200); and non-sequential successors (69, 66, 22, 242, 186, 160).
   - s2 is unchanged. s2x keys never collide with s2 (the compiler throws if they do).
2. **CLOS-in-reach.** The H122 divergence had a root cause: `runClos` decodes its operands from the VM byte cursor. In a readable walk the cursor was left at the fn's entry, so it decoded wrong bytes. The symptoms were `CLOS:badMode:11`, a Promise resolver receiving an Array, and hangs. The fix places the cursor at the CLOS step's own post-fetch (hpc, hkey) before `runClos`, as the dispatcher would. All CLOS-in-reach fns are now allowed (`fnClos` 490 per run).
3. **Nested CALL.** Op201 inside an exclusive fn calls the callee through its closure wrapper (`fnNestedCall`), writes the result to the dest register, and resumes at the listing-space fallthrough. No in-VM frame is pushed, so the caller is never handed back to the interpreter.
   - I bisected all 26 CALL-in-reach fns. Exactly one, `fn_then_cb_120988`, diverged the sha: its call returns undefined with no exception, and the sha was nondeterministic across runs.
   - That fn is kept on the interpreter, decided at entry (`call-nested-diverges`). The cause is not yet identified; it is probably VM frame state the wrapper does not share, such as preemption or the this register.

## H124 — Native call ops as nested calls; then-callback root cause (2026-10-30)

### Finding 1: `fn_then_cb_120988` is deterministic stack fingerprinting, not nondeterminism
H123 said this function's sha divergence was "nondeterministic across runs". That was wrong. I checked it by execution:
- It is a promise then-callback. The callback returns undefined with no exception on both paths. The divergence comes from what its callee observes, not from the callback's own return value.
- Through an op16 site, its callee `probe_12_helper_120600` reaches an `Error.prepareStackTrace` capture that the VM installs. The payload hashes the captured JS stack.
- On the readable path, that stack contains the closure-wrapper and runtime frames instead of the interpreter's single dispatcher frame. The frames' columns depend on the runtime source text. That is why the sha changed whenever `lift-subst-runtime.js` was edited, which looked like run-to-run variation.
- No env pin is possible, because the VM owns `prepareStackTrace`. The function is kept on the interpreter, decided at entry, with reason `call-stack-observed`.

### Finding 2: native call handler semantics (ops 16, 168, 114, 113)
I executed the real handler at every listing site with tagged inputs (`tools/deob-auto/call-mode-sites.mjs` → `out/3158346939/call-modes-h124.json`, positions only):
- **method** (881 sites; ops 16/168/114): if the receiver is undefined, it calls `key(args)` with `this=undefined`. Otherwise it calls `receiver[key](args)` with `this=receiver`. A null receiver throws a TypeError, as in the interpreter.
- **plain** (136; op113): `r0(args)` with `this=undefined`.
- **other** (35; op16): a direct variant, or a read order that differs from the listing. These sites are not translated, and those fns stay on the interpreter.

The listing semantics ignored the undefined-receiver rule. A first translation threw `undefined[fn]` on the FN path (fnBoth at `fn_canvas_getImageData_42503`; ops 168/114 hung). The runtime evaluator has no arrows and no ternary, so the rule is the `MC(obj,key,…args)` primitive in `lift-subst-runtime.js`. A VM closure callee goes through its closure wrapper, which makes its own entry decision (nested XOR). Non-VM callees are counted in `fnCallNat`.

### Finding 3: `probe_14_helper_198085` (combo-only divergence)
Each op family on its own was golden: 168, 16, 114, 113, the CALL family 22/201/242, and the branch families 160/186/69. The combination was not golden, with fnBoth 0. Two functions become gateable only in the combination. Bisection:
- denying `probe_14_helper_198085` → golden;
- denying only `fn_canvas_getImageData_30198` → not golden.

The function runs `new globalThis[r213]()` at pc 198210. By execution (constructor-name count on the readable path, same table ± this deny), gating it adds exactly **one** readable-path `Error` construction (2 vs 1) and the sha diverges. This is the same stack-fingerprint class as Finding 1, kept on the interpreter (`call-stack-observed`). I did not trace the exact stack read for this one.

- Still skipped: redacted literals (233×271, 200×113, 138×2). Their values must not enter a committed table, so they are out of scope. Also skipped: op66 not-seq 29, listing-not-unique 23, and a few singles (154, 166, 249, 233 not-seq, 242 call-fall-none, 200 get-shape).
- The CALL self-check compares translated against handler-observed for 71 sites: 2 are ambiguous and skipped.

## H125 — Redacted-literal pins (local), OP52 handler, small-fn structure (2026-10-30)

### Redacted-literal sites: runtime pins, never committed
- `tools/deob-auto/pin-redacted-sites.mjs` reads the values of redacted-literal sites (op233, op200 keys, op138 keys) by running the real handler on the local offline capture. It writes them to a **local, uncommitted** pin file, which is regenerated for each build. For this build: 452 pins, 0 bad.
- The committed table holds only positions and `needPins` markers. At load, the runtime reads the pin file through `RUN_LIFT_PINS` (`run-plain.mjs` → `LIFT_TAB.pins`). If a function's pins are missing, it is dropped to the interpreter at entry. Live drops: 0.
- A sidecar `<pins>.shapes.json` holds only register shapes (`{recv,args,ret,w}` for op138, `{w}` for op200), with no literal values. The compiler uses it to emit `W(ret, R(o)[KP(pc)](args))` for op138. Without the shape, a redacted op138 dropped its return register, and the function hung or read null partway through. A site with no matching shape is skipped (`redacted-138-shape` / `redacted-200-shape`).
- `page.mjs`: the table embedded in the page now escapes `</script` and non-ASCII characters. A pin value containing `</script>` had split the outOfLine script, so the lift was not installed.
- Leak check (**corrected in §H127**): the original claim here, that none of the pin values occurs in `subst-tab-h125.json`, `fn-report-h125.json`, the shapes file or the docs, was wrong. Re-measured in H127 (by count only, no values printed): **17 distinct pin values (length ≥ 4) do occur** in those committed files. All 17 were already present in the tree before the H125 commit (in earlier committed subst tables, stage listings and readable renders), so H125 introduced **no new** pin value. What H125 actually guaranteed is narrower: the pin *file* is never committed, and no pin value that was not already committed was added.

### OP52: real handler executed
- `tools/deob-auto/verify-op52-handler.mjs` runs the real op52 handler at all 195 listing sites (32 seq, 163 guard) and compares it with the compiled semantics: **195/195 ok**, 0 register writes.
- The compiled precondition is `P52 [reg, props, imm]`, checked at entry only. Each hop must be a non-null object. Each property must be either a data property or the platform `MessageEvent.prototype.data` getter. That getter is captured at load and checked by identity, so a getter installed by the page is refused. A string whose hash equals `imm` would take the hash arm, which was never recorded, so that invocation falls back at entry (`P52:hash`).
- `probe_13_helper_130954` is now gated. The P52 entry check passes 5× per run. At T2/T4, one invocation per run hit `P52:hash` and ran on the interpreter. That is an entry decision, not a partial run.

### Small functions
- `no-annotated-stmts` (3 fns): `extractFns` counted braces inside string literals. It now uses a string- and comment-aware `braceDelta`. `fn_Worker_165395` and `probe_2_DfaES6` are now gated. `fn_performance_cb_141924` now gets as far as structure, and its one remaining blocker is a missing edge (op69 call-shape at 141942).
- Still on the interpreter:
  - `fn_then_71987` and `probe_3_helper_196195` (`ann-not-in-reach`): the unreached statements are only reachable through a VM try handler (`TP`). In `probe_3` this is `String.fromCodePoint(-1)`, which throws a RangeError into its handler. The compiled body has no exceptional-edge semantics, so these stay on the interpreter.
  - `main` (`no-entry-label`): this is the VM bootstrap at pc 0, not a function. It runs once and leaves through `goto L479767_244` with no RET.

### call-stack-observed: 6 (same mechanism as H124)
- Seed (3): `fn_then_cb_120988`, `probe_14_helper_198085`, `probe_12_helper_120600`. The last one is the op16 callee that reaches the VM-installed `Error.prepareStackTrace` capture. It became gateable only once pinned, and it diverged the sha, so it is denied.
- Derived (3): `probe_12_helper_121708`, `probe_12_rwlrt9`, `probe_14_document_documentElement_outerHTML_CSSOM`. The compiler takes the transitive closure of callers that `CLOS`-invoke a denied fn. A gated caller would add wrapper frames to the stack the VM captures.
- The brief said to leave "the 2" on the interpreter. The other 4 are the same stack-fingerprint mechanism, and bisection showed `probe_14_document_…` diverging before it was denied.

## H126 — Handler-derived site operands, unlisted-key edges, call-stack-observed closure (2026-10-30)

### Wrong op138 operands found by executing the real handlers
- `tools/deob-auto/derive-sites-h126.mjs` runs the real handler at every listing site. Each register is seeded with its own callable proxy, so receiver, arguments and destination come from what the handler reads, not from values in the capture. Output: `out/3158346939/derived-sites-h126.json`, which holds positions, registers and small immediates only. It has `dec` 28274, `o66` 39, `o69` 10, `o138` 666 and `u` 14. Skipped: `138:shape` 789 (`.call`-style sites, translated elsewhere), `138:err` 2, `66:err` 1.
- **51 op138 call-guard entries in the shared s2 (from H122) had a wrong register:** 50 had the wrong receiver and 1 had the wrong argument. H122 had picked the register by matching values in the capture, and two registers can hold equal values. This caused the mid-fn `c:noProp` guard failures seen when new fns were gated.
- The compiler (`compile-fn-bodies-h126.mjs`, FIX138) regenerates those 51 entries from the handler-derived operands (`fixed 51, skipped 0`; knob `H126_FIX138=0`). The table built with the H125 fn set is still golden after the fix.

### Missing edges: exact decode plus unlisted-key derivation
- Edge resolution is exact: the listing record at (lpc, pre-decode key) maps to exactly one (hpc, hkey, op) table key. A successor that was never executed in the capture gets an s2x entry from that listing record, using the same translation as native sites.
- Some successor keys appear nowhere in the listing (`u`). For those, the derive tool decodes (pc, key) and executes the real handler, for op66, 195, 114, 168 and 16. For the call ops it checks both receiver modes against `MC` semantics. It also walks forward until it reaches a listed site. op66 member names are local `m<pc>k<key>` pins, so the committed `u` entries contain only `R`/`W`/`C`/`KP`/`MC` code.
  - The targets come from the compiler (`H126_UMISS`), and I iterated this to a fixpoint over the union of targets.
  - A decode whose hpc is not pc+1 is rejected (`prefix`). It folded op72 prefix steps that the runtime executes as steps of their own, and it gave a wrong key at 59059.
  - A `u` entry is used only where the pc has no captured entry at all. It is also kept out of the by-pc index, so it cannot change how a captured key resolves. Before this guard, `fn_canvas_getImageData_56911` lost 4 steps and failed at T1.
- Still missing: the generic ops seen in the `u` skips (233, 236, 245, 150, 7, 200, 199, 222, …) have no handler model in the derive tool. The synth skips are op249 not-seq 127, op249 op-unsupported 64, 166 call-shape 2, 154 op-unsupported 2, 242 call-fall-none 1, 200 get-shape 1.

### call-stack-observed: 9 (same mechanism as H124/H125)
- I bisected the one sha divergence to `fn_then_cb_121106`. Both of its arms call cell[25] = `CLOS(fn_then_cb_120988)`, which is a seed fn, so I added it to `CALL_DENY`.
- The transitive CLOS-caller closure now also covers `fn_then_121265` and `probe_multi_1_2_3`.
- Set: `fn_then_cb_120988`, `probe_14_helper_198085`, `probe_12_helper_120600`, `fn_then_cb_121106` (denied), plus the derived `probe_12_helper_121708`, `probe_12_rwlrt9`, `probe_14_document_documentElement_outerHTML_CSSOM`, `fn_then_121265`, `probe_multi_1_2_3`.
- The original 6 stay on the interpreter, as the brief asked.

- `fn_then_71987` and `probe_3_helper_196195` still need exceptional-edge semantics. In probe_3, `String.fromCodePoint(-1)` throws into a `TP` handler.
- `fn_performance_cb_142218` and `bag_init_48fields` are newly `ann-not-in-reach`: their edges now resolve exactly, but some annotated statements are not reached from entry. Not yet root-caused; a handler edge is the likely cause, but I have not verified it.

## H127 — VM try-handler edges, op249/op150 from executed handlers, corrected H125 leak claim (2026-10-30)

### op249 and op150 derived by executing the real handlers
- `tools/deob-auto/derive-sites-h127.mjs` extends the H126 derive tool. Output: `out/3158346939/derived-sites-h127.json` (positions, registers, small immediates only): `dec` 28274, `o66` 39, `o69` 10, `o138` 666, **`o249` 192**, **`u` 548** (H126: 14).
- **op249 = `THROW R(reg)`**. Each of the 192 listing sites was run with a tagged register file; the handler throws the tagged value of exactly one register and writes nothing. Compiled as a terminal statement (`throwN`).
- **op150 (unlisted handler-code successors)**: the first op of every VM catch handler is a register move out of the exception register. The derive tool runs the real handler twice with two distinct tag sets (`runMove`) and accepts the site only when there is a single read register, a single non-`N` write whose value is that register's tag in both runs, and the same next (pc, key) in both runs. Derived entry: `W(dst,(R(src)))`. 533 such `u150` entries are used by the compiler.
- Remaining `u` skips (not modelled, documented): op7 229 (property get over two registers), op233 37 and op236 26 (constant loads that would need new pins), `prefix` 34, `195:shape` 11, others small. Synth skips: `166:call-shape` 2, `154:op-unsupported` 2, `242:call-fall-none` 1, `200:get-shape` 1.

### VM catch rule, modelled in the compiled body
- The orchestrator run loop catches every throw from a step (the `__deobBP` hook runs before the switch inside the same try, so a hook throw is equivalent). It pops the top of the try stack `R(48)`. If the popped handler `h` is truthy, it resumes at `(h[0]+1):h[1]`, writes the exception to `R(147)`, restores the cell pointer to `h[2]` and truncates `R(194)` to length `h[3]`. If `h` is undefined (the closure wrapper pushes an undefined sentinel) or `R(48)` is empty, it rethrows.
- `TP(R(48), pc, key, R(194))` (try-push) statements now give the compiler a handler edge. The compiler (`compile-fn-bodies-h127.mjs`) explores handler arms in a second, *exceptional* region of the BFS (`qx`), only after all normal edges. A missing edge inside the exceptional region is counted `tpSoft`, not `missing-edges`: it can only be reached by a throw, and a throw that lands there raises the self-error `H127 handler outside fn`, which counts as `fnFail` + `fnBoth` and fails the gate. It never resumes partway on the interpreter.
- Runtime (`lift-subst-runtime.js`): the CFG walk wraps the body in a try. A self-error (`SELF_ERR`, runtime assertion messages) always rethrows. Otherwise, if the `R(48)` top is falsy, the error escapes the fn exactly like the VM rethrow (`fnEsc`; counted as a completed readable invocation, not a fallback). If it is truthy, the handler key must be inside the fn's compiled set; then the runtime applies the same four effects as the VM and continues (`fnCatch`).
- Table-wide: 90 fns have `tryN` > 0 (307 TP sites), 88 have `tpSoft` > 0 (299 soft handler edges), 3 have `throwN` > 0.
- Decision is still made at entry only; the catch is part of the readable path, not a switch to the interpreter.

### Target fns
- `fn_webrtc_offer_79952` is **gated** (tryN 186, throwN 186, tpSoft 184). Its H126 missing edges were all op249 sites.
- The H126 `ann-not-in-reach` fns `fn_then_71987` (tryN 6, tpSoft 2), `probe_3_helper_196195` (tryN 1; the `String.fromCodePoint(-1)` throw lands in its TP handler) and `fn_performance_cb_142218` (tryN 1) are **gated**: their unreached annotations were the handler arms.
- `bag_init_48fields` stays `ann-not-in-reach`. Its unreached annotation is an op233 `[N]` statement, not a handler arm, so try edges do not explain it. Not root-caused.

### Kept on the interpreter (documented, not fixable within the rules)
- **call-stack-observed 9** (same set as §H126). These fns, or a callee they reach through `CLOS`, read a JS stack captured by the VM's own `Error.prepareStackTrace`, and the payload hashes it. On the readable path the stack contains the runtime's wrapper and compiled-body frames, with function names and line/column positions from `lift-subst-runtime.js`; on the interpreter path it contains only the VM dispatch frames. To make them match I would have to (a) install or wrap `prepareStackTrace`, which the VM owns and observes, or (b) pin the captured stack string in the environment, which is a recorded-return stub. Both change VM-observable behaviour, so both are excluded. Running the gated body inside the VM's own dispatch frame would mean running both paths, which the XOR rule forbids.
- **main** (`no-entry-label`): the VM bootstrap at pc 0. It runs once and leaves through `goto L479767_244` with no RET, so it is not a function body.

## H128 — exec-lift of op7 / op233 / op236 and other unlisted sites; bag_init_48fields root-caused (2026-10-30)

### Exec-lift: entries derived by executing the real handler
- New module `tools/deob-auto/lib/exec-lift-h128.mjs` (`makeExecLift`). For a site the earlier derive rules skip, it runs the real handler on a cloned VM with a planted register file and reads back the writes, the next (pc, key) and the branch target. It never changes CF script bytes; it only reads the offline capture.
- Register values are planted with `regInit` (not a read hook). A discovery pass finds the read set first (cached per site). A read the handler makes after its own write therefore sees the written value, which branch ops such as op186 need.
- 12 numeric runs with hashed, non-monotonic register values plus one string run. Monotonic values let relational ops fit as constant `false`; the hashed runs remove that. The string run fixes the operand order of `+`.
- Value fit: register copy, constant, or a binop over every ordered pair of read registers. Hits are grouped by canonical form (mirrored relational ops are the same form); exactly one canonical form must remain, otherwise the site is skipped (`x-fit`).
- Lifts, in order: cell store, property get (op7: `R(a)[R(b)]` over two registers), named get, native call, `CLOS` (op216), `new` (op1, meta `['N', c]`, same form as the compiler's translate), flow / compare-branch (the condition is the comparison, or `R(w)` when the destination is an operand; arms checked across runs).
- Constant loads (op233, op236): the value the handler writes is a redacted literal. It is stored as a pin `KP(m<pc>k<key>)`; the value goes only into the scratch pin file (regenerated per build, never committed). Only strings and finite numbers are pinned; anything else skips (`x-const-type`).
- Self-check against captured s2 entries: op7, 233, 236, 216, 201, 22, 242 identical; op186 54 identical and 4 value-specialized; op150/195/222/240 differences are all s2 specializations (constant-propagated registers, fused `>>> 0`, fused named gets). op1 `new`: 40/40 identical to the listing.
- `derive-sites-h128.mjs` falls back to exec-lift on handler-specific skips and writes `u` (unlisted keys) and `x` (listed sites whose translate failed). `compile-fn-bodies-h128.mjs` uses `DER.x` (`xFallback`) when the decode matches. The derive/compile loop was iterated until the target set stopped growing (1106 targets).
- `derived-sites-h128.json`: `dec` 28274, `o66` 39, `o69` 10, `o138` 666, `o249` 192, **`u` 1963** (H127: 548), **`x` 5**. Largest `u` ops: op150 674, op236 323, op7 318, op233 237, op138 86, op200 79, op66 68.
- Remaining derive skips (sites not needed by any gated path): `prefix` 284, `x138:x-err` 45, `x245:x-err` 20, `x160`/`x210`/`x199`/`x161` `x-nowrite` 45, `x-fit` 13, small others.

### bag_init_48fields: root cause
- Its one unreached annotation is the op233 statement at lpc 479767 (key 244). That statement is **main's** continuation: main does `CLOS` at 128, op150 at 146, then op53 at 149, which jumps to 479767:244 over the closure body (442192..479766). The statement only lies inside bag_init's pc range in the listing; no edge inside bag_init reaches it.
- The compiler now records listing predecessors. An unreached annotation whose predecessors are all outside the fn (and which is not the fn entry) is counted as soft `ann-foreign-entry`, not `ann-not-in-reach`.
- With that fixed, bag_init_48fields reports its underlying reason: **call-stack-observed**. Its body contains `CLOS(probe_multi_1_2_3)`, a call-stack-observed fn, so it joins the set through the existing transitive CLOS closure (§H125). It stays on the interpreter.

### Target fns
- All 17 H127 missing-edges fns are **gated**, including `fn_navigator_cb_32024` and `fn_Promise_48662` (the last two closed after further derive/compile iterations).
- missing-edges **0**, ann-not-in-reach **0**.

### Kept on the interpreter (intentional)
- **call-stack-observed 10**: `probe_12_helper_120600`, `fn_then_cb_120988`, `fn_then_cb_121106`, `fn_then_121265`, `probe_12_helper_121708`, `probe_12_rwlrt9`, `probe_14_helper_198085`, `probe_14_document_documentElement_outerHTML_CSSOM`, `probe_multi_1_2_3`, `bag_init_48fields`. Same mechanism as §H127 (the payload hashes a stack captured by the VM's own `prepareStackTrace`); bag_init_48fields is the transitive CLOS caller described above.

## §H130: executed orchestrator readable, stage1 function-level gate, variation root causes

Start: H129. 314 stage2 SHA-gated, stage1 per-op only, orchestrator translated-unverified,
canvas_noread / native / noworker unexplained.

### Orchestrator: executed instrumented readable + colFit (H117 method)

- `tools/deob-auto/orch-exec-h130.mjs <orch.dat> <out.readable.js> <pins.json> [--posmap <map.json>] [--check]`
  builds the executable readable. It applies the H129 normalize + pretty-print and replaces 391 sensitive
  literal sites (cfg 37, str 346, tpl 1, ident 5, hexSplit 2; 124 pin values) by
  `globalThis[Symbol.for("deob.orchPin")][i]`. The pin table and posmap are local files only.
  Result: sha256 `7a1a0b75be45…`, 514195 bytes, 11323 lines. It is committed as
  `out/3158346939/int-plain-1.orchestrator.exec-h130.readable.js` and as `final/orchestrator.readable.js`.
- Byte relation between the committed readable and the served text (deterministic, recorded per run as `orchReadable`):
  1. `run-plain.mjs` loads `RUN_ORCH_READABLE` and refuses to run unless the posmap sha256 (`RUN_ORCH_STACKMAP`)
     equals the readable's sha256. The pin table (`RUN_ORCH_PINS`) is installed by an out-of-line `ORCH_GATE` script.
  2. `page.mjs buildInstrumentedHtml(…, {orchText, orchPosMap})`:
     - `instrument(text, {map, colFit, comments:false})`;
     - map entries re-pointed through the posmap (readable line:col → original VM line:col);
     - `injectEncryptHooks`;
     - `anchorColumns(original VM text, …)`.
     Tokens sit at the original page columns, so CF's own `Error.prepareStackTrace` capture sees the
     original positions. The harness never overrides it.
  3. `RUN_RAW` labels (baseline) serve the captured page.
- `RUN_ORCH_RAWDIAG=1` is **diagnostic only**, off by default and not part of any gate. It serves the verbatim
  readable with an `Error.prepareStackTrace` frame remap (`ORCH_STACK_SRC`), which the project rule forbids for gates.
- Gate with the executed readable: T1/T2/T4 baseline×2 + subst×3, **15/15** `7ca3e0532109`. SC miss 0 / err 0
  (label sha `9f14516bebc3`, as every shadowcmp run). VERIFY 23/23, INVC 10/10, INVN 13/13.
- VERIFY / INV exercise the VM paths, not the `ORCH_GATE` pin script itself.

### Stage1 function-level gate

- `H130_STAGE=1` makes `derive-sites-h128.mjs`, `call-mode-sites.mjs` and `pin-redacted-sites.mjs` work on
  the stage1 VM program (captureFirst without input). The same `compile-fn-bodies-h128.mjs` produces the bodies.
- `merge-stage1-fnbodies-h130.mjs` merges them into `subst-tab-h130.json`. Stage1 bodies are keyed `s1|hpc:hkey`,
  their FN-only entries are in `s1x` (46), and stage1 pins are `KP(s1p<pc>)`. `meta.h130` = {s1Fns 10, s1xN 46}.
  Stage2 is unchanged from `subst-tab-h128.json`. Every stage1 body has `readableHash == execHash`.
  Re-deriving stage1 with the H130 store rule (below) gives byte-identical derived sites and pins.
- `lift-subst-runtime.js` selects `s1|` bodies and `s1x` only while the stage1 table is active, and counts
  `fnS1` / `__deobFnS1Names`.
- Gate (`subst-tab-h130.json`, local stage1+stage2 pins):
  - T1/T2/T4 × baseline + subst×3: **12/12** `7ca3e0532109`;
  - fnFail 0, fnBoth 0, fnPartial 0, fnEntryFb 0; fnInvoc 3305 (T4 3309); fnS1 40;
  - shadowcmp miss 0 / err 0 (hits 170136);
  - VERIFY 23/23, INVC 10/10, INVN 13/13.
- Classes: 8 SHA-gated (executed in every run), `main` SC-verified, 2 gated-not-executed:
  - `probe_29_helper_169` is the `toJSON` CLOS @3017 / stored @3027, which runs only on JSON serialization of its object;
  - `probe_29_helper_688` is a 5000 ms `setTimeout` webrtc cleanup @4151, and the run halts first.
- `build-final-h130.mjs [--check]` (from h129) emits stage1 files from `s1|` bodies (sha-checked), classes,
  `index.stage1Gate`, and `evidence-h130.json` (shas and counts only). `--check`: OK, 340 files.

### Variation root causes (by execution)

- `RUN_BP_DIAG='[{name, at, off, expr}]'` (diagnostic only) adds CDP conditional breakpoints that
  record hits and `expr` values and never pause. `run-plain.mjs` also logs target lifecycle events and failed-request
  path shapes (word segments only).
- **native**: the BP sites were computed before the inline `RUN_HOOK` splice, so the capture breakpoint sat at a stale
  column. They now come from the final html. baseline = subst = `4de65633c1c6`.
- **noworker**: `new zW.Worker` throws `TypeError` → catch → reject → `unsupported_browser`; the VM runs 0 instructions.
  This is a deliberate bail, so there is no payload in any mode.
- **canvas_noread**: baseline / tracebase / shadowcmp agree; subst fails on the readback-failure path, which no golden run
  takes. New real-handler rules in `derive-sites-h128.mjs` (`H130_RET=0` disables them), each self-checked against the
  captured stage2 table with `H130_RETCHK`:
  - `ret245` (op245 return): 636/680 byte-same; the other 44 differ only because the captured entry constant-folds the returned value;
  - `br16x` (op160/161 branch): 1348/1348;
  - `store19x` (op199/210 keyed store; two tagged runs, exactly one recorded set, no other effects): 1251/1251.
  `H130_TRACE=<tracebase.trace.json,…>` seeds prefix sites from offline traces. The first failure moved from @57928 op199
  to **@58022 op200** in `fn_canvas_getImageData_56911`: the derived call successor is 58026:27, the live one 58027:27.
  Still 9 fnFail (all fnBoth). `out/3158346939/derived-sites-h130.json` is this **candidate** derivation. It is not
  in `subst-tab-h130.json` and not gated.

### End state

- stage1: 8 SHA-gated, 2 gated-not-executed, 1 SC-verified.
- stage2: 314 SHA-gated, 11 SC-verified.

## §H131: canvas_noread closed by execution (op138 apply, op53 jump, VM throw rule, op103/op26 stores)

Start: H130. canvas_noread subst failed in `fn_canvas_getImageData_56911` (fnFail 9, all fnBoth), first at **@58022 op200**.

### op200 @58022: not the fault

- The H130 "successor mismatch" (derived 58026:27, live 58027:27) compared two different things. Runtime lookup keys
  are pre-fetch keys, `hk = (nextPc + 1) + ':' + nextKy`. The derived op200 successor (58026) is the `nextPc`
  of the entry, and the live 58027 is the `hk` pc of that same successor. They name the same step.
- The real failure was the next step, **58027:128 op138**. That is the apply form `rDst = rF.call(rThis, …args)`:
  the handler applies a register value with an explicit `this` register and does no property get. H130's
  op138 derivation knew only the member-call form, so the step had no entry.
- `derive-sites-h128.mjs` `run138g` / `derive138`: the real handler runs twice (T/U tags) with a distinct
  proxy per register (`get`, `apply`, `has` traps).
  - Member form: one get and one call on the same receiver, with `this` equal to the receiver, a literal name
    (stored as a pin `KP(m<pc>k<key>)`, tag `['c', recv, {kp}, args]`), read-register args, a single non-N write
    equal to the call result, and the same successor in both runs.
  - Apply form: no get, one apply, `this` and args are read registers, tag `['C', f, this, args]`.
  - The runtime resolves `{kp}` guards from the local pin file at load time (`lift-subst-runtime.js`). A fn with
    a missing pin is dropped before entry, as before.
- `H131_138CHK=<tab>` self-check against every captured stage2 op138 entry in `subst-tab-h122.json`:
  **1343 same**, 51 differ, 63 have no captured entry, 0 skipped. Of the 51, **50 differ only in the receiver
  register** and 1 only in an argument (the capture has the literal `16`, the derivation has the register).
  The derivation reads the register that the handler actually reads, since every register gets its own proxy.
  The capture compiler names a register by its observed value. A second register holding the same value, or
  an inlined literal, therefore gives the same result in the captured runs (SC miss 0), but the derivation is
  the execution-backed one. Captured entries win over derived ones, so these 51 sites keep the captured form.
- op53 = unconditional jump (`jump53`): both T/U runs read no register other than N, write nothing and reach the
  same (pc, key). `H131_53CHK`: **4070/4070** same as captured.

### VM throw rule in fns with no try/throw op (`H131_ESC`)

- On a read-blocked canvas, `getImageData` throws natively inside a fn that has no op194/throw op. Before H131,
  the runtime rethrew straight out of the fn body for such fns.
- The interpreter's run loop pops `r48` for every throw. The fn body now does the same for every fn: resume at the
  in-fn handler if there is one, escape the run on an empty `r48` (`fnEsc`), or fail as before if the handler lies
  outside the fn. Runtime self-errors are never treated as VM throws. `G.__deobH131Esc = false` disables the rule.
  canvas_noread: fnCatch 604, fnEsc 3. Golden runs: fnEsc 0.

### op103 / op26 stores dropped by `liftFlow` (root cause of the last canvas_noread failures)

- `lib/exec-lift-h128.mjs` `liftFlow` fits a step as pure register writes from numeric runs. A property store on a
  register that holds a number is silently dropped, and `runProxy` had no `set` trap, so its event check could not
  see the store either.
- Example: the derived entry at 247899:65 op103 in the `probe_38_hMYS1` catch handler was `W(204,(1));`, while the real
  handler also does `R(202)[R(217)] = 1`. That store sets the status field `kYpkN9` in the probe envelope.
  The store form covers 40 op103 and 24 op26 derived entries. The committed `subst-tab-h128.json` and `subst-tab-h130.json`
  contain none of them.
- Fix:
  - `runProxy` records `set`, `defineProperty`, `deleteProperty` and `has` events;
  - `liftFlow` additionally needs one proxy run with no event (`x-effect`);
  - new `liftStore` form (tried before `liftFlow`): an optional small-const write plus exactly one store,
    `R(o)[R(k)] = v`, `R(o)[name] = v` (name → pin) or `R(o)[imm] = v`, with `v` a small const or a read register.
    The write must equal the stored const (op103) or the index (op26). The T and U proxy runs must agree on
    every event, the numeric runs 0/1/3 must write the same const, and all runs must reach the same successor.
- Before / after on 247899:65:103:

  ```js
  // before (liftFlow)            // after (liftStore)
  W(204,(1));                     W(204,(1));
                                  R(202)[R(217)] = 1;
  ```

- `H131_XCHK=<tab> H131_XOPS=103,26` self-check against every captured op103/op26 entry of `subst-tab-h122.json`
  (source compared after normalizing the prelude, whitespace and parentheses, with pins compared by value):
  **same 829**, sameModInlinedValue 528, sameModInlinedKey 52, **diff 0**. The "inlined" classes are captured
  entries where the capture compiler wrote an observed literal (a stored value, or a key such as `width`) in place
  of the register that the lift keeps.
- Derivation: `derived-sites-h131.json`, u 2362, x 5. Against `derived-sites-h130.json` (u 2134): none lost, 228 new
  (op138 49, op103 38, op150 41, op160 39, op200 38, op233 8, op53 3, …), and 122 changed (op138 96 under the new op138 rule,
  op26 24 and op103 2 under `liftStore`). A rerun of the
  derivation with all checks on gives a byte-identical file. Re-deriving stage1 (`H130_STAGE=1`) with the H131 rules
  gives a byte-identical `h130/s1` derivation.

## §H132: readExec pilot, op138 derived display, catch blocks, recount

Table `subst-tab-h132.json` = h131 plus `readExec` / `execSrc` / `erf` on 16 pilot fnBodies (fns 066–080 and 315),
written by `erf-apply-h132.mjs` (lib `lib/erf-h132.mjs`). Runtime: `lift-subst-runtime.js` `/*ERF-BEGIN*/` block.
Builder: `build-final-h132.mjs`; evidence: `final/evidence-h132.json`. Times box-local.

### readExec: the pilot fns execute their file

Before H132 every SHA-gated fn ran table entries compiled from its readable text; the file itself was never parsed at
run time. For a readExec fn the runtime parses the file once and runs each body step from the line that names its key:

```js
// lift-subst-runtime.js, erfParse: one map entry per step key; the last key on a line carries the statement
for (var j = 0; j < keys.length; j++) {
  var k = keys[j].slice(1);
  if (k in map) throw new Error('H132 erf dup key ' + k);
  map[k] = (j === keys.length - 1) ? st : null; n++;
}
// erfTr: rN -> R(N), cell[N] / alias -> C(N), "rN = E" -> W(N, E), "cell[N] = E" -> CW(N, E)
```

Control steps (jump, branch, CALL, CLOS, OP52, RET, THROW) keep their table successors. A text that does not name
every step key exactly once is rejected before the first step. Counters: `fnRx` (body steps run from text),
`fnRxNoop` (keys with no statement), `fnRxEnt`, `fnRxNames`.

### 080 before / after

Before (H131 `final/stage2/080_fn_canvas_getImageData_56911.js`, compile source, 308 lines, no catch text):

```js
function fn_canvas_getImageData_56911() { // was fn_canvas_getImageData_56911; entry L56889_238
  t245_17086 = undefined;  // @56911 k72 op233 [L 1]
  ...
  TP(r48, 59276, 75, r194);  // try: handler L59276_75    // @56965 k224 op194 [L 1]
  t196_17095 = 0;  // @56971 k201 op72 [L 1]
  t204_17096 = cell[49];  // @56974 k60 op236 [L 1]
  t205_17097 = "width";  // @56977 k183 op233 [L 1]
```

After (H132, readExec, 471 lines, byte-equal to `execSrc`):

```js
function fn_canvas_getImageData_56911() { // entry L56889_238; H132 ERF (readExec): the runtime executes the statements of this text
  // H132 rewrites: 3 single-use temps folded, 20 dead register writes dropped (liveness with try-handler edges)
  // alias ctx2d_c50 = cell[50]  evidence (executed member uses): .fillStyle×1 .font×1 .textBaseline×1 .getImageData×1 .drawImage×1
L56889_238:
  r245 = undefined;                                     // #56890:134:53 #56900:44:233 ... #56912:220:233 [L d1 h1 c1]
  TP(r48, 59276, 75, r194);                             // #56963:224:233 #56966:201:194 [L d1 h1 c1]  try → catch L59276_75
  r196 = 0;                                             // #56972:60:72 [L d1 h1 c1]
  r204 = cell[49];                                      // #56975:183:236 [L d1 h1 c1]
  r199 = 80; r204.width = 80;                           // #56978:79:233 #56983:141:103 [L d1 h1 c1]
  r202 = ctx2d_c50;                                     // #56998:242:236 [L d1 h1 c1]
```

The dead `r205 = "width"` write is dropped (its key is a no-op on the `r199 = 80` line), the property name is folded
into `r204.width`. Pilot totals: 40 folds, 174 drops, 3031 body steps, 18 catch regions (117 steps), one alias.
Registers are shared VM-wide (callees and handlers read them), so a fold/drop is only emitted when liveness across
calls and handler edges proves it; that is why the counts are small. Registers are not named.

### Catch blocks

Try-handler regions (359 regions, 1987 steps in all gated fns) are now rendered. Example from 080:

```js
  /* catch L57889_58: handler pushed by TP(r48, 57889, 58, r194) at #57207:39:194; the VM run loop pops r48 on a throw and resumes here with r147 = exception, cells restored */
  catch_L57889_58: {
  L57889_58:
    r204 = r147;                                        // #57890:191:150 [L c1]
    r199 = 0;                                           // #57893:218:72 [L c1]
    r205 = cell[52];                                    // #57896:93:236 [L c1]
```

Marks: `[L dN hN cN]` = hit counts in the H131 default / headless / canvas_noread tracebase runs, `[-]` = none,
`[L rN]` = none of the three but N hits in the H122 reference capture. The `[L rN]` form fixes 74 lines (71 `[L]`, 3 `[N]`
in H122) that a first H132 pass wrongly showed as `[-]`; root cause: marks were taken from the three runs only.
The 92 canvas_noread-only keys are all in the text (92/92); 94 handler keys run in all three runs.

### op138: the table already ran the derived form; the H131 claim is corrected

H131 RESULT gap 12 said the derived op138 form "is not substituted for captured entries". Wrong: the s2 table runs the
derived entries since H126 (commit bd294bc, 51 sites). At 174:130:138 h122 has `["c",205,"charCodeAt",[202]]`, h126 and
later `["c",204,…]`. `chk138-alias-h132.mjs` runs the real handler offline with a distinct value per register:


The other 6 sites are inside readExec text, which is built from the derived entries.

## §H133: fn-local registers, folds and names for all 314 stage2 fns

Table `subst-tab-h133.json` = h132 with `readExec` / `execSrc` / `erf` on all 314 stage2 fnBodies (all other sections
equal to h132). Written by `erf-apply-h133.mjs` (lib `lib/erf-h133.mjs` on top of `lib/erf-h132.mjs`). Runtime:
`lift-subst-runtime.js` (`LW` / `V`, `erfParse` `let` lines). Builder: `build-final-h133.mjs`; evidence:
`final/evidence-h133.json`. All gate runs under `env -i` (see "Incident" below). Times box-local.

### Localization rule

A register web is one live range of a register: its defs and the uses they reach through branches, try-handler
edges and calls. A web becomes a `let` local only when none of these holds: live-in at the fn entry (`liveIn`),
crosses a CALL (`crossCall`), has a table-made def (`tableDef`), reaches RET (`retOut`, registers are VM-global so the
caller can read it), or is a special register (`special`). Kept webs stay `rN` and are counted per fn in `erf.kept`.

Locals are write-through, so the VM register file equals the table program's at every step:

```js
// lift-subst-runtime.js, erfLocRegs: NAME = E runs as LW(K, E), a read of NAME as V(K)
o.V = function(k) {
  if (!LX.b[k]) { erfStale++; throw new Error('H133 erf unbound local ' + k); }
  var cur = regs.read(X.HOME[k]);
  if (!Object.is(cur, LX.v[k])) { erfStale++; throw new Error('H133 erf stale local ' + k); }
  erfV++; return LX.v[k];
};
o.LW = function(k, v) { regs.write(X.HOME[k], v); LX.v[k] = v; LX.b[k] = 1; erfLW++; return v; };
```

A stale local is a self-error (the invocation aborts and is counted), never a silent divergence. `erfStale` is 0 in
every gate run.

### Folds

`constProp` replaces a single-def constant web's uses by the literal (1719 webs, 1727 uses), `strKeys` rewrites
`X["id"]` to `X.id` (265), and the H132 `optimize` pass runs twice (77 single-use folds, 1331 dead writes dropped).
Literals come from the s2 table step itself. For example `#156689:124:233` writes `"\" class=\""`; the H122 display
showed it as `" class="`, but the table value contains the quote characters. These are the two lint `dblQuote` hits.

### Names

A diagnostic table (`erfTypes:true`, scratch only) records the class tag of every value written to each local in the
default, headless and canvas_noread runs (fnFail / fnBoth / fnPartial / erfStale 0 in all three). A local whose values
all had one non-primitive class gets `<short>_vN` (for example `Window` gives `win`,
`OffscreenCanvasRenderingContext2D` gives `ctx2d`, and `HTML<X>Element` gives `el<X>`). The fn header carries one
evidence line per name:

```js
let canvas_v1 /* r204 */, v2 /* r199 */, ctx2d_v3 /* r202 */, ...;
// name navigator_v13: all 3 values written in the three erfTypes runs had class Navigator
```

### Batches

The brief asked for batched readExec. All 314 fns localized without a failure, so one table carries all of them and
one full gate set ran on it. The per-fn fallback in `erf-apply-h133.mjs` (a fn whose localization throws gets the raw
R/W H132 rendering; one that fails again keeps its H122 text) was used 0 times (`raw` 0, `kept` 0). Three registers
(`NOLOC` in `lib/erf-h133.mjs`) are never localized; they account for the 2 `special` webs.

### Incident: stale shell environment

Early H133 runs inherited `RUN_*` / `LIFT_*` exports from an old shell session (fnPinDrop 81, fnInvoc 2679, a false
"handler outside fn" failure). `gate-vclock.sh` also set `LIFT_SUBST` to an old table before the scratch wrapper's
default. Fix: every gate runs under `env -i PATH HOME`, and the wrapper sets its defaults before sourcing
`gate-vclock.sh`. The contaminated runs were discarded; every number above comes from clean runs.

### Gaps

- retOut is conservative: no inter-fn return liveness, so 3929 webs stay `rN`.
- Folds into uses are rare, because the write-through home register stays observable.
- Names come only from class tags; primitive or mixed-class locals stay `vN`. Cells are not renamed. Table operands
  in display show the home register.
- Stage1 (8 SHA-gated fns) is not part of H133.

## §H134: structured control flow, RET locals, trailing keys for all 314 stage2 fns

Goal: make `final/stage2` read as JavaScript instead of a register machine. H133 `final/stage2` had 44661 lines,
2317 `goto` and 17207 lines touching `rN` or `cell[`. The table changes only in `fnBodies` (`subst-tab-h134.json`,
built by `erf-apply-h134.mjs` with `lib/erf-h134.mjs`). Every other section is byte-equal to h133, and
`mkev134` asserts this.

### Runtime (`lift-subst-runtime.js`)

- The runtime derives J/BR successors from the text. `if (C) { … } else { … }`, `for (;;)`, labelled blocks,
  `break L` / `continue L` and `try { } catch (rN) { }` are parsed and executed. A structured jump that does not match the
  table successor fails the fn (`fnFail`).
- CALL/CLOS successors are asserted against the table (`ctlAsr` 14377 per T1 run), not derived from the text.
- RET sugar: `return E;` executes the table RET step. The stats count 11921 `retSugar` lines.
- RET locals: a register web whose only blocker in H133 was reaching a RET is now a `let` local (`retLocals` 3444).
  Write-through and the `erfStale` read check are unchanged from H133.
- Trailing keys: `+#hpc:hkey:op` after the marks names no-op steps (dropped fallthrough jumps) that run after the line.
  They are dup-checked, and a trailing key on a non-statement line is an error (1667 keys on 1636 lines).
- `let` lines hold up to 10 names, grouped by class. The notation legend is printed once per file.

### Counts (apply stats, 314 fns)

if 1334, else 1063, `for (;;)` 87, block 21, try 359, break 30, continue 3, out 352, labelledLoop 2, labelledJump 32,
irreducible 0, deadSteps 0, jumpDropped 1762, blockUnwrapped 776, elseHoisted 201, emptyElse 49, op52Match 45.
Localization: locals 20050, named 4943, retLocals 3444, regWebs 493, constWebs 1719, strKeys 265, folds 77, drops 1331.

`final/stage2` (metrics over `final/stage2/*.js`, 325 files):

| | H133 | H134 |
|---|---|---|
| lines | 44661 | 37558 |
| `goto` | 2317 | 126 (all in the 11 SC-verified display-only fns) |
| lines with `rN` / `cell[` | 17207 | 11772 |
| same, code part only | 13924 | 9603 |

| fn | H133 lines / goto / rN-lines | H134 |
|---|---|---|
| `010_fn_navigator_4147` | 201 / 6 / 103 | 167 / 0 / 63 |
| `080_fn_canvas_getImageData_56911` | 530 / 50 / 258 | 480 / 0 / 64 |

## §H135: expression folds, stage1 readExec, one proven `while`

Goal: shorten `final/stage2` further by folding single-use temporaries into their uses, run the 10 gated stage1 fns
from their text like stage2, and recover loops only where the runtime parser proves the rewrite equal. The table is
`subst-tab-h135.json`, built by `erf-apply-h135.mjs` and then by the post-pass `erf-loops-h135.mjs`. Types were
regenerated on the H135 table, because folding renumbers the local index that types are keyed by.

Commits: `f66da8a` folds, `5b55435` stage1 readExec, `cd873b8` final/ rebuild, `703dba3` loop recovery.

### Folds (`erf-apply-h135.mjs`)

Rule: a single-use def `W(a, E)` moves into its use when all of these hold:
- the use is the first-evaluated read of the target statement or BR condition;
- `a` is dead after the use;
- the path between def and use has only bodyless, guardless, single-pred J steps.

Liveness is static, narrowed by the h131 regTrace facts: for an executed step, live-in = static ∩ the trace set.

| | stage2 trace-bound (shipped) | stage2 static only (scratch) | stage1 (shipped) |
|---|---|---|---|
| xfolds (cross/same step) | 955 (xSame 1) | 524 | 16 |
| xCond (into a BR condition) | 253 | 150 | 1 |
| folds (H133 constant/temp) | 202 | 77 | 1 |
| drops (dead writes) | 4252 | 1331 | 92 |
| kept: liveIn / crossCall | 21 / 26 | 13 / 22 | n/a |

Localization stage2: locals 16115, retLocals 2579, named 5206. Stage1: locals 340, named 155, retLocals 45.

### Stage1 readExec

All 10 gated stage1 fns are readExec (`readable-h135/stage1.readexec.js`). 8 of them run in every H135 subst run
(g15, cng, var; 40 invocations, `fnS1` 40). `probe_29_helper_169` and `probe_29_helper_688` never run. Their text
is gated by its hash only.

### Loops (`erf-loops-h135.mjs`)

The post-pass makes one rewrite:

```
[L: ]for (;;) { if (C) { B } else { E } }   →   [L: ]while (C) { B } E
```

Two conditions apply:
- the `if` must be the whole loop body, with no statement on its line;
- `erfParse` / `erfBody` on the new text must give the same entry, sorted `succ`, `br` {c,t,f}, catch heads, dead keys
  and per-key statements.

One exception: RET/THROW successors in cfg fns are left out of the comparison. The runtime never reads them, because
`lift-subst-runtime.js` breaks out of the step loop on RET before it reads `succ`.

Of 90 `for (;;)` loops in 73 readExec fns, 1 was a candidate and 1 was kept (`probe_32_helper_147599`, 22 → 19 lines).
A rerun gives 0 candidates and the table is byte-equal (idempotent).
Negative controls, in scratch:
- an inverted condition gives `br` unequal;
- a reordered tail gives `br` and `succ` unequal.

```js
  while (elDiv_v1) {                                     // #147647:138:160
    elDiv_v4 = elDiv_v1;                                // #147658:161:150
    if (elDiv_v4.contains(elDiv_v2)) {                  // #147661:110:138 #147682:133:160
      return elDiv_v1;                                  // #147693:83:245
    }
    elDiv_v1 = elDiv_v1[v3];                            // #147688:129:53 #147638:31:7 +#147642:51:53
  }
  v5 = undefined;                                       // #147653:178:53 #147705:204:233 [-]
  return v5;                                            // #147708:44:245 [-]
```

Before, this was `for (;;) { if (elDiv_v1) { … } else { v5 = undefined; return v5; } }`.

Correction to §H134: on a line `S; if (C) {` the runtime evaluates C **before** S (the erf-apply legend, and the
runtime comment "evaluated before the body"). §H134 said "after". So `v29 = v26 <= v22; if (v26 <= v22) {` runs the
compare, then the assignment. The other 7 single-`if` loops with an `else` all have such an S, and
`while ((S, C))` would state the wrong order. They are not rewritten.

## §H136: static soundness, guard-clause loops, the 11 interpreter fns

Goal: every readability rewrite must be sound on every path, not just on the traced ones. Recover more loops, each one
proven by the runtime parser, and decide whether the 11 fns that still run on the interpreter can get readable bodies.
The table is `subst-tab-h136.json` (sha256 prefix `231479d5e4a8`). It is built by `erf-apply-h136.mjs` (static only)
and then the post-pass `erf-loops-h136.mjs`. `final/` is built by `build-final-h136.mjs`, and evidence is in
`final/evidence-h136.json`.

Commits: `67142ee` static-only apply + `chk-sound-h136.mjs`, `db8f15c` loop recovery v2 + table + readable-h136,
`b4bf2e2` `stack-frames-h136.cjs`, `ec332c2` final/ rebuild.

### Static soundness

`erf-apply-h136.mjs` rejects dynamic liveness facts. Liveness is computed over the whole fn CFG, never-run arms and
try-handler edges included:
- a drop needs the write to be overwritten on every path before the fn ends or throws;
- a fold needs one use, with the register dead afterwards on every path.

`chk-sound-h136.mjs` re-derives this from the final table alone (it uses `subst-tab-h131.json` for the original steps):

| fns | steps | removed | drops | folds | foldsCond | constUses | brSwap | unsound / unsoundFold / impureDrop / foldBad / unexplained / constBad / brArm |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 324 | 30611 | 1956 | 1420 | 536 | 150 | 1803 | 65 | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 |

Stage2 rewrite counts: xfolds 524, xCond 150, folds 77, drops 1331 (stage1: 4 / 0 / 1 / 19). This is exactly the H135
static-only scratch variant. The H135 shipped table relied on trace-bound liveness (955 / 253 / 202 / 4252), and
`chk-sound-h136.mjs` flags it with 4266 unsound and 22 foldBad. Root cause: the trace narrowed live-in sets, but an
untraced path can still read the def (H135 example `fn_canvas_getImageData_56911`). Undoing those rewrites accounts for
the line growth below.

The rerun was reproduced in a sandbox: the typed apply on the h131 var run gives table sha `f7d9ed39d4b1`, which equals
the committed pre-loop table, with identical sums.

### Loops (`erf-loops-h136.mjs`)

The H135 `while` rule is kept (1 loop, `probe_32_helper_147599`). New in H136 is the guard-clause flatten. The trailing
`[S; ]if (C) { B } else { E }` of a `for (;;)` body, where E ends in a top-level `return` / `break` / `continue` /
`outside()`, becomes:

```
[S; ]if (!(C)) { E } B          (C = !(X) gives X; mirror case where B jumps: if (C) { B } E)
```

This repeats up to 64 passes per loop. The `[S; ]` prefix is safe because the parser evaluates C before S on that line.
A rewrite is kept only when `erfParse` / `erfBody` on the new text derive the same:
- entry and sorted successors (RET/THROW successors in cfg fns excluded, as in H135);
- catch heads, unreached keys and per-key statements;
- every BR, identical or negated with swapped arms.

`fn_Object_3571`, before (sandbox pre-loop render) and after (`final/stage2/009_fn_Object_3571.js`):

```js
    v34 = v28 < v33; if (v28 < v33) {                   // #4070:188:186
      v20 = arr_v25[v28];                               // #4088:19:7
      ...
      v19 = v28; v28 = v28 + v29;                       // #4056:204:48
    } else {
      return arr_v27;                                   // #4136:163:245
    }
```

```js
    v34 = v28 < v33; if (!(v28 < v33)) {                // #4070:188:186
      return arr_v27;                                   // #4136:163:245
    }
    v20 = arr_v25[v28];                                 // #4088:19:7
    ...
    v19 = v28; v28 = v28 + v29;                         // #4056:204:48
```

| | count |
|---|---|
| readExec fns with loops | 73 |
| `for (;;)` before / after | 90 / 89 |
| `while` | 1 |
| guard clauses tried / kept / rejected | 43 / 43 / 0 (all negated) |
| fns with guard clauses | 34 (edits stage2 33, stage1 2) |
| readable lines | stage2 37793 → 37750 (−43), stage1 789 → 786 (−3); goto and rN lines unchanged |

There is no `for…of` recovery. The parser has no such form. The one iterator loop (`fn_document_7057`, `entries()`)
reads `.next` on every iteration, while `for…of` reads `[Symbol.iterator]` and `next` once and calls `return()` on
break (an observable GetMethod lookup). The loop also has a counter bound and other exits.
`for (init; cond; update)` is not produced either.

### Interpreter fns (feasibility)

The 11 fns are `main`, `probe_12_helper_120600`, `fn_then_cb_120988`, `fn_then_cb_121106`, `fn_then_121265`,
`probe_12_helper_121708`, `probe_12_rwlrt9`, `probe_14_helper_198085`,
`probe_14_document_documentElement_outerHTML_CSSOM`, `probe_multi_1_2_3`, `bag_init_48fields`. Native callees inside
them capture stack frames, so a readable body must give the same frames.

`tools/deob-auto/stack-frames-h136.cjs` runs offline in node `vm` (example.invalid URL, no capture data) and uses
structured `CallSite` data from a probe without changing `Error.prepareStackTrace`:

| experiment | result |
|---|---|
| interp16 | callee sees `probe ← D (U, 3:145, enclosing col 90) ← W (3:247)` |
| readable16 | runtime frames (`rtCall`, `G`) sit between callee and dispatcher, and the dispatcher frame is at the hook column; with `stackTraceLimit` 10 the bottom frames fall off |
| cst | `Error.captureStackTrace` on our side cannot remove frames from a stack the callee captures itself |
| interp7 | non-call ops capture too: ToPrimitive in a compare runs `valueOf` with D at the compare column (3:185) |
| twin3 | a second script with the same URL matches name/file/line/col/enclosing col only if it contains the VM's D/W text byte for byte at the same offsets (everything else blanked, newlines kept); `getScriptHash` still differs |

Why colFit (H117) does not carry over: colFit re-anchors the VM's own call-site tokens to their original columns,
which works because the capturing frames are those VM call sites. In a readable body, two distinct statements cannot
share one source position. So every op of one kind would have to run at the single handler site of that kind, and that
is interpreter dispatch, not readable code. The frame below the handler must also be the VM loop at its own column.
From the hook, the only ways back there are a mid-fn handoff to the interpreter, which breaks entry-only XOR, or edits
to the served VM code, which break CF bytes untouched.

Status: not feasible within the rules. The 11 fns stay on the interpreter, and nothing is implemented.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| H135 | 33582 | 126 | 11172 | 9400 |
| H136 | 37087 | 126 | 11707 | 9599 |

`final/stage1`: 780 lines, 1 goto, 268 rN lines (code part 222). Names: 5554 stage2, 157 stage1.
`build-final-h136.mjs --check`: CHECK OK 340 files.

### Gates (env -i)

| gate | result |
|---|---|
| g15 | 15/15 `7ca3e0532109`, plainLen 64600, fnFail 0, erfStale 0, fnS1 40 |
| cng | 12/12 `a8317f7c964e` |
| var default / headless / canvas_noread | subst = baseline (`7ca3e0532109` / `90bc453bcb2c` / `a8317f7c964e`) |
| shadowcmp | `9f14516bebc3`, match 170084, miss 0, err 0 (H135 170069, same sha) |
| VERIFY / INVC / INVN | 23/23, 10/10, 13/13 |
| negative control | `fn_document_7057` #7608:160:65 `===` → `!==` gives `72076c8398ef` ≠ golden (plainLen 64608) |
| lint stage2 | parseErr 0, undefIds 0, rawRC 0, dblQuote 2 (known), joinRisk 0 |

### Gaps

- The 11 interpreter fns stay compiled (infeasible, see above).
- The 89 remaining `for (;;)` loops have no `while` / `for` header, because their exits do not fit either proven rule.
- The static-only rule gives up the 2921 trace-only drops. Getting them back needs a path-sensitive proof, not traces.
- The shadowcmp match count still varies run to run (miss 0).

## §H137: statically proven register reads (the pilot's L1b, done soundly)

Goal: bring the readability win of the tool-export pilot into the strict pipeline, with a static proof on every path
instead of a traced-path one. The table is `subst-tab-h137.json` (sha256 prefix `e22b2c6a8c0c`). It is built by
`erf-apply-h137.mjs` (lib `lib/erf-h137.mjs`) and then `erf-loops-h137.mjs`, and checked by `chk-sound-h137.mjs`.
`final/` is built by `build-final-h137.mjs`, and evidence is in `final/evidence-h137.json`.

Commits: `37aa0ee` CLOS write sets (581/581) + `webs()` honoring them, `bca8c42` localizeProven + OP52 edges + table +
readable-h137, `43b61ab` `chk-sound-h137.mjs` + mutation tests, `ee186d1` erfTypes re-recorded + layout guard,
`ecfd5e3` final/ rebuild.

### Pilot note (`pilot/tool-export`, not merged)

The pilot (doc `docs/TOOL-EXPORT-PILOT.md`, code `tools/tool-export/`) ran off-the-shelf tools over a standalone export:
- restringer and lit_fold: 0 lines changed;
- wakaru: failed;
- babel evaluate / deadcode / mangle: unsound, because they assume property reads have no side effects and getters break that;
- babel_simplify: only minifies.

Its one real win was its own pass L1b, which strips the `$v(...)` wrappers and the `guard(N)` calls (3908 → 3553 lines on 16
canvas fns, golden held). L1b was proved on the traced path only, which breaks the H136 rule.

### Mapping to the main pipeline

`final/` has no `$v` / `guard` text. The export's `$v(x)` corresponds to the runtime local read `V(k)`, which throws
`H133 erf unbound/stale local` (and counts erfStale) when the local no longer equals its home register. Its `guard(N)`
corresponds to the control asserts. The noise in `final/` that matches what L1b removed is the raw `rN` reads of webs that
H133 kept as registers, because some def of those webs was not a text write. H137 removes those reads where a static
proof holds and leaves the rest.

### Rule (`localizeProven`)

A statement or BR-cond use of a register web is proven when its reaching-definition set holds only `D|` defs: statement
writes `W(h, E)` in non-CTRL steps, over every CFG path including try-handler edges. The set must not contain the
fn-entry value, a CALL, CLOS or OP52 pseudo-def, a CALL result, or an r147 handler value, and the register must not be 48,
147 or 194. Proven uses are grouped by union-find over their def ids, with one local per group whose home is the register
the uses read. Their defs become write-through `LW(k, E)`, so the register still gets the value and the VM sees no change.

Two CFG facts had to be fixed first, both found by execution:
- CLOS: H136 killed every register at a closure creation. `clos-writes-h137.json` records, for each of the 581 CLOS
  sites, the registers that closure can write (apply counts closWrites 484). `webs()` kills only those (`37aa0ee`).
- OP52: under CTL the runtime asserts that an OP52 hash hit goes to the then arm, but `s.succ` held only the
  fallthrough. The 47 then-arm blocks were unreachable for optimize / constProp / localize. erf-apply-h137 adds the 47
  edges (= op52Match). chk-sound-h137 found this as one stale `V(247)` in stage1 `probe_29_webrtc`; the fix also changes
  constUses by +36.

Result: 89 uses + 7 cond uses in 10 webs proven (40 locals, 48 defs); 715 uses in 21 webs left as `rN` (their reaching
sets contain a non-text def).

### Static proof (`chk-sound-h137.mjs`)

The checker rebuilds everything from `subst-tab-h131.json`, the final table and `clos-writes-h137.json`, and does not
trust apply. It runs a freshness lattice per home register: each home holds the local k it carries, or TOP. Every home is
TOP at fn entry. `LW(k)` sets home(k) = k. CALL and OP52 set every home to TOP, and CLOS sets its write set to TOP. Handler
edges join into the try-stack top, or into all TP targets when the stack is unknown. A `V(k)` read is fresh when
home(k) = k on every path.
- freshness: 323 fns, 2111 homes, vReads 17386, vFresh 17386, vStale 0, vUnreached 0, closBad 0, homeBad 0;
- H136 checks on the same table: 324 fns, 30611 steps, removed 1956 (1420 drops, 536 folds), constUses 1839, brSwap 65,
  0 for each of unsound / unsoundFold / impureDrop / foldBad / unexplained / constBad / brArm.

Mutation tests (`MUT=1`, up to 25 evenly spaced per kind per fn):

| mutant | tested | caught | accepted |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 `LW`→`W` (drop write-through) | 4938 | 3154 | 1784 |
| M4 register rewrite after `LW` | 4938 | 3154 | 1784 |
| M3 swap a `V(k)` to another local of the same home | 3818 | 3818 | 0 |
| M2 register read → local read where not proven | 703 | 703 | 0 |
| M5 read a local after a CLOS that writes its home | 368 | 368 | 0 |
| M5b CLOS with its write set ignored | 442 | 442 | 0 |

The 1784 accepted M1/M4 mutants are equivalent: 1783 locals have no `V` read anywhere, and in the remaining one
(`fn_performance_142453` local 163) the local is overwritten before any read.

A position bug in the M2 mutant builder was found and fixed before the counts above.

### Names: layout bug and guard

`nameLocals` keys erfTypes by `fn|K`. `bca8c42` applied the H136 types table to the H137 numbering, which the CLOS
write sets had shifted. 3699 of 5707 names sat on locals of another H136 class (e.g. `bag_helper_1184` got `win_v3`
where H136 had `crypto_v4`). Found by comparing names at equal def positions. Fix (`ee186d1`):
- types re-recorded on the untyped H137 table with three env -i runs (default, headless, canvas_noread: gatedFns 324,
  fnFail 0, erfStale 0);
- structural guard: erf-apply-h137 hashes the local layout (fn, K, home, def ids), and a types table whose `__layout`
  differs makes it exit 3 and write nothing. Tested with the H136 table (exit 3).

Result: 5560 stage2 + 158 stage1 named. 5694 of them have the H136 class prefix at the same def positions, 21 are new,
there are 0 class conflicts, and 14 H136 names are gone because those locals became proven or merged. The code
diff against `bca8c42` is names only: 10 lines in stage2, 0 in stage1, plus evidence comments.

## §H138: explicit sources (binds) and the OP52 write set

Goal: turn the register reads that H137 left as `rN` (715 uses in 21 webs, whose reaching sets contain a fn-entry, CALL,
CLOS or OP52 value) into ordinary locals, each one statically proven. The table is `subst-tab-h138.json` (sha256 prefix
`e47a754cccb4`). It is built by `erf-apply-h138.mjs` (lib `lib/erf-h138.mjs`) and then `erf-loops-h138.mjs`, and checked
by `chk-sound-h138.mjs`. `final/` is built by `build-final-h138.mjs`, and evidence is in `final/evidence-h138.json`.

Commits: `256699e` binds + OP52 kill set + table + readable-h138, `186c736` `chk-sound-h138.mjs` + mutants M6-M9,
`15f6b76` loop pass on the H138 table, `92c3962` final/ rebuild.

### Rule (`placeBinds`)

A source is a value that no text statement writes: the fn-entry value of a register (E), a CALL result (C), or the
CALL / CLOS pseudo-def (X). For each source whose register is read later in an `rN` web, a bind `LW(K, R(h))` goes into
the first body step after the source. It is rendered as `NAME = rN;`. The bind writes the register's own value back, so
the register file is unchanged and the VM sees nothing new. With the bind, every def that reaches the later reads is a
text write, and H137's `localizeProven` turns those reads into `V(K)`.

The bind step is found by walking single-successor jumps from the source:
```js
function bindStepFrom(S, start) {
  ...
    if (BINDOK.has(s.kind) && s.isBody && !s.tp) return k;
    if (s.kind === 'J' && s.succ.length === 1 && (!s.isBody || s.tp)) { k = s.succ[0]; continue; }
    return null;
```
A bind is kept only when it becomes an `LW`, is not redundant, and its local has at least 2 reads. With 1 read the bind
would only move the `rN` to the bind line. Binds are re-placed until a fixpoint (2 rounds). Registers 48, 147, 194 and
pc / key are never bound.

Result: 1483 candidates, 510 kept (E 391, X:CALL 85, X:CLOS 26, C:CALL 8). Dropped: 882 fewReads and 91 notLocal (the
bind def stays in an `rN` web because a value that no kept bind covers also reaches a read of that web), 0 redundant.
Proven webs 10 → 17 (84 locals, 100 defs), proven uses 89 + 7 cond → 295 + 20 cond; provenLeftUses 715 / 21 webs →
475 / 26 webs.

### OP52 kills only pc / key

H137 treated OP52 like CALL and killed every register. H138 kills only the VM's pc / key registers (text r169 / r141):
- a real-VM boot on both stage instances shows the pc / key views are text r169 / r141;
- the real op52 handler, run at all 195 listing sites on the miss path and on the hash-hit path (cache pre-seeded), makes
  0 frame writes and changes no `values[]` / `H[]` entry outside pc / key in 2 × 195 runs;
- `runOp52` in the runtime writes only machine fields and the hash cache. The full match branch needs a BLAKE3 preimage,
  so the code and the runtime `V(k)` check cover it;
- no text statement, condition or operand names r169 / r141 (`pckeyRead 0`).

The checker's transfer at OP52:
```js
if (s.kind === 'OP52') { /* H138: OP52 writes only pc / key (never homes, asserted above) */
  toHandler(s.sk, st);
  const ow = over && over.op52w && over.op52w.has(s.sk) ? over.op52w.get(s.sk) : PCKEY;
  ...
  for (const h of ow) if (HS.has(h)) st.set(h, TOP);
```

### Static proof (`chk-sound-h138.mjs`)

The checker strips identity binds from both texts before the H136 diff, and checks freshness on the full text.
- H136 checks: 324 fns, 30611 steps, removed 1956 (1420 drops, 536 folds), constUses 1839, brSwap 65, 0 for each of
  unsound / unexplained / foldBad / constBad / impureDrop / brArm;
- freshness: 2521 homes, vReads 21587, vFresh 21587, vStale 0, closBad 0, pckeyRead 0, binds 510;
- rawFresh 14: register reads left in the text whose home provably holds a local, all in the `probe_4_fetch` /
  `probe_5_fetch` catch blocks. `webs()` sends the defs reaching anywhere in a step to its handler, while freshness
  uses the state at the throwing statement, so a bind earlier in the same step is not credited. These are the 7 M2
  mutants that are accepted below.

Mutation tests (`MUT=1`; M1-M5 up to 25 per kind per fn, M6-M9 exhaustive):

| mutant | tested | caught | accepted |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 `LW`→`W` | 4997 | 3347 | 1650 (1649 locals with no read; 1 dead write in `fn_performance_142453`) |
| M4 register rewrite after `LW` | 4997 | 3347 | 1650 (same) |
| M3 swap a local of the same home | 3856 | 3856 | 0 |
| M2 register read → local read where not proven | 982 | 975 | 7 (the rawFresh reads, equivalent) |
| M5 read a local after a CLOS that writes its home | 373 | 373 | 0 |
| M5b CLOS with its write set ignored | 448 | 448 | 0 |
| M6 bind reads another register | 510 | 510 | 0 |
| M7 bind dropped | 510 | 510 | 0 |
| M8 bind moved before its CALL / CLOS source | 91 | 91 | 0 (419 not built: the 391 E-source binds have no source step, 28 CALL / CLOS sources have no single J / BR body predecessor) |
| M9 OP52 writes every home | 48 | 47 | 1 (`probe_13_helper_130954`: a home holds a local across the OP52, but no later `V` read depends on it; equivalent) |

### Names and loops

The binds add locals and renumber K, so the erfTypes were re-recorded on the untyped H138 table (three env -i runs:
default, headless, canvas_noread). The layout guard holds: the types table and the table carry the same `__layout`, and
5748 locals are named (stage1 166). `erf-loops-h138.mjs` (the H136 rules) keeps 1 while + 43 guard clauses and rejects 0.

`for (;;)` exit shapes (89 loops in the table, 87 in `final/stage2`), classified by the first top-level exit of the body:

| shape | loops | why no proven rule |
|---|---|---|
| head exit `if (C) { exit } rest` (no prelude) | 0 | (this is the `while (!C)` shape) |
| `S; if (C) { exit } rest`, prelude 1 / 2 / 3+ statements | 6 / 26 / 11 | the prelude runs before the test on every iteration; `while` would need the prelude in the test (`while ((S, !C))`, which the runtime ERF parser does not accept) or duplicated. 39 of the 43 preludes are call-free assignments |
| exits only nested (returns inside arms / `try`) | 38 | no top-level exit to lift into a loop test |
| exits only nested (labelled `break` / `continue`) | 8 | same |

No new loop rule was added in H138.

## §H139: VM machine registers and named entry / closure values

Goal: remove the `rN` that H138 kept by rule (the VM-internal registers r48 / r194 / r147) and the `rN` of fn-entry
(E, 756 uses) and CLOS (589 uses) sources, without one bind line per read. The table is `subst-tab-h139.json`. It is
built by `erf-apply-h139.mjs` (lib `lib/erf-h139.mjs`), then `erf-loops-h139.mjs`, then `erf-machine-h139.mjs`, and
checked by `chk-sound-h139.mjs`. `final/` is built by `build-final-h139.mjs`, and evidence is in `final/evidence-h139.json`.

Commits: `c37cfed` step A (machine registers named by execution, table, readable-h139, final/), `d0c9a94` step B
(header parameters and CLOS slots, runtime, checker mutants P1-P4, erfTypes re-recorded, final/).

### Step A: what the three VM registers are (by execution)

The real handlers of the captured stage-2 VM were stepped one dispatch at a time on the real register file (box-local
scratch harness, offline):

| register | name | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| r48 | `tryStack` | TP (op194) pushes `[pc, key, cells, depth]` on r48 at 356/356 sites; RET (op245) truncates r48 to `r194.pop()` at 614/614 sites; the catch rule pops r48 at 60/60 sites; a closure call pushes an undefined sentinel (556/557 entries, 1 with no step) |
| r194 | `callDepths` | TP reads it (356/356); each closure call pushes the current r48 length, RET pops it (614/614), exits restore it (117/117 returns) |
| r147 | `caught` | the catch rule writes the exception value to r147 before resuming at the handler (60/60) |

None of them is `this`, a scope, `arguments` or an accumulator: these live in cells and other registers that the lifter
already names, and no handler reads r48 / r194 / r147 as an operand value. `erf-machine-h139.mjs` renames them in the
106 fns that use them. Each fn gets a `// machine tryStack = r48, callDepths = r194, caught = r147` line (runtime
`X.MA`, `erfTr` maps the name back to `R(N)`), and the catch arm is `catch (caught)`. Tokens: tryStack 706, callDepths
366, caught 654; catch lines 361; rN left from these registers: 0. Proof: per fn, `erfParse` of the renamed text gives
the same erfBody string for every step key, the same entry / successors / catch heads / unreached keys, every BR cond
equal after `erfTr`, and the same locals; any difference aborts the pass.

### Step B rule (`placeBinds` forms)

- **par**: the bind source is the fn-entry value E, the entry step has 0 predecessors, every later step on the walk
  from the entry to the bind step has exactly 1 predecessor (succ, OP52 match, try and throw edges counted), and no
  step before the bind writes the home register. The bind becomes a header parameter `NAME /* rH */`. The runtime binds
  it to the home register's value when the invocation starts (the fn-entry value).
- **cb**: the bind source is a CLOS step whose closure write is the home register, under the same single-predecessor
  chain from the CLOS successor. The bind becomes the CLOS slot `NAME = clos;`, bound right after the step. The runtime
  throws `H139 erf` when the slot is on a non-CLOS step.
- Tagged binds are exempt from the fewReads drop, because they cost no extra line. Locals are ordered params first.

Runtime (`lift-subst-runtime.js`): `erfParse` reads header params into `X.PAR` and CLOS slots into `X.cb`; `erfBindK`
reads the home register through the quiet read path and writes only the JS-side local (`LX.v` / `LX.b` / types). It
makes no VM register write, and the CF bytes and the entry-only XOR path are unchanged (all golden shas below).

Result: 745 params in 251 fns, 382 CLOS slots in 140 fns. Binds kept 1220 of 1483 candidates (E 745, X:CLOS 382,
X:CALL 85, C:CALL 8); dropped fewReads 171 (H138 882), notLocal 92.

### Static proof (`chk-sound-h139.mjs`)

The entry state sets the home of each `X.PAR` local; in the CLOS branch an `X.cb` local is bound only when its home is
the CLOS write register, otherwise closBad. cbBad counts a slot on a non-CLOS step or an unknown key.
- unsound 0, unexplained 0, constBad 0, impureDrop 0, brSwap 65;
- freshness: vReads 22297, all fresh, vStale 0, closBad 0, cbBad 0, homeBad 0; params 745, cbBinds 382, binds 93,
  machineFns 106, rawFresh 14.

| mutant | tested | caught | accepted |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 param bound to another register | 745 | 745 | 0 |
| P2 param moved from the header to a `let` line | 745 | 745 | 0 |
| P3 another local in the CLOS slot | 382 | 382 | 0 |
| P4 CLOS slot removed (`clos;`) | 382 | 382 | 0 |
| MA1 tryStack / callDepths swapped | 106 | 106 | 0 |
| MA2 machine name points at the next register | 106 | 106 | 0 |
| MA3 machine line removed | 106 | 106 | 0 |
| M3 swap a local of the same home | 3900 | 3900 | 0 |
| M5 / M5b CLOS write set | 399 / 469 | 399 / 469 | 0 |
| M6 / M7 bind other / dropped | 93 / 93 | 93 / 93 | 0 |
| M8 bind before its source | 69 | 69 | 0 (24 not built) |
| M1 / M4 | 4943 | 3177 | 1766 (1765 locals with no read, 1 dead write in `fn_performance_142453`) |
| M2 | 284 | 276 | 8 (rawFresh reads in `probe_4_fetch` / `probe_5_fetch`, equivalent) |
| M9 | 48 | 47 | 1 (`probe_13_helper_130954`, equivalent) |

### Names and loops

The params renumber the locals, so the erfTypes were re-recorded on the untyped step-B table (three env -i runs,
runtime erfPar 6155 / 6195 / 5939, erfCb 1073 / 1081 / 1054, fnFail 0, erfStale 0). The layout guard holds (types
`__layout` = table layout); 6167 locals named (H138 5748), stage1 188. Loops unchanged: 89 in the table, 1 while,
43 guard clauses.

### Snippet (`fn_now_cb_124450`, `final/stage2/111_fn_now_cb_124450.js`)

H138:
```js
function fn_now_cb_124450() {
```

A: a CALL whose return register has one later read binds `NAME = cell[N](args);`; the runtime checks the bind on every
normal return (`erfCs` 1318 checks in g15 T1, erfStale 0). B: a stage2 CALL's normal return changes only the return register
and 12 table registers; every other home keeps its value across the call.

```
// lib/erf-h142.mjs
export const CALLCLOB = [16, 36, 88, 92, 139, 227, 243, 248, 162, 147, 48, 194];
// lib/erf-h133.mjs: the exception edge keeps the full kill
const killsX = (s, r) => (s.kind === 'CALL' && Array.isArray(s.kw)) || kills(s, r);
// chk-sound-h142.mjs fresh(): CALL normal edge
o = new Map(st); const kill = new Set([+(s.guard || [])[2], ...CALLCLOB]);
```

The set comes from execution (85 readExec CALL sites, 6890 nested calls): callee argument registers, the VM run() result

## §H143 Multi-key while header, prefix-while loops, rawR classification

Commits: `04e6107` (final/ from readable-h142), `85b2013` (runtime grammar, loops), `8e91df9` (final/ from readable-h143), `eb02ee5`
(rawR classifier).

### Slice 1: final/ from readable-h142

`build-final-h142.mjs`: names layout guard (evidence `names.localLayout` must equal the erfTypes `__layout`); layout unchanged, no
type re-record. `--check` OK, 340 files: stage2 SHA-gated 314, SC-verified 11; stage1 SHA-gated 8, SC 1, gated-not-executed 2.

### Slice 2: multi-key ERF line and prefix-while loops

Runtime grammar (`lift-subst-runtime.js`): the key comment of a `while` line may hold groups split by `|`. The header must be one
parenthesized comma list `while ((P1, ..., [S, ]C))` (top-level split `erfCommas`); with G groups it has G or G+1 parts. The last key
of group i carries part i; the last group is the loop-exit branch: its statement is S (G+1 parts) and its condition C. Groups on any
other line throw. Lines without `|` are parsed exactly as before.

```js
// before (H142)
for (;;) {
  fn_v8 = win_v45.eval;                               // #5461:191:7 [L d1 h1 c1]
  win_v9 = MC(undefined, fn_v8, "this");              // #5465:220:114 [L d1 h1 c1]
  v10 = !(!(win_v45 === win_v9));                     // #5470:24:65 #5474:225:130 #5477:198:130 [L d1 h1 c1]
  if (v10) {                                          // #5480:125:160 [L d1 h1 c1]
    v11 = cell[1];                                    // #5496:172:236 [L d1 h1 c1]
    ...                                               // rest of the fn body, one level deeper
  } else {
    // #5486:153:53 [-] /* never executed in any replay */   (then back to the loop head)
  }
}
// after (H143)
while ((fn_v8 = win_v45.eval, win_v9 = MC(undefined, fn_v8, "this"), v10 = !(!(win_v45 === win_v9)), !(v10))) {  // #5461:191:7 | #5465:220:114 | #5470:24:65 #5474:225:130 #5477:198:130 | #5480:125:160
  // #5486:153:53 [-] /* never executed in any replay */
}
v11 = cell[1];                                        // #5496:172:236 [L d1 h1 c1]
...                                                   // rest of the fn body at the loop's indent
```

`erf-loops-h143.mjs` rule 3 (tryPrefix): a `for (;;)` whose body starts with one-expression statements and then `[S;] if (X)`
becomes `while ((P..., [S,] !X)) { B } E` when the `if` arm jumps out, or for an if/else tail `while ((P..., [S,] X)) { B } E` and,
when E does not end in a jump, the swapped `while ((P..., [S,] !(X))) { E } B`. A candidate is kept only when the per-fn runtime
erfParse signature (entry, successors, branches with negation, loop heads, dead steps, per-step body) is equal to the H142 one.

|---|---|
| `for (;;)` before / after | 90 / 12 (stage2 87 / 11, stage1 3 / 1) |
| tried / kept / swapped / rejected | 85 / 78 / 35 / 7 |
| `while ((` headers | stage2 76, stage1 2 |
| grammar mutants (swapParts, dropBar, extraBar, swapGroups) | 78/78 rejected each |
| chk-sound | unsound 0, vStale 0, vReads 22462, callKeep 126, keepAcrossN 914 |
| lint stage2 | parseErr 0, rawR 1647, liveIn 38, gotoN 244, labelN 265, closUndef 5 (as H142) |

Remaining 12 `for (;;)`: labelled-break exits 6 (fn_document_7057 x2, fn_performance_142453, fn_navigator_177297,
fn_canvas_getImageData_246536, stage1 probe_29_helper_1104), nested-`return`-only exits 5 (fn_canvas_getImageData_39320,
fn_document_167504 x2, probe_22_helper_184670, fn_navigator_cb_226841), the SC-verified dispatcher probe_multi_1_2_3 1.
